## STORY-002 — Orchestrator Agent Coordination

- [x] Scaffold agents/ package: response contract, Orchestrator, one stub agent
  - Date: 2026-08-20
  - Session: CC-20260820-q7m2
  - What changed: Added agents/contracts.py (AgentQuery/AgentResponse dataclasses, validate_response() defining what "invalid response" means), agents/base.py (Agent protocol), agents/orchestrator.py (Orchestrator.coordinate() dispatches a query to every configured agent, validates each response, retries once on an invalid response before recording failure), agents/stub_agent.py (trivial fixed-recommendation agent for exercising the loop before a real analysis agent exists), and agents/logging_setup.py (JSON logger stamped "service": "agents", a copy of data_integration/logging_setup.py's pattern rather than a shared import, kept local so this story doesn't modify a file outside its own directory).
  - Verification: python -m py_compile passes; manually ran Orchestrator([StubAnalysisAgent()]).coordinate() and confirmed timestamped JSON logs for orchestration_started / agent_response_validated / orchestration_completed.
  - Notes: Did not extend data_integration/logging_setup.get_logger() with a service parameter (the DRY alternative) because that touches a file outside this story; flagged as a follow-up if a third service needs the same logger shape.

- [x] Add pytest suite for agents/ orchestrator and logging
  - Date: 2026-08-20
  - Session: CC-20260820-q7m2
  - What changed: Added agents/tests/test_orchestrator.py (happy path; invalid response triggers one re-evaluation then recovers; still-invalid after re-evaluation fails; agent raising an exception is caught, logged as agent_communication_failed, and does not crash coordinate(); one failing agent doesn't block a sibling agent's result; lifecycle log events fire in order) and agents/tests/test_logging_setup.py (JsonFormatter stamps a timestamp and the correct service name on every log line). Installed pytest==8.4.2 locally via pip3 --user (matches the version already pinned in data_integration/requirements.txt; no pytest was present in this environment).
  - Verification: 7/7 tests passing (`python3 -m pytest agents/tests -v`).
  - Notes: `python3 -m pytest agents/tests data_integration/tests` fails to collect the data_integration tests — psycopg2/google-auth aren't installed in this environment. Pre-existing gap, unrelated to this story; agents/ has no external dependencies so it's unaffected. Not fixed here since it's outside STORY-002's scope.

- [x] Add timeout handling for agent calls
  - Date: 2026-08-20
  - Session: CC-20260820-q7m2
  - What changed: agents/orchestrator.py — Orchestrator now takes an agent_timeout_seconds param (default 10.0) and runs each agent.run() call via a ThreadPoolExecutor with future.result(timeout=...); a TimeoutError is caught, logged as agent_timeout with the configured timeout in context, and recorded as a coordination failure instead of hanging the orchestrator. executor.shutdown(wait=False) lets the orchestrator return immediately since a synchronous Python thread can't be forcibly killed — documented as a comment since it's a non-obvious constraint (the timeout bounds how long the orchestrator waits, not how long the agent's thread keeps running).
  - Verification: 8/8 tests passing (`python3 -m pytest agents/tests -v`), including a new test with a HangingAgent that sleeps 5s against a 0.05s timeout, asserting the orchestrator returns in <1s with outcome="failure" and an agent_timeout log entry.
  - Notes: First pass at this test had a copy-paste bug (RaisingAgent's run() method ended up orphaned onto HangingAgent because an earlier Edit only matched part of the class body) — caught immediately by the test run itself (AttributeError instead of the expected error), fixed before this entry was written.

- [x] Add data inconsistency detection across agents
  - Date: 2026-08-20
  - Session: CC-20260820-q7m2
  - What changed: agents/orchestrator.py — added _detect_data_inconsistency(), a deterministic check comparing confidence values across successful ("ok") responses in one coordinate() run; a spread greater than MAX_CONFIDENCE_SPREAD (0.5) is logged as agent_responses_inconsistent (warning) and rolls the orchestration_completed log's outcome up to "inconsistent". Individual agents that each returned a structurally valid response still get outcome="success" on their own CoordinationResult — inconsistency is a cross-agent, orchestration-level concern, not a per-agent failure, so it doesn't trigger re-evaluation.
  - Verification: 10/10 tests passing (`python3 -m pytest agents/tests -v`) — new tests cover a wide confidence spread (0.9 vs 0.2) getting flagged with the correct spread value in the log, and two agreeing agents (0.85 vs 0.8) NOT being flagged.
  - Notes: Deliberately used a deterministic, structured signal (confidence spread) rather than comparing free-text recommendations semantically — this repo's own governance doc states "LLMs are probabilistic, production systems must be deterministic," and free-text contradiction detection would need an LLM judge call, which is out of scope for this story. Known limitation (fixed in the next entry below): at this point the inconsistency flag was log-only, not attached to what coordinate() returned to the caller.

- [x] Add orchestrator-crash guard; fix data-inconsistency limitation by returning a CoordinationRun object
  - Date: 2026-08-20
  - Session: CC-20260820-q7m2
  - What changed: agents/orchestrator.py — coordinate() now wraps its body in a try/except so an unexpected internal error (a bug in coordination logic itself, not a per-agent failure, which _run_agent already isolates) is caught, logged as orchestrator_crashed, and returned rather than propagating out of coordinate(). To carry that outcome back to the caller, coordinate()'s return type changed from list[CoordinationResult] to a new CoordinationRun dataclass (query_text, results, inconsistency, crash_error, plus a computed .outcome property: "success" | "partial" | "inconsistent" | "crashed"). This is a breaking change to coordinate()'s public return type; per this repo's own contract rule ("breaking contract change requires updating consumers in the same diff"), every call site in agents/tests/test_orchestrator.py was updated in this same commit (results = ... -> run = ...; assertions moved to run.results / run.outcome / run.inconsistency). This also directly fixes the data-inconsistency limitation logged in the previous entry: run.inconsistency and run.outcome are now inspectable directly on the returned object, not just in the log stream.
  - Verification: 11/11 tests passing (`python3 -m pytest agents/tests -v`), including a new test that forces _detect_data_inconsistency to raise and confirms coordinate() still returns (outcome="crashed", crash_error="boom", results=[]) instead of propagating the exception, with a logged orchestrator_crashed error. Also manually ran Orchestrator([StubAnalysisAgent()]).coordinate() end-to-end and confirmed the returned CoordinationRun's .outcome, .inconsistency, .crash_error, and .results fields are all as expected.
  - Notes: All 5 of STORY-002's listed failure paths are now handled and tested: agent communication failure, invalid agent response, timeout errors, data inconsistency, and orchestrator crash.

- [x] Code review and fixes prior to first commit
  - Date: 2026-08-20
  - Session: CC-20260820-q7m2
  - What changed: Ran /code-review (medium) against the full agents/ diff. Fixed 2 real bugs and 1 governance gap: (1) CoordinationRun.outcome checked crash_error/inconsistency by truthiness, so an exception with no message (str(exc) == "") silently reported outcome="partial" instead of "crashed" — changed both checks to `is not None`. (2) validate_response() only branched on status == "ok" or "error"; AgentResponseStatus is a Literal but Python doesn't enforce that at runtime, so an agent returning any other status string (e.g. "pending") passed through as a valid response with zero checks — added an explicit else branch rejecting unrecognized statuses. (3) CLAUDE.md's Observability Framework requires a correlation_id (UUID v4) propagated through every log line from a request's entry point; coordinate() is that entry point but wasn't stamping one — added correlation_id generation in coordinate(), threaded it through _run_agent's log calls, added it to JsonFormatter's pass-through keys, and exposed it on CoordinationRun for callers.
  - Verification: 20/20 tests passing (`python3 -m pytest agents/tests -v`) — added agents/tests/test_contracts.py (7 tests; validate_response had no direct unit tests before this, only indirect coverage via orchestrator tests) plus 2 new orchestrator tests proving the crash_error and unknown-status fixes. Manually ran the stub agent end-to-end and confirmed one correlation_id appears on all 3 log lines (orchestration_started / agent_response_validated / orchestration_completed) and on the returned CoordinationRun.
  - Notes: Review also flagged that coordinate() dispatches to configured agents sequentially (a list comprehension over _run_agent, each of which already uses its own thread + timeout internally) — with N agents each near the timeout, total wall-clock time is O(N * timeout) instead of O(timeout). Left as a known limitation/follow-up rather than fixed here: no acceptance criterion or listed failure path requires concurrent dispatch, and nesting a top-level ThreadPoolExecutor around calls that already spin their own per-agent executor adds real complexity/risk that isn't justified right before this commit. Worth revisiting once a real multi-agent workload (STORY-003/004) exists to size the actual latency impact.

## STORY-011 — Trust Spine Implementation for Data Processing

- [x] Build audit trail (idempotency + unique-id/timestamp logging) and wire it into the data-processing pipeline
  - Date: 2026-08-20
  - Session: CC-20260820-x9k1
  - What changed: Added data_integration/audit_trail.py — an `AuditStore` that persists one JSONL record per processing attempt (UUID4 `record_id`, UTC `timestamp`, dataset, outcome, `error`) keyed by a caller-supplied idempotency key; `record()` returns the existing entry instead of writing a new one when a key was already seen, and reloads existing records on construction so idempotency survives across process restarts, not just within one run. Added `run_integration_with_audit()` to data_integration/orchestrator.py, a wrapper around the existing (untouched) `run_integration()`: successful attempts are keyed on dataset name + a SHA-256 fingerprint of the fetched rows (so identical data reprocessed produces the same key and no duplicate entry), failed attempts are keyed on a fresh id every time (a recurring failure is itself diagnostic information and must not be collapsed away). Wired this into data_integration/run_sample_integration.py's `main()` via a `SUPPLYMIND_AUDIT_LOG_PATH`-configurable, gitignored default audit log file, so the demo script's own reruns are the end-to-end proof of the guarantee.
  - Verification: 24/24 tests passing (`python3 -m pytest data_integration/tests -v`), including new tests in test_audit_trail.py (unique id + timestamp on create, no duplicate on reprocess, `has_processed()`, idempotency across separate `AuditStore` instances, error outcome recorded with detail, write failure raises a typed error) and test_orchestrator.py / test_run_sample_integration.py (one audit entry per dataset, reprocessing identical data doesn't duplicate, error detail recorded, repeated failures aren't deduped, audit entries created even when every dataset fails). Also ran `python3 -m data_integration.run_sample_integration` twice live against the sample 8-dataset config (no real credentials in this environment, so every dataset fails both runs) and confirmed 16 audit lines (8 datasets × 2 runs, each failure with its own record_id — the correct, by-design behavior, since failures are deliberately not deduped).
  - Notes: Installed the pinned dependencies from data_integration/requirements.txt (psycopg2-binary, gspread, google-auth) via `pip3 install --user`, which weren't present in this environment — that also unblocked 12 previously-uncollectable STORY-001 tests (test_orchestrator.py, test_postgres_connector.py, test_sheets_connector.py) that had been silently skipped since STORY-002.

- [x] Code review and fixes prior to first commit
  - Date: 2026-08-20
  - Session: CC-20260820-x9k1
  - What changed: Ran /code-review (medium) against the staged STORY-011 diff. Fixed 3 real bugs, each verified directly and each fixed with a test reproducing the original break: (1) audit_trail.py's `_load_existing` only caught OSError/JSONDecodeError/KeyError, so a schema-mismatched audit-log line raised a raw, undocumented TypeError instead of the class's own documented AuditTrailWriteError — added TypeError to the except clause. (2) orchestrator.py's `run_integration_with_audit` let an AuditTrailWriteError from one dataset's audit write propagate out of the whole function, discarding the already-fetched results for every other dataset in the run — contradicting the module's own per-dataset failure-isolation guarantee; wrapped the `audit_store.record()` call in a try/except that logs `audit_trail_unavailable` and continues, so the full results list is always returned. (3) `_content_fingerprint` hashed row order along with content, but the sample queries have no ORDER BY, so identical underlying data returned in a different row order across two runs produced a different SHA-256 hash and a spurious duplicate audit entry — now sorts each row's canonical JSON string before hashing so row order no longer affects the fingerprint.
  - Verification: 27/27 tests passing (`python3 -m pytest data_integration/tests -v`) — 3 new tests added, one per fix, each confirmed failing against the pre-fix code before the fix landed: malformed existing record raises AuditTrailWriteError not TypeError; an audit write failure for one dataset doesn't lose the other dataset's already-fetched, already-analysis-ready result; reprocessing the same rows in a different order does not create a duplicate audit entry.
  - Notes: No unresolved review findings remain.

- [x] Confirm STORY-011 acceptance criteria in .colaberry/progress.json
  - Date: 2026-08-20
  - Session: CC-20260820-x9k1
  - What changed: Replaced STORY-011's empty `criteria: []` / `state: "not_started"` entry with the 3 criteria text (matching the story card exactly) marked passed:true, and `state: "in_progress"` — same pattern as the existing STORY-000/001/002 entries. Bumped .colaberry/manifest.json's generated_at.
  - Verification: Both files still parse as valid JSON. The 3 criteria map directly to the automated tests verified in the entries above: no-duplicate-on-reprocess (test_run_integration_with_audit_reprocessing_same_data_does_not_duplicate, test_main_run_twice_with_unchanged_data_does_not_duplicate_audit_entries, and the reordered-rows variant added in the review-fixes entry), error logged with detail (test_run_integration_with_audit_records_error_detail_on_failure), and an audit trail created for each transaction (test_run_integration_with_audit_creates_one_entry_per_dataset, test_main_creates_one_audit_entry_per_dataset_even_when_every_dataset_fails).
  - Notes: This is portal-facing tracking (.colaberry/), separate from this file. The portal has read-only access via the GitHub remote, so this change needs to be committed and pushed for the portal to see it.

- [x] Second code review pass before commit; harden AuditStore against a corrupted/partial audit log
  - Date: 2026-08-20
  - Session: CC-20260820-x9k1
  - What changed: Re-ran /code-review (medium) against the full staged STORY-011 diff (including the round-1 fixes and the two PROGRESS.md/.colaberry entries). Found and fixed one more real bug: `AuditStore._load_existing` raised `AuditTrailWriteError` for any single corrupted line, crashing construction of the whole store. Since JSONL appends aren't atomic, a process killed mid-write (OOM, SIGKILL, power loss) leaves a truncated trailing line — and `run_sample_integration.py`'s `main()` doesn't catch that error, so the script would crash unrecoverably on every subsequent run against that audit log file, with no documented recovery path (a Failure-First Design violation). This also meant the round-1 fix for the same method (catching TypeError so a malformed record raised the *typed* error) was too shallow — it changed which exception propagated but not whether one bad line should be fatal at all. Rewrote `_load_existing` so only a failure to open/read the file itself (genuine I/O failure) is fatal; a per-line JSON/schema error is now logged as `audit_record_skipped_corrupted` (warning) and that one line is skipped, letting every other valid record still load.
  - Verification: 28/28 tests passing (`python3 -m pytest data_integration/tests -v`). Replaced the round-1 test (which asserted a malformed line raises `AuditTrailWriteError`) with two tests matching the corrected behavior: a truncated trailing line (simulating crash-mid-write) doesn't crash `AuditStore()` and the valid line before it still loads; a schema-mismatched line (missing fields) is likewise skipped, not fatal.
  - Notes: `test_write_failure_is_surfaced_not_swallowed` (a real OSError from a read-only directory on `record()`) still passes unchanged — that failure mode is unaffected, since it's a write failure, not a load-time corrupted-line case.

## STORY-003 — Demand Forecasting Implementation

- [x] Scaffold forecasting/ core: deterministic trend model + data-quality assessment
  - Date: 2026-08-20
  - Session: CC-20260820-b3n7
  - What changed: Added forecasting/demand_model.py (`forecast_demand()`: weighted-linear-trend + seasonal-index forecaster over monthly `DemandPoint`s, fit by least squares, confidence = R²; raises `ForecastingError` on too-short history or bad parameters) and forecasting/data_quality.py (`assess_data_quality()`: flags sparse history, gap periods, and non-positive demand values as human-readable warnings). Pure computation, no I/O — logged assumption (not escalated, per this repo's autonomy model): periods are "YYYY-MM" calendar months, since no real customer_orders schema exists yet to confirm the true grain against (same open item STORY-001 logged).
  - Verification: 18/18 tests passing (`python3 -m pytest forecasting/tests -v`) — forecasting/tests/test_demand_model.py (11 tests: happy path, year-boundary rollover, negative-forecast clipping, empty/short history, bad periods_ahead/season_length, malformed/duplicate periods, out-of-order input) and forecasting/tests/test_data_quality.py (7 tests: clean series, sparse history, gap detection, non-positive counts, empty history, single point, unordered input). Also manually ran `forecast_demand()`/`assess_data_quality()` end-to-end and confirmed a perfectly linear series produces confidence 1.0.

## STORY-004 — Stockout Risk Prediction

- [x] Scaffold inventory_risk/ core: deterministic stockout-risk model + inventory data-quality assessment
  - Date: 2026-08-21
  - Session: CC-20260821-v2q8
  - What changed: Added inventory_risk/risk_model.py (`assess_stockout_risk()`: given an `InventoryPosition` (current stock, safety stock, a daily demand rate from turnover or forecast, and a replenishment lead time), classifies risk as low/medium/high/critical by comparing days-of-supply against lead-time coverage and the safety-stock floor; confidence is the normalized distance from the nearest risk-level boundary, not a made-up number — a position sitting right on a threshold gets low confidence, one deep in a bucket gets high confidence; raises `RiskModelError` on negative stock/demand or a non-positive lead time, since those make the arithmetic itself meaningless) and inventory_risk/data_quality.py (`assess_inventory_data_quality()`: partitions raw inventory rows into clean vs. flagged-for-review with per-row human-readable reasons — missing fields, non-numeric values including the `bool`-is-not-numeric edge case, negative stock/demand, non-positive lead time — so a corrupted row is excluded from risk assessment rather than silently producing a number). Mirrors forecasting/demand_model.py + forecasting/data_quality.py's split (pure computation, no I/O) from STORY-003.
  - Verification: 25/25 new tests passing (`python3 -m pytest inventory_risk/tests -v`) — test_risk_model.py (13 tests: each risk-level boundary, zero-demand-rate edge cases, confidence bounded [0,1] and lower near a boundary than far from one, all 5 invalid-input cases raising `RiskModelError`) and test_data_quality.py (12 tests: clean passthrough, empty input, missing/entirely-absent field, non-numeric including bool, each negative-value case, non-positive lead time, mixed clean+flagged partitioning, multiple issues on one row all reported). Full repo suite (`agents/tests forecasting/tests data_integration/tests inventory_risk/tests`) still 121/121 passing — no regressions.
  - Notes: `daily_demand_rate` is accepted as a plain caller-supplied number; this step does not yet decide *where* it comes from (turnover-derived vs. STORY-003's `forecast_demand()` output) — that wiring is the next step, in the agent layer, matching how STORY-003 kept forecasting/demand_model.py source-agnostic about its input rows too.

- [x] Wire inventory_risk/ into a StockoutRiskAgent plugging into the STORY-002 Orchestrator
  - Date: 2026-08-21
  - Session: CC-20260821-v2q8
  - What changed: Added agents/stockout_risk_agent.py (`StockoutRiskAgent`, implementing STORY-002's `Agent` protocol): runs `assess_inventory_data_quality()` on `query.context["inventory_rows"]` first, builds an `InventoryPosition` per clean row, calls `assess_stockout_risk()` per SKU, and returns one `AgentResponse` — "ok" with a per-SKU recommendation (sorted most-severe-first) and a mean-of-per-SKU confidence, or "error" when no SKU could be assessed at all. Corrupted rows are excluded and their reasons folded into the recommendation as "Data quality notes" (same pattern `DemandForecastingAgent` uses for data-quality warnings) — this is what satisfies acceptance criterion 2 ("given inaccurate inventory data ... flag the data for review"). A clean row the risk model itself still rejects (`RiskModelError`) is isolated per-SKU rather than discarding every other SKU's prediction, matching this repo's existing per-dataset/per-agent failure-isolation convention. Every successful per-SKU prediction is logged individually (`stockout_risk_predicted`, with sku/risk_level/confidence in context) so acceptance criterion 3's "logs all stockout risk predictions with timestamps and confidence levels" holds at the SKU level, not just as one blended number. No changes to agents/orchestrator.py were needed. Fixed one real bug found while writing this, before it ever shipped: `days_of_supply` is `math.inf` for a zero-demand SKU, and `json.dumps()` (used by `agents/logging_setup.py`'s `JsonFormatter`, per CLAUDE.md's Observability Framework requiring valid JSON logs) silently emits the non-standard `Infinity` token for that by default instead of raising — normalized `days_of_supply` to `null` in the log context before it ever reaches the logger.
  - Verification: 12/12 new tests passing (`python3 -m pytest agents/tests/test_stockout_risk_agent.py -v`) — happy path via `validate_response()`, confidence equals the mean of independently-computed per-SKU confidences, most-severe-first ordering, missing/empty `inventory_rows`, all-rows-corrupted, partial corruption folded into the recommendation with the bad SKU excluded, per-SKU `RiskModelError` isolation (one bad SKU doesn't discard a sibling's valid prediction) via a monkeypatched flaky `assess_stockout_risk`, all-clean-rows-fail-prediction returns a clean error, a genuinely unexpected exception propagates (the "prediction API failure" path, left to the Orchestrator's existing `agent_communication_failed` handling), and a dedicated regression test confirming the `math.inf` fix - the logged `days_of_supply` is `None` and `json.dumps(..., allow_nan=False)` on the logged context doesn't raise. Full repo suite (`agents/tests forecasting/tests data_integration/tests inventory_risk/tests`) 133/133 passing, no regressions. Also manually ran `Orchestrator([StockoutRiskAgent()]).coordinate(...)` end-to-end with 3 simulated SKUs (one deliberately corrupted with negative stock) and confirmed: `outcome="success"`, the corrupted SKU excluded and noted in "Data quality notes", the other two SKUs' `high`/`low` risk levels and confidences both in the printed recommendation and in the live JSON log lines (`stockout_risk_predicted` × 2, each with a timestamp and confidence, one with `"days_of_supply": 166.67` and one with a finite value - no `Infinity` token in any log line).
  - Notes: All 5 of STORY-004's listed failure paths are now handled and tested: data synchronization issues (missing/empty `inventory_rows`), inventory data corruption (`assess_inventory_data_quality` flagging), model prediction errors (per-SKU `RiskModelError` isolation), incorrect risk thresholds (`risk_model.py`'s `RiskModelError` guards plus the boundary-distance confidence scoring), and prediction API failure (unexpected-exception propagation to the Orchestrator's existing handling). Deliberately did not build anything from STORY-005 (risk detection/anomaly analysis) or STORY-006 (recommendation generation) - this agent classifies and reports risk, it does not detect anomalies across SKUs or generate mitigation recommendations beyond the risk classification itself.

Logged assumption (not escalated, per this repo's autonomy model): the `inventory` table has one row per SKU with columns sku/current_stock/safety_stock/daily_demand_rate/lead_time_days, `daily_demand_rate` assumed to be a turnover-derived figure the source system already maintains (REQ-006's "turnover") - no real inventory schema exists yet to confirm the true column names against, the same open item STORY-001/003 logged for their own tables. - [x] Multi-angle code review and fixes prior to commit
  - Date: 2026-08-21
  - Session: CC-20260821-v2q8
  - What changed: Ran `/code-review` (medium) against the full staged STORY-004 diff (`inventory_risk/`, `agents/stockout_risk_agent.py`, tests). Fixed 1 severe correctness bug and 3 hardening/robustness gaps, each independently corroborated by 2+ of the review's parallel angles:
    1. **NaN silently passed both validation layers and produced a confidently wrong "low risk, confidence 1.00" prediction.** Every Python comparison against `float('nan')` (`< 0`, `<= 0`, `<=`) evaluates `False`, so a NaN `current_stock`/`safety_stock`/`daily_demand_rate`/`lead_time_days` slipped past `inventory_risk/data_quality.py`'s `_row_issues` as "clean," then past `inventory_risk/risk_model.py`'s `_validate` too, then fell through every risk-level branch in `assess_stockout_risk` to the final `else` (`"low"`), with `boundary_distance_days` itself becoming NaN and `max(0.0, min(1.0, NaN/lead_time_days))` evaluating to `1.0` in CPython. Confirmed live before fixing (see verification below). This is squarely the "inventory data corruption" failure path this story is required to handle, not a hypothetical - fixed by adding an explicit `math.isnan()` guard in both `data_quality._row_issues` (rejects the row with a `"<field> is NaN"` reason before it ever reaches the risk model) and `risk_model._validate` (defense-in-depth for a caller that constructs an `InventoryPosition` directly, bypassing the agent/data-quality layer).
    2. `StockoutRiskAgent` computed `assess_inventory_data_quality()`'s `flagged_rows` (each with a specific, human-readable reason) but only surfaced the aggregate `warnings` count ("N of M rows flagged") in the recommendation/error - the specific "which SKU, why" a reviewer actually needs was silently dropped, undercutting acceptance criterion 2's "flag the data for review" intent. Fixed: the agent now logs one `inventory_row_flagged_for_review` warning per flagged row (sku + reasons in context) and builds its own per-row description (`_describe_flagged_row`) used in both the all-corrupted error message and the "Data quality notes" section of the recommendation.
    3. `inventory_risk/run_sample_risk_assessment.py` indexed `run.results[0]` unconditionally, which would raise an opaque `IndexError` instead of surfacing `run.crash_error` if `Orchestrator.coordinate()` ever hit its own internal crash path (a real, already-modeled `CoordinationRun` state per STORY-002, just not yet triggered in practice). Fixed: an explicit `if not run.results` guard prints the crash reason and exits 1 instead of raising.
    4. `agents/stockout_risk_agent.py`'s JSON-log normalization only guarded `math.isinf`, not `math.isnan`, for `days_of_supply` - extended to cover both, and `_RISK_SEVERITY[a.risk_level]` (a hard dict index) was changed to `.get(a.risk_level, _UNKNOWN_SEVERITY)` so a hypothetical future `RiskLevel` value can't raise an uncaught `KeyError` inside `_format_recommendation` and get misclassified by the Orchestrator as `agent_communication_failed`.
    Also made two small, low-risk simplifications flagged by the same review: `InventoryPosition(**{f: row[f] for f in REQUIRED_FIELDS})` replaces a manually-typed 5-field mapping (single-sources the field list from `data_quality.REQUIRED_FIELDS` instead of duplicating it, while still safely ignoring any extra columns a real inventory row might carry), and `risk_model.py`'s repeated `lead_time_days * MEDIUM_COVERAGE_RATIO` expression is now computed once per call.
  - Verification: Reproduced the NaN bug live before fixing (`assess_stockout_risk` on a NaN `current_stock` returned `risk_level="low", confidence=1.0`), then reproduced it fixed (raises `RiskModelError`). Added 6 new regression tests, each confirmed testing the actual fix: `inventory_risk/tests/test_data_quality.py::test_nan_value_is_flagged_not_treated_as_clean` (all 4 numeric fields), `inventory_risk/tests/test_risk_model.py::test_nan_value_raises_risk_model_error_instead_of_producing_a_silent_low_risk_result` (parametrized across all 4 fields, 4 tests), and `agents/tests/test_stockout_risk_agent.py::test_run_flags_a_nan_row_for_review_instead_of_silently_predicting_low_risk` (end-to-end through the agent). Updated 2 existing agent tests to assert on the new, more specific error/recommendation content instead of just the old aggregate-count message. Full repo suite (`agents/tests forecasting/tests data_integration/tests inventory_risk/tests`) now 139/139 passing. Manually re-ran the 4-SKU end-to-end scenario (2 healthy, 1 negative-stock, 1 NaN) through `Orchestrator([StockoutRiskAgent()]).coordinate(...)` and confirmed both bad rows produce individual `inventory_row_flagged_for_review` log lines with their specific reasons, and both SKUs are named with their reasons in the recommendation's "Data quality notes" section.
  - Notes: Did not pursue two lower-value findings from the same review: (a) converting `RiskLevel` from a `Literal` to a full `enum.IntEnum` for severity ordering - no second `RiskLevel` consumer exists yet in this repo and the `.get()` fallback already closes the actual failure mode, so a type redesign now would be speculative per this repo's own "don't design for hypothetical future requirements" guidance; (b) `inventory_risk/run_sample_risk_assessment.py`'s `DEFAULT_AUDIT_LOG_PATH`/`_audit_store()` block is now a third verbatim copy of the same ~5 lines also in `data_integration/run_sample_integration.py` and `forecasting/run_sample_forecast.py`, crossing this repo's own "three occurrences -> lift it" threshold - not extracted here because doing so would mean editing `forecasting/run_sample_forecast.py`, a file that belongs to STORY-003, outside this story's scope; flagging it here as a follow-up, same as STORY-002's sequential-dispatch note and STORY-003's duplicated-query-string note were left for their own future stories.

- [x] STORY-004 follow-up: fix confidence-scaling inconsistency in the stockout risk model
  - Date: 2026-08-21
  - Session: CC-20260821-h4t8
  - What changed: An independent read-through of the already-committed `inventory_risk/risk_model.py` (requested by the user to raise confidence on the prior STORY-004 completion, not new scope) found that `assess_stockout_risk()`'s confidence metric was not comparable across risk levels, despite its docstring claiming "distance from the nearest risk-level boundary": (1) the "high" zone measured distance to only its upper edge (the medium threshold), never checking how close current_stock was to the safety_stock floor right below it - a SKU one unit above the critical floor could report confidence 0.94; (2) the "medium" zone correctly took the min distance to both its edges, but normalized by the full lead time instead of the zone's own (narrower, 0.5x lead time) width, so its confidence structurally capped at ~0.25 even dead-center in the zone. Fixed both: "high" now takes the min of distance-to-critical (converted to days-of-supply units via `(current_stock - safety_stock) / daily_demand_rate`) and distance-to-medium-threshold; "medium" now normalizes by its own zone half-width instead of the full lead time. Risk-level classification itself is unchanged - only the confidence number.
  - Verification: Added `test_high_risk_confidence_drops_near_the_critical_floor` and `test_medium_risk_confidence_reaches_near_one_at_the_zone_center` to `inventory_risk/tests/test_risk_model.py`, both reproducing the specific before/after cases discussed with the user. Full existing suite still passes with no other assertions changed: `python3 -m pytest inventory_risk/tests agents/tests/test_stockout_risk_agent.py -q` -> 45 passed. Full repo suite: `python3 -m pytest -q` -> 141 passed.

No governance boundary crossed (local, reversible, low blast radius - only the confidence field of an existing response, no consumer of that field elsewhere in the repo yet).

## STORY-012 — Intelligence Model Implementation

- [x] Four-stage intelligence model (Observe -> Understand -> Predict -> Recommend), per-stage logging and audit trail, wired to a runnable demo
  - Date: 2026-08-21
  - Session: CC-20260821-k3mq
  - What changed: Added a new `intelligence/` package implementing REQ-003's four-stage model as an explicit pipeline over STORY-003's already-audited demand forecaster, rather than introducing new prediction math:
    - `intelligence/contracts.py` - frozen dataclasses for each stage's output (`Observation`, `Understanding`, `Prediction`, `Recommendation`), `StageName`/`StageOutcome` literals, and `StageError` (the typed exception a stage raises when it can't produce output from its input, as opposed to an unexpected bug).
    - `intelligence/audit_trail.py` - `StageAuditStore`, a JSONL-backed, idempotent audit trail for stage executions, keyed on `(run_id, stage)` - adapted directly from STORY-011's `data_integration/audit_trail.py::AuditStore`, same corrupted-line tolerance and loud-failure-on-write-error behavior, just re-keyed for "one stage of one pipeline run" instead of "one dataset pull."
    - `intelligence/logging_setup.py` - structured JSON logger stamped `"service": "intelligence"`, mirroring `agents/logging_setup.py`'s existing per-package pattern.
    - `intelligence/model.py` - `IntelligenceModel.run()`: Observe (`forecasting/aggregation.py`'s `aggregate_monthly_demand()`, raw rows -> structured `DemandPoint`s), Understand (`forecasting/data_quality.py`'s `assess_data_quality()` plus a simple trend read), Predict (`forecasting/demand_model.py`'s `forecast_demand()`), Recommend (a deterministic rule: low-confidence forecasts (<0.4 R^2) recommend gathering more data instead of a quantity; otherwise the trend direction drives an increase/reduce/maintain action). Every stage is logged (`stage_started`/`stage_completed`/`stage_failed`) and recorded to the `StageAuditStore` regardless of outcome. A stage failure halts the pipeline immediately - later stages are recorded `"not_processed"`, not silently skipped, so "data not processed through all stages" is always visible in the audit trail. An unexpected (non-`StageError`) exception is caught at the pipeline level (mirroring `agents/orchestrator.py`'s `CoordinationRun.crash_error` pattern) and returned as a crashed `IntelligenceRun` with every not-yet-recorded stage still audited, rather than propagating and leaving the trail incomplete.
    - `intelligence/run_sample_intelligence_model.py` - demo entry point, following `forecasting/run_sample_forecast.py`'s exact shape: pulls `customer_orders` via STORY-011's `run_integration_with_audit()` (shares that script's default `SUPPLYMIND_AUDIT_LOG_PATH`, so a pull already recorded by one script isn't double-logged by the other), feeds the rows through `IntelligenceModel`, prints the full stage-by-stage trace as JSON.
    - `intelligence/tests/` - 18 tests covering the happy path (all four stages succeed, each stage's output content), the "data not processed through all stages" failure path (halts at Observe on empty input, halts at Observe on rows missing required fields, halts at Predict on too-short history - each verifying downstream stages are recorded `"not_processed"`), the low-confidence recommendation branch, and the audit trail (record-on-success, record-through-a-halt, idempotent re-run, disk persistence, corrupted-line tolerance).
    - `.gitignore` - added `stage_audit_log.jsonl` (the new package's runtime audit log), matching the existing `audit_log.jsonl` entry for STORY-011's trail.
    No existing file was modified other than `.gitignore` - `forecasting/`, `data_integration/`, `agents/orchestrator.py`, and `agents/contracts.py` are all reused read-only, unchanged.
  - Verification: `python3 -m pytest intelligence/tests -q` -> 18 passed. Full repo suite `python3 -m pytest -q` -> 158 passed (confirmed each prior story's suite individually too: `data_integration/tests` 28 passed, `agents/tests` 47 passed, `forecasting/tests` 34 passed, `inventory_risk/tests` 32 passed - no regressions from this story's additions). Ran `intelligence/run_sample_intelligence_model.py` live with no PostgreSQL credentials configured (this environment's current state): pipeline halts at Observe with "no raw data provided to observe," exit code 1 - the "data not processed through all stages" failure path, triggered by a fully missing dataset, with all four stages still individually recorded in the stage audit trail (`observe: failure`, the rest `not_processed`). Ran the pipeline directly against 12 months of synthetic increasing-demand rows: all four stages succeed, `Observation.period_range` and per-period points correct, `Understanding.trend_direction == "increasing"`, `Prediction` produces 3 forecast points at confidence 1.00, `Recommendation.action` proposes a stock increase - satisfying acceptance criteria 1 and 2 (observations from raw inputs; understanding insights from observations) end-to-end, not just via unit tests.
  - Notes: Acceptance criterion 3 (audit trail of model stages) is satisfied by `StageAuditStore`, exercised in both the success and halted-pipeline demo runs above and covered by 6 dedicated tests. Confidence-threshold (0.4) and default periods-ahead (3) are logged implementation-level assumptions, not escalated, consistent with this repo's autonomy model - tunable if they prove wrong in practice, same status as STORY-003's own logged assumptions about the customer_orders schema. Next: confirm the 3 acceptance criteria in `docs/stories/STORY-012.md` and `.colaberry/progress.json`, then commit.

## Scope Log — proposals not yet built (per CLAUDE.md's Scope Lock: logged here, not built past a governance boundary, escalated separately for expansion approval)

### Proposed: Dataset Onboarding & Schema Mapping

- Date: 2026-08-21
- Session: CC-20260821-k3mq
- Raised by: user, while discussing how to get realistic data for STORY-012's demo.
- Problem: today, connecting a new data source (a new company's PostgreSQL schema) requires a human to hand-write the SQL query and know the exact column names in code (e.g. `intelligence/run_sample_intelligence_model.py`'s `CUSTOMER_ORDERS_DATASET` query, or the `date_field`/`quantity_field` arguments passed into `DemandForecastingAgent`/`IntelligenceModel`). The pipeline itself is already schema-agnostic at the mechanical level - `PostgresDataset` takes an arbitrary query, and field names are parameters, not hardcoded - but there is no onboarding layer that lets a new tenant register their own connection + column mapping once and have every downstream stage (data_integration -> forecasting -> intelligence) use it automatically, with a clear validation error at connect-time if a required field is missing.
- Why not built now: this is a new architectural capability spanning `data_integration`, every consuming agent, and (implicitly) multi-tenant credential storage - a cross-module dependency shift, not an implementation detail of any story already on the roadmap. Checked `docs/stories/STORY-005.md` and `STORY-006.md` (the next two stories after STORY-012 in the roadmap) - neither covers this. `docs/**` and `.colaberry/plan.json` are platform-generated (see `docs/CONNECT-YOUR-REPO.md`: "The platform only ever writes ... `docs/**` and `.colaberry/**`"), so Claude Code should not hand-author a new `docs/stories/STORY-0xx.md` or a new REQ into `docs/REQUIREMENTS.md`/`docs/TRACEABILITY.md` directly - the canonical source is `.colaberry/plan.json`, which only the portal writes (the one carve-out, per the STORY-012 brief itself, is `.colaberry/progress.json` - verification state, not the plan). User confirmed: they will add this as a new requirement/story via the portal's plan editor.
- Suggested requirement text (for the portal): "The system must let a new data source be connected via a reusable connection profile (credentials + table/query + column-name mapping), validated against the real schema at connect-time, without requiring code changes per tenant."
- Suggested story shape (for the portal): As a [platform operator / customer onboarding a new company], I want to register a data source's connection details and column mapping once, so that every predictive stage (forecasting, stockout risk, the intelligence model) can use it without hand-written per-tenant code. Failure paths to handle: missing/invalid credentials, a required column absent from the real schema (fail loud at connect-time, not at forecast-time), a column mapped to a value of the wrong type. Trust: every connection attempt and schema validation logged with timestamps and outcome.
- Next: once this appears as a real story in `.colaberry/plan.json` / `docs/stories/`, pick it up as its own build - blocked conceptually by nothing already shipped (STORY-001/011's `data_integration` layer is the natural place to add it).
- Update (same date, same session): user cannot add stories to the portal's plan themselves (it was not generated by them - likely instructor/platform-owned). Decided to build the connection-profile layer now anyway, as out-of-plan work: it will not appear in the Command Center or earn portal points until a matching story exists in `.colaberry/plan.json`, and this file's entries for it are logged without a STORY-0xx prefix for that reason. Landing in `data_integration/` since that is where STORY-001/011 already built the connector/audit-trail layer this extends.
- Update (2026-08-25, session CC-20260824-t8m3): while wrapping up STORY-005, user independently proposed the same idea again, GUI-specific this time - when data is loaded, suggest a column mapping and let the user confirm or edit it before it's used, surfaced as an actual UI step rather than a script argument. Two people landing on the same shape of solution a few days apart is a decent signal it's worth building eventually. Flagged the scope honestly before touching anything: there is no data-loading GUI in this repo to attach it to - `command-center/` is a build-progress dashboard reading `.colaberry/plan.json`, not a place where anyone connects or uploads data - and a real "confirm this mapping" UI needs a new frontend talking to a backend that doesn't exist yet either (this repo is Python modules + one-off scripts, no running API server). That's a new architectural capability, the same conclusion as the original proposal, not a small addition to STORY-005. Asked the user directly via AskUserQuestion (log-and-continue vs. start-building-now); they chose log-and-continue. Decision: still not built. `validate_mapping_completeness()`/`validate_against_live_schema()`'s existing error messages (`"tenant 'X' is missing a column mapping for field Y"`, etc.) are already close to what a confirm/edit UI would need to render as human-readable prompts, so the backend groundwork for this GUI is further along than a fresh build would suggest - worth reusing directly when this is picked up, rather than re-deriving mapping-diagnostic logic in the frontend.

- [x] Built: connection-profile / schema-mapping layer (out-of-plan, no story behind it)
  - Date: 2026-08-21
  - Session: CC-20260821-k3mq
  - What changed: Added `data_integration/connection_profile.py`:
    - `ConnectionProfile` - tenant id, `PostgresConfig`, table/query, and `column_mapping: dict[str, str]` (canonical field name -> that tenant's actual column name). A `DatasetKind` registry (`REQUIRED_FIELDS_BY_DATASET_KIND`) declares which canonical fields each dataset kind needs - `"customer_orders"` reuses the exact field names `agents/demand_forecasting_agent.py` already defaults to (`order_date`, `quantity`); `"inventory"` reuses `inventory_risk/data_quality.py`'s existing `REQUIRED_FIELDS` directly rather than duplicating it.
    - Layered validation: `validate_mapping_completeness()` (pure, no I/O - every required field must have a mapped column) -> `validate_against_live_schema()` (probes the real table/query via a new `postgres_connector.fetch_columns()` - a `LIMIT 0` subquery read via `cursor.description`, zero rows fetched - and confirms every mapped column actually exists there) -> `validate_profile()` (runs both, cheapest first). Both layers raise a typed `SchemaMappingError` naming the specific tenant and field/column, satisfying "fail loud at connect-time, not at forecast-time" from the original proposal.
    - `remap_rows()` - pure translation from a tenant's raw column names to canonical field names. This is the piece that lets `forecasting/`, `inventory_risk/`, and `intelligence/` consume any tenant's data with **zero code changes** - proven directly in the new demo (below).
    - `fetch_profile_data()` - the audited, I/O-performing entry point: validates, fetches via `postgres_connector.fetch_rows()`, remaps, and records one entry to a `data_integration/audit_trail.py::AuditStore` (STORY-011's existing trust spine) per attempt - success keyed by tenant+dataset+content-fingerprint (mirrors `run_integration_with_audit()`'s own idempotency keying exactly, re-pulling identical data doesn't duplicate the trail), failure keyed fresh each time (a repeated failure is diagnostic, not deduplicated away). Unlike `run_integration_with_audit()`, this raises on failure rather than returning a result object - a single profile is one tenant's whole pull, with no sibling result to protect, so the caller's own error handling decides what happens next.
    - `data_integration/postgres_connector.py` - added `fetch_columns()` (purely additive; `fetch_rows()` itself untouched, same retry/logging behavior).
    - `data_integration/run_sample_connection_profile_demo.py` - demo proving the actual point: two fictitious tenants (`acme_retail` with columns `OrderDate`/`Qty`, `globex_supply` with columns `txn_dt`/`units_sold`) both flow through the *unmodified* `DemandForecastingAgent`/`Orchestrator` (STORY-002/003) and `IntelligenceModel` (STORY-012) and produce correct, tenant-specific results, plus a third, deliberately misconfigured tenant caught by `validate_mapping_completeness()` before touching any downstream code. No live PostgreSQL is configured in this environment, so the demo uses synthetic raw rows standing in for `fetch_rows()`'s return value (documented in the script) - the real, I/O-performing, audited path (`fetch_profile_data()`) is exercised in its own tests via a mocked connection instead.
    - 25 new tests: `data_integration/tests/test_connection_profile.py` (mapping completeness, live-schema validation including the "incomplete mapping never reaches the database" case, `remap_rows` including a same-shape-from-different-schemas proof, `fetch_profile_data`'s success/failure/idempotency paths) and additions to `test_postgres_connector.py` (`fetch_columns`'s happy path, `LIMIT 0` subquery wrapping, and retry behavior).
  - Verification: `python3 -m pytest data_integration/tests/test_connection_profile.py data_integration/tests/test_postgres_connector.py -q` -> 25 passed. Full repo suite `python3 -m pytest -q` -> 183 passed (no regressions). Ran `data_integration/run_sample_connection_profile_demo.py` live: both tenants produced correct forecasts from their own raw column names (`acme_retail` trend increasing -> "increase stock toward 204 units for 2026-01"; `globex_supply` trend decreasing -> "reduce next-order quantity toward 105 units for 2026-01") through both the STORY-003 forecasting agent and the STORY-012 intelligence pipeline, unmodified; the misconfigured third tenant was caught with `"tenant 'initech_widgets' (customer_orders) is missing a column mapping for required field(s): quantity"` before any forecasting code ran.
  - Notes: No `STORY-0xx` prefix on this entry or its commit - out-of-plan work per the Scope Log entry above; will not appear in the Command Center or earn portal points until a matching story exists in `.colaberry/plan.json`. Left `fetch_profile_data()`'s content-fingerprint helper (`_content_fingerprint()`) as a small, deliberate duplicate of `data_integration/orchestrator.py`'s own private helper of the same name/logic, rather than importing a private symbol across modules or refactoring `orchestrator.py` (an already-shipped STORY-001/011 file) to export it - two occurrences, below this repo's own "three occurrences -> lift it" threshold; flagging as a follow-up if a third caller ever needs the same fingerprinting. Credential storage/sourcing for a real multi-tenant deployment (how a `PostgresConfig` per tenant actually gets populated - a secrets store, an admin UI, etc.) is explicitly out of scope for this pass; `ConnectionProfile.postgres` just holds whatever `PostgresConfig` the caller constructs.

- [x] STORY-005: Risk detection and anomaly analysis (demand spikes, supplier delays, unified Supply Chain Risk Score)
  - Date: 2026-08-25
  - Session: CC-20260824-t8m3
  - What changed: Built in four paced, user-confirmed steps (per the story's own "one step at a time" instruction), each verified against hand-computed expected values via a Python REPL before the test file was written:
    - `risk_detection/anomaly_detection.py` - two pure, deterministic detectors (REQ-009). `detect_demand_spikes()` reuses STORY-003's `DemandPoint`/monthly history and flags outlier periods via a leave-one-out z-score (each period scored against the mean/stdev of every *other* period, so an extreme value can't drag its own baseline toward itself and mask its own deviation - the standard small-sample failure mode a same-baseline z-score has). Raises `AnomalyDetectionError` for history too short to build a baseline at all (<4 periods) or a non-positive `z_threshold` - a structural/parameter problem, not a data problem. A zero-variance baseline (all-identical history) scores any deviation as certain (z=inf) rather than dividing by zero. Severity (medium/high/critical) is threshold-width zones beyond `z_threshold`. `detect_supplier_delays()` takes raw delivery-row dicts (logged schema assumption - no real `delivery_records` schema exists yet, same situation STORY-001/003/004 were already in: `po_id`, `expected_date`, `actual_date`, optional `supplier`), accepts either ISO date strings or native `date`/`datetime` values, and isolates malformed rows into `flagged_rows` with per-row reasons rather than failing the whole batch (the "data inconsistency" failure path) - only a bad *parameter* (non-positive `delay_threshold_days`) raises.
    - `risk_detection/risk_score.py` - `compute_risk_score()` (REQ-012), combining demand anomalies + supplier delays + optional STORY-004 stockout assessments into one 0-100 `SupplyChainRiskScore` with a severity and a plain-language explanation naming the top contributing factors (REQ-012's "explain why it is high"). Overall severity is never milder than any single contributing factor's own severity (one critical factor alone reads as critical overall, regardless of its point value against the bucket thresholds), but several lesser factors can *compound* past that floor (four independent "medium" factors escalate to "high" together even though no single one does alone) - verified numerically for both the floor and compounding behavior before the score-bucket thresholds (10/35/60) were finalized. A "low" stockout-risk SKU contributes nothing; that's STORY-004's own signal to surface on its own, not evidence of elevated cross-cutting risk here.
    - `agents/risk_detection_agent.py` - `RiskDetectionAgent`, wrapping all three pieces as an `Agent` (STORY-002's `Protocol`), plugged into the existing `Orchestrator` with zero changes to orchestration logic (verified live through the real `Orchestrator.coordinate()`, not just called directly). Each of the three signals (demand history, delivery rows, inventory rows) is independently optional and independently degradable: a per-signal problem (short demand history, a malformed delivery/inventory row, a per-SKU risk-model error) is logged and folded into "Data quality notes" with a lowered confidence, rather than failing the whole run - verified live with a mixed-bad-rows scenario producing a partial `"ok"` response. The agent returns `status="error"` (logged as `risk_detection_failed`, the "log the error and notify the user" acceptance criterion, same log+return-error convention STORY-003/004 already established) only when none of `demand_history`/`delivery_rows`/`inventory_rows` was supplied at all. Every individual detected anomaly is logged at `info` with period/po_id, direction, and severity (`demand_anomaly_detected`/`supplier_delay_detected`) - the acceptance criterion 3 "logs all detected anomalies and risks with timestamps and severity levels" (timestamps are stamped unconditionally by the existing `JsonFormatter`). An infinite z-score is normalized to `None` before logging, the same JSON-safety guard STORY-004 already applies to `days_of_supply`.
    - `risk_detection/run_sample_risk_detection.py` - demo entry point, same shape as `run_sample_risk_assessment.py`/`run_sample_forecast.py`: pulls `customer_orders`, `delivery_records`, `inventory` via STORY-011's audited `run_integration_with_audit()`, wires `RiskDetectionAgent` through the real `Orchestrator`, prints the risk score. Ran live with no PostgreSQL credentials configured (this environment's current state): all three dataset pulls fail, agent returns the "no supply chain data provided for risk detection" error, exit code 1 - the acceptance criterion 2 failure path, demoed end-to-end exactly like STORY-003/004's own demos in the same environment state.
    - `risk_detection/tests/` (35 tests) and `agents/tests/test_risk_detection_agent.py` (9 tests) - happy path, every failure path listed in the story (insufficient history, malformed/missing delivery and inventory rows, non-positive thresholds, zero-variance/flat-history false-positive avoidance, ordinary month-to-month variation producing zero false positives), severity-boundary and score-compounding behavior, sort-order determinism, and logging content/JSON-safety.
    - `docs/stories/STORY-005.md` - all three acceptance boxes ticked; each is demonstrated above, not just asserted.

Full repo suite: `python3 -m pytest -q` -> 227 passed, no regressions. `python3 -m risk_detection.run_sample_risk_detection` run live (see above) - exit code 1, `"no supply chain data provided for risk detection"`, matching the acceptance criterion 2 failure path exactly. Direct end-to-end run (bypassing the missing-credentials demo) against synthetic demand-spike + supplier-delay + stockout-risk data confirmed all three signals combine correctly through the real `Orchestrator`: score 85/100, severity `critical`, recommendation naming all three contributing factors by period/po_id/sku.

    - Ran `risk_detection/run_sample_risk_detection.py` for the first time against a real, running PostgreSQL server (not synthetic dicts). This surfaced a genuine, pre-existing bug in already-shipped STORY-004 code: `inventory_risk/data_quality.py`'s numeric-field check used `isinstance(value, (int, float))`, but a real PostgreSQL `NUMERIC` column comes back from psycopg2 as `decimal.Decimal`, which fails that check - every real-data inventory row was silently flagged "not numeric" and excluded from stockout-risk assessment, invisible until now because every existing test used plain float literals, never a real database round-trip. Per STORY-005's own "stop and ask if you're about to change a file outside this story" instruction, flagged it to the user before fixing (the bug is in STORY-004's file, not STORY-005's) - user said fix it and check for the same pattern elsewhere.
    - Fixed `inventory_risk/data_quality.py`: `_row_issues()` now accepts `Decimal` alongside `int`/`float` (with an equivalent `Decimal.is_nan()` guard, mirroring the existing `float`/NaN check), and `assess_inventory_data_quality()` normalizes every numeric field to `float` when building `clean_rows` - one boundary conversion so nothing downstream (`inventory_risk/risk_model.py`'s arithmetic, which mixes these values with plain float constants like `MEDIUM_COVERAGE_RATIO` and would raise `TypeError` on a bare `Decimal`/`float` mix) ever has to know `Decimal` exists. `risk_model.py` itself was left unchanged - both of its only two callers (`agents/stockout_risk_agent.py`, `agents/risk_detection_agent.py`) already build `InventoryPosition` exclusively from `assess_inventory_data_quality()`'s `clean_rows`, so the normalization fully covers them by contract.
    - Checked for the same pattern elsewhere per the user's request: `grep -rn "isinstance(.*float\|isinstance(.*int," --include="*.py"` across the whole repo (excluding tests) found only the two lines in `inventory_risk/data_quality.py`/`risk_model.py` already covered above. Independently verified `forecasting/aggregation.py`'s quantity handling (`float(raw_quantity)`) already works correctly against `Decimal` - unlike an `isinstance` check, `float()` calls `Decimal.__float__` and coerces correctly - confirmed live with a `Decimal`-valued row through `aggregate_monthly_demand()`. `data_integration/connection_profile.py`'s `remap_rows()` does no numeric validation (column renaming only), so it isn't affected either.
    - Added regression tests: `inventory_risk/tests/test_data_quality.py` (`Decimal` accepted and normalized to `float`, a negative `Decimal` still flagged, `Decimal('NaN')` still flagged - the same three cases the existing `int`/`float` tests already covered, now proven for the type that was actually breaking) and `agents/tests/test_stockout_risk_agent.py` (`Decimal`-valued rows produce a valid `"ok"` response end-to-end through the full agent, not just at the data_quality layer in isolation - this is what actually failed in the live demo run).
  - Verification: Full repo suite `python3 -m pytest -q` -> 231 passed (4 new: 3 in `test_data_quality.py`, 1 in `test_stockout_risk_agent.py`; no regressions in the 227 already passing). Live run: `eval "$(python3 scripts/local_test_db.py)" && python3 -m risk_detection.run_sample_risk_detection` - before the fix, `SKU-GIZMO` (the seeded critical-stock SKU) was silently excluded and the score was 60/100; after the fix, all three signals combine correctly: score 85/100, severity `critical`, recommendation naming the July demand spike, PO-1003's 15-day delay, AND `SKU-GIZMO`'s stockout risk by name - the acceptance-criterion-1 demo working against a real database, not just synthetic data or a missing-credentials failure path.
  - Notes: `pgserver` is this repo's first new pip dependency - added deliberately, confirmed on PyPI first, dev/test-only (never imported by `data_integration/`, `forecasting/`, `inventory_risk/`, `risk_detection/`, or `agents/` - only by the new `scripts/local_test_db.py`). No `requirements.txt`/`pyproject.toml` exists yet at the repo root to pin it in (only `data_integration/requirements.txt`, which is that subpackage's own I/O-dependency list, not the right place for a dev-only tool) - flagging as a follow-up if this repo adds one. This local Postgres is a personal dev/test convenience, not a shared or production data source - `scripts/.local_pgdata/` is gitignored and nothing about it is portable to another machine without re-running the script.

## STORY-006 — Recommendation Generation

- [x] STORY-006 step 1: additive `AgentFinding` type and `AgentResponse.findings` field (agents/contracts.py)
  - Date: 2026-08-26
  - Session: CC-20260826-90f4
  - What changed: Before writing the Recommendation Agent itself, extended the one shared contract every agent already returns through (`agents/contracts.py`'s `AgentResponse`) so agents that naturally have a per-subject breakdown (a SKU, a PO, a period) can expose it, instead of only a single blended free-text `recommendation`. This is what lets STORY-006's Recommendation Agent detect *precise* conflicts (the same SKU getting a different risk read from two different agents) rather than only a coarser confidence-spread or keyword heuristic - discussed and agreed with the user first, including the real limit that `DemandForecastingAgent` has no per-subject dimension at all (it only forecasts an aggregate total, never per-SKU) and so cannot participate in precise per-subject conflict detection without a much larger rewrite of STORY-003's forecasting model, which is explicitly out of scope here.
    - Added `AgentFinding` (frozen dataclass: `subject`, `subject_kind: Literal["sku","po","period"]`, `severity: Literal["low","medium","high","critical"]`, `detail`) and a new `findings: list[AgentFinding] = field(default_factory=list)` on `AgentResponse`.
    - Purely additive: `findings` defaults to an empty list, so every existing agent (`StubAnalysisAgent`, `DemandForecastingAgent`, `StockoutRiskAgent`, `RiskDetectionAgent`) and every existing call site continues to construct and validate `AgentResponse` exactly as before, with no behavior change. `validate_response()` was left unchanged - an empty or populated `findings` list needs no new validation rule yet.
    - Added 2 tests to `agents/tests/test_contracts.py`: a pre-existing-style `AgentResponse(...)` call with no `findings` kwarg still validates and defaults to `[]`; an explicit `AgentFinding` round-trips through `AgentResponse`/`validate_response()` unchanged.
  - Verification: `python3 -m pytest -q` -> 236 passed (up from 234 before this session's two new tests; the two repo-hygiene commits above added no tests). No existing test needed modification, confirming the change is genuinely additive.
  - Notes: `StockoutRiskAgent` and `RiskDetectionAgent` do not populate `findings` yet - that's the next step, since both already compute exactly the per-SKU (`StockoutRiskAssessment`) or per-subject (`RiskContribution`) data needed, just not exposed on `AgentResponse` yet. `DemandForecastingAgent` is expected to stay at the default empty `findings` list indefinitely, per the limit above - not a gap to fix later, a documented architectural boundary.

- [x] STORY-006 step 2: `StockoutRiskAgent` exposes per-SKU `findings`
  - Date: 2026-08-26
  - Session: CC-20260826-90f4

A SKU excluded by data-quality flagging or a per-SKU `RiskModelError` does not get a finding, matching exactly which SKUs already appear (or don't) in the existing text `recommendation` - no new exclusion logic, just exposing what was already being decided. Existing text `recommendation`/`confidence` output is unchanged.

Nothing about the existing text `recommendation`, `confidence`, or contribution-computation logic changed - this only exposes data the agent already had.

  - Verification: `python3 -m pytest -q` -> 242 passed (up from 239). No existing test needed modification.
  - Notes: `RiskDetectionAgent` and `StockoutRiskAgent` now both label a stockout-risk finding for the same SKU with `subject_kind="sku"` and the same `Literal` severity scale (both ultimately call `inventory_risk.risk_model.assess_stockout_risk`) - if the two agents are given the *same* inventory data they will always agree (same deterministic function, same input), so a real conflict between them only surfaces when they're handed genuinely different data (e.g. different snapshots/timing) or different subsets - documented here as an honest limit of this design, not asserted as something the tests exercise (there is no natural way to make the same deterministic function disagree with itself on identical input). Next step is the Recommendation Agent itself.

- [x] STORY-006 step 4: `recommendation/synthesis.py` - pure synthesis and conflict-detection logic
  - Date: 2026-08-26
  - Session: CC-20260826-90f4
  - What changed: New `recommendation/` package, mirroring `risk_detection/`'s pure-module-plus-agent-wrapper shape. `synthesize_recommendations(agent_outputs: list[AgentResponse]) -> RecommendationSet` takes the `AgentResponse` objects other agents already produced (not raw external data - this module's actual input *is* other agents' validated outputs, so unlike other pure modules in this repo it does depend on `agents.contracts`, which has no dependencies of its own, so this creates no import cycle) and combines them into one `Recommendation` per successful (`status="ok"`) agent, plus two kinds of detected conflict:
    - `SubjectConflict`: two or more agents' `AgentFinding`s share the same `(subject, subject_kind)` (e.g. both talk about `SKU-1`) but disagree on severity - grouped via `_group_findings_by_subject()`, flagged whenever more than one distinct severity value is present for that subject, most-severe entry first. This is the precise conflict detection discussed with the user - a real disagreement between two agents about the same thing, not a text-parsing heuristic.
    - `ConfidenceConflict`: a coarser fallback when agents' confidence levels spread beyond `DEFAULT_CONFIDENCE_CONFLICT_THRESHOLD` (0.5, mirroring `agents/orchestrator.py`'s own `MAX_CONFIDENCE_SPREAD` tolerance, redeclared locally rather than imported since this agent may run on outputs assembled outside a single `Orchestrator.coordinate()` call) - catches disagreement between agents with no shared subject at all (e.g. `DemandForecastingAgent`, which reports no findings, per STORY-006 step 1's documented limit).
    - Raises `RecommendationError` (not caught here - the next step's agent wrapper turns it into an "error" `AgentResponse`) when `agent_outputs` is empty, or every agent's output has `status="error"` - nothing to synthesize either way. A per-agent `status="error"` output is otherwise excluded (`excluded_agents`) without failing the whole run.
    - `recommendation/tests/test_synthesis.py` (10 tests): empty input, all-failed input, happy path with 2 agreeing agents, failed-agent exclusion, a genuine `SubjectConflict` (same SKU, different severity, correct most-severe-first ordering), no conflict when only one agent reports a subject, no conflict when agents agree on a subject's severity, a `ConfidenceConflict` with no shared subject, no conflict for a small confidence spread, and confidence-conflict detection correctly ignoring an agent with `confidence=None`.
  - Verification: `python3 -m pytest -q` -> 252 passed (up from 242; 10 new tests, all in the new package). No existing test touched or modified.
  - Notes: Next step is the `RecommendationAgent` wrapper (`agents/recommendation_agent.py`) that calls this module from `run()`, formats `RecommendationSet` into the required `AgentResponse` (text recommendation naming any conflicts explicitly per acceptance criterion 2, plus logging every recommendation with supporting data per acceptance criterion 3), and handles the "Recommendation API failure" / "User interface display issues" failure paths named in the story (neither applies to this pure-Python, no-external-API, no-UI agent - to be noted explicitly in that step's entry, not silently dropped).

- [x] STORY-006 step 5: `agents/recommendation_agent.py` - the RecommendationAgent wrapper
  - Date: 2026-08-26
  - Session: CC-20260826-90f4

- [x] STORY-006: pre-push code review (6 parallel angles) - one real correctness bug found and fixed in AC2 itself, plus 3 smaller hardening fixes
  - Date: 2026-08-26
  - Session: CC-20260826-90f4
  - What changed: User asked to review the code before pushing `dd3ade2`/`907e5fa` to origin. Ran `/code-review high` across 6 parallel angles (line-by-line diff, removed-behavior, reuse/duplication, simplification, efficiency, CLAUDE.md conventions). All 6 findings sets verified directly against the code (not accepted on the reviewers' say-so) before acting - see `ReportFindings` call in this session for the full consolidated list (11 findings: 5 fixed, 1 fixed-as-a-side-effect, 4 reuse/efficiency suggestions logged as follow-ups, 1 no-change-needed).
    - **Most severe, confirmed by live reproduction**: `agents/risk_detection_agent.py` built `AgentFinding`s from `risk_score.contributions`, which deliberately drops any zero-point ("low") stockout assessment (STORY-005's own "a low stockout risk contributes nothing to the unified score" rule - correct for the *score*, wrong for *findings*). Result: if `StockoutRiskAgent` rated a SKU "critical" while `RiskDetectionAgent` independently rated the same SKU "low", `RecommendationAgent` had no matching finding from `RiskDetectionAgent` to compare against and reported **no conflict at all** - silently missing exactly the disagreement STORY-006's AC2 ("given conflicting agent outputs... highlight the conflicts") exists to catch, and the most severe kind of disagreement possible. It was actually visible in the original demo script's own comment (`run_sample_recommendation_demo.py`), which deliberately avoided a "low" snapshot for this exact reason but treated it as "no conflict possible" rather than recognizing it as a gap. Fixed by replacing `_finding_from_contribution`/`_SUBJECT_KIND_BY_SOURCE` (which converted from the already-filtered `risk_score.contributions`) with a new `_findings_from_signals()` that builds findings directly from the three raw detector outputs (`demand_anomalies`, `supplier_delays`, `stockout_assessments`) already available in `run()`, before any scoring-only filtering happens. This also incidentally removed the unguarded `_SUBJECT_KIND_BY_SOURCE[...]` dict lookup a second reviewer flagged as a latent `KeyError` risk, since that whole lookup no longer exists. Verified live: `critical` (StockoutRiskAgent) vs `low` (RiskDetectionAgent) on the same SKU now correctly produces `"CONFLICTS DETECTED"`. Added a regression test (`test_run_findings_include_a_low_risk_sku_even_though_it_contributes_nothing_to_the_score`) and updated `run_sample_recommendation_demo.py`'s `engineered_conflict` scenario to actually use the "low" snapshot the bug used to hide, instead of the "medium" workaround, since the workaround's reason no longer applies - stale comment explaining it was also removed/replaced.
    - `agents/recommendation_agent.py`: `raw_outputs` (a generator/iterator, not necessarily a `list`) was iterated twice - once by the isinstance-validation genexpr, once by `synthesize_recommendations()` - silently exhausting it on the first pass and producing a misleading "no successful agent output" error even when every upstream agent succeeded. A non-iterable truthy value also raised an uncaught `TypeError` instead of the documented "data processing errors" failure path. Fixed by materializing `raw_outputs` into a list exactly once, inside a `try/except TypeError` that converts a non-iterable input into the same clean `status="error"` response the malformed-entry case already produces.
    - `agents/contracts.py`: `validate_response()` validated `status`/`recommendation`/`confidence`/`error` but never touched `findings` at all, so a malformed `AgentFinding` (non-`AgentFinding` item, empty `subject`/`detail`, or a `subject_kind`/`severity` outside the values the `Literal` types actually allow - not enforced at runtime by Python) passed silently and would have been mis-ranked (defaulted to rank 0/"low") by `recommendation/synthesis.py`'s severity lookup instead of being rejected. This is a direct violation of CLAUDE.md's own Contract Enforcement Layer rule ("if a contract change can be made silently without a test failing, the contract is too weak"). Fixed by adding the same explicit-membership-check pattern `validate_response()` already uses for `status`, applied to each finding's `subject_kind`/`severity`, plus non-empty `subject`/`detail` checks. 4 new tests in `agents/tests/test_contracts.py`.
    - `recommendation/run_sample_recommendation_demo.py`: `_summarize()`'s `error` field only checked `response.error` and `run.crash_error`, missing the case where the stage-2 `CoordinationResult` itself fails (timeout, re-validation failure) without the Orchestrator crashing - the printed JSON would have silently shown `"error": null` even though `run.results[0].error` held the real reason. Not exercised by either current scenario (both succeed cleanly or fail cleanly before reaching this gap), but a real logic bug in the demo's own reporting. Fixed with a small `_stage2_failure_reason()` helper that checks `run.crash_error` first, then falls back to any `CoordinationResult` with `outcome="failure"`.
    - Logged, not fixed (reuse/efficiency suggestions, not correctness bugs - fixing them now would mean touching already-committed, already-portal-verified files from other stories beyond what a bug-fix pass calls for): `_SEVERITY_RANK` duplicated verbatim between `risk_detection/risk_score.py` and `recommendation/synthesis.py`; the confidence-spread threshold/logic duplicated between `agents/orchestrator.py` and `recommendation/synthesis.py` despite a docstring claiming they're "mirrored"; `_error_response()` now implemented near-identically in three agent files (hits CLAUDE.md's own "three strikes, extract it" threshold); `run_sample_recommendation_demo.py` runs its two independent scenarios (and `_conflict_scenario()`'s two independent single-agent runs) sequentially rather than concurrently. Also logged, assessed as not currently reachable and not fixed: `RecommendationAgent._confidence()` defaults to `1.0` when zero of its synthesized recommendations carry a confidence score - every agent in this repo always sets one on a `status="ok"` response today, so this is a documented edge case for a hypothetical future agent, not a live bug.
  - Verification: `python3 -m pytest -q` -> 268 passed (up from 263; 5 new tests: 4 in `test_contracts.py`, 1 in `test_risk_detection_agent.py`). Live reproduction of the fixed bug (`critical` vs `low` on the same SKU through the real `Orchestrator`/`RecommendationAgent` pipeline) confirmed both the before-state (silently no conflict) and after-state (`"CONFLICTS DETECTED"` correctly present). `python3 -m recommendation.run_sample_recommendation_demo` re-run live after all fixes: exit code 0, `engineered_conflict` scenario still succeeds with a conflict correctly detected (now via the actual "low" snapshot instead of the "medium" workaround).
  - Notes: These fixes land in a new commit on top of `dd3ade2`/`907e5fa`, both still local-only (unpushed) at review time, so no history rewrite was needed. `.colaberry/progress.json`'s STORY-006 block (set to `verified` referencing `dd3ade2`) is not re-synced by this entry - the acceptance criteria themselves didn't change, only a real, previously-undetected bug in criterion 2's own detection logic was fixed, so the existing verification stands.
