- [x] STORY-013: Supplier Reliability Evaluation - deterministic per-supplier Supplier Risk Score, flag-for-review logic, and a durable audit trail of every evaluation
  - Date: 2026-09-01
  - Session: CC-20260901-a7c2
  - What changed: Implemented REQ-007 end-to-end as a new `supplier_evaluation/` package, built and confirmed with the user one step at a time:
    1. Promoted `risk_detection/anomaly_detection.py`'s private `_parse_date` to a public `parse_delivery_date` (with a docstring explaining why - `supplier_evaluation` needs the same delivery-date parsing rule STORY-005 already hardened, and two copies could silently drift). Confirmed via grep this had zero external callers before renaming, so no compatibility alias was needed. The one file this story touches outside its own package, done with the user's explicit sign-off first.
    2. `supplier_evaluation/reliability.py` - pure computation, no I/O. `evaluate_supplier_reliability()` groups delivery rows by supplier and calls `detect_supplier_delays()` once per group, reusing STORY-005's per-row validation, date parsing, and delay-severity scoring wholesale rather than reimplementing any of it. Produces a 0-100 Supplier Risk Score (same polarity as `risk_detection/risk_score.py` - higher = worse) with severity, per-supplier metrics (on-time rate, avg/max delay), and `flagged_for_review` driven by three independent triggers: score past a review threshold, a persistently low on-time rate even when no single delay is severe, or too few/too corrupted records to score confidently (the last case forces the score to the conservative max-risk end rather than reporting a falsely-reassuring number - the "Incorrect Supplier Risk Score" failure path). Rows with no usable `supplier` value are routed to `unattributable_rows`, never silently dropped.
    3. `supplier_evaluation/audit_trail.py` + `logging_setup.py` - `SupplierEvaluationAuditStore`, a near-exact mirror of `intelligence/audit_trail.py`'s `StageAuditStore` (STORY-012): JSONL persistence, idempotent on `(evaluation_id, supplier)`, corrupted-trailing-line tolerance on reload, and a typed `SupplierEvaluationAuditWriteError` raised loudly on a failed write rather than swallowed - the "audit trail not recorded for evaluations" failure path.
    4. `supplier_evaluation/evaluator.py` - `SupplierEvaluator.run()` ties the two together: one audit record per supplier on success, and - the key hardening pass - a single run-level audit record under a reserved `__evaluation_run__` supplier key when the evaluation process itself can't produce any scores at all (a bad parameter, or a genuinely unexpected crash), mirroring `intelligence/model.py`'s pipeline-boundary crash handling so "the evaluation process fails" can never mean "and left no trace."
    5. `supplier_evaluation/run_sample_supplier_evaluation.py` - the demo, following `recommendation/run_sample_recommendation_demo.py`'s established two-scenario convention for a story this environment's missing PostgreSQL credentials can't fully exercise with real data: `real_data` (pulls `delivery_records`, gets 0 rows here, completes cleanly) and `synthetic_suppliers` (three suppliers chosen to hit every acceptance criterion in one deterministic run - see Verification).
  - Verification: `python3 -m pytest -q` -> 294 passed (up from 268; 26 new tests across `test_reliability.py`, `test_audit_trail.py`, `test_evaluator.py`, including a real write-failure case for the audit store and a mocked unexpected-crash case for the evaluator). `python3 -m supplier_evaluation.run_sample_supplier_evaluation` run live - exit code 0. All three acceptance criteria demonstrated in that one run: AC1 - "Acme" (3/3 on-time) gets Supplier Risk Score 0/100 (low), unflagged; AC2 - "SlowFreight" (3 deliveries, 15-20 days late) scores 90/100 (critical) and is flagged with two reasons (score threshold + 0% on-time rate), and "NewVendor" (1 delivery on record) scores 0/100 but is separately flagged for insufficient sample size, proving the low-data case is caught rather than reported as a clean pass; AC3(Trust) - `audit_trail.records_written: 3` matches the 3 suppliers evaluated, with every JSONL record visible in the printed log lines.
  - Notes: STORY-014 (Shipment Delay Analysis) and STORY-015 (Data Quality Monitoring) were left untouched, per this story's own instruction not to build them yet. Added `evaluation_audit_log.jsonl` to `.gitignore` (matching the existing `audit_log.jsonl`/`stage_audit_log.jsonl` entries) since the demo script generates that file as a runtime artifact. Two logged, non-escalated implementation assumptions (both documented inline in `reliability.py`): a minimum of 3 valid deliveries before a score is treated as confident, and a 70% on-time-rate floor below which a supplier is flagged regardless of individual delay severity - both tunable defaults, not governance-boundary decisions.

- [x] STORY-013: post-merge code review (7 parallel angles) - 4 real correctness bugs found and fixed, plus 3 smaller simplification fixes; 4 reuse/efficiency suggestions logged as follow-ups
  - Date: 2026-09-01
  - Session: CC-20260901-a7c2
  - What changed: User asked to review the STORY-013 code, then check for gaps against already-created stories. Ran `/code-review high` against commit `3e0b9c5` across 7 parallel angles (line-by-line diff, removed-behavior, reuse/duplication, simplification, efficiency, CLAUDE.md conventions, cross-file tracer, altitude/bandaid check). All findings verified directly against the code before acting - see `ReportFindings` call in this session for the full consolidated list (11 findings: 7 fixed, 4 reuse/efficiency suggestions logged as follow-ups).
    - **Most severe, confirmed live**: `supplier_evaluation/evaluator.py`'s `run()` left literally zero audit records for a "successful" evaluation that found no scoreable suppliers (empty delivery data, or every row unattributable) - re-opening STORY-013's own "audit trail not recorded for evaluations" failure path. Confirmed via the demo script's own `real_data` scenario, which showed `records_written: 0` before the fix. Fixed by writing one run-level audit record (`supplier=None`, `outcome="success"`) whenever `report.scores` is empty, so a completed run always leaves at least one trace. Re-ran the demo live after the fix: `real_data` now shows `records_written: 1`.
    - `RUN_LEVEL_AUDIT_SUPPLIER = "__evaluation_run__"` (the sentinel marking a run-level failure record) could collide with a real supplier literally or coincidentally named that string, since `_supplier_key()` places no restriction on supplier content - the collision would silently return a stale record via the idempotency short-circuit instead of persisting a real evaluation. Fixed by widening `SupplierEvaluationAuditRecord.supplier` (and `record()`/`has_recorded()`) to `str | None`, using `None` for every run-level record instead of a sentinel string - reusing the invariant `reliability.py`'s `_supplier_key()` already establishes (a real supplier is never `None` or empty) rather than inventing a second, string-based convention a real value could collide with.
    - `evaluator.py`'s per-supplier audit-record loop, and `_fail_run()`'s own record call, were both unguarded against `SupplierEvaluationAuditWriteError` - a broken audit store (disk full, permissions) during either path propagated as an unhandled exception instead of the documented `SupplierEvaluationRun(crash_error=...)` result, and when it happened inside `_fail_run()` specifically, it masked the original, more useful exception (e.g. a bad `delay_threshold_days`) that triggered `_fail_run()` in the first place. Fixed with a shared `_try_record()` helper that catches the write error, logs it, and returns it instead of raising - the success loop now returns a crashed run immediately rather than looping further (no retry, matching this repo's "infinite retry loops are prohibited" rule), and `_fail_run()` always returns the *original* exception's message regardless of whether its own audit write also failed.
    - Three smaller simplification fixes, all local to this story's own new code: merged two `except` blocks in `evaluator.py` with identical bodies (`SupplierEvaluationError` is already a subclass of `Exception`); derived `FLAG_SCORE_THRESHOLD` from `_SCORE_SEVERITY_THRESHOLDS` instead of a second hand-copied `35.0` literal that could silently drift from the table it's documented to mirror; removed a `max(0, ...)` clamp on `on_time_count` that implied a reachable negative case which the data flow makes impossible, replaced with a comment naming the actual invariant.
    - Logged, not fixed (reuse/efficiency suggestions, not correctness bugs - fixing them now would mean touching already-committed, already-portal-verified files from other stories, or expanding scope beyond a bug-fix pass, matching the same call the STORY-006 review made): `supplier_evaluation/audit_trail.py` and `logging_setup.py` are a third/fourth near-identical copy of the JSONL audit-store and JSON-logging patterns already in `data_integration/` and `intelligence/` - a future shared-base extraction (`common/audit_trail.py`, `common/logging_setup.py`) would collapse all of them, but that touches 3-4 already-verified modules at once. The idempotency key's naive colon-join (`f"{evaluation_id}:{supplier or ''}"`) can theoretically collide if either string itself contains a colon - an inherited pattern identical to `intelligence/audit_trail.py`'s own `run_id:stage` composition, best fixed as part of that same future shared-base extraction rather than diverging unilaterally here. Two efficiency notes at today's synthetic demo scale (immaterial, would matter if supplier/run counts grew by orders of magnitude): the audit store does one independent open/mkdir/write/close per supplier per run instead of one batched write; `evaluate_supplier_reliability()` sorts suppliers twice (alphabetically, then by score) where one combined sort would do.
  - Verification: `python3 -m pytest -q` -> 299 passed (up from 294; 6 new regression tests in `test_evaluator.py` covering each fixed bug: zero-suppliers audit trace for empty input and for all-unattributable input, a real supplier named like the old sentinel string getting its own record, a broken audit store producing a crashed run instead of an unhandled exception, and the original exception surviving a simultaneous audit-write failure in `_fail_run`). `python3 -m supplier_evaluation.run_sample_supplier_evaluation` re-run live after all fixes - exit code 0, `real_data` scenario's `audit_trail.records_written` changed from 0 to 1, `synthetic_suppliers` scenario unchanged (3 records, same scores).
  - Notes: These fixes land in a new commit on top of `bb789fa`, both still local-only (unpushed) at review time. `.colaberry/progress.json`'s STORY-013 block (already `verified`, referencing `3e0b9c5`) is not re-synced by this entry - the 3 acceptance criteria themselves didn't change, only real bugs in the audit-trail guarantee behind AC3 were fixed.

## STORY-007 — Root Cause Analysis

- [x] STORY-007: Root Cause Analysis - deterministic causal correlation, insufficient-data notification, and an audited analysis trail
  - Date: 2026-09-02
  - Session: CC-20260902-q7x2
  - What changed: Built as a new `root_cause/` package, following STORY-013's (`supplier_evaluation/`) established layering (pure computation -> audit trail -> evaluator -> Agent wrapper -> demo), one step at a time with the user confirming each step before it was built:
    - `root_cause/analysis.py`: `analyze_root_cause(issue, demand_anomalies=, supplier_delays=, supplier_scores=)` - pure, deterministic correlation (no LLM/model call, per CLAUDE.md's core principle). Takes an `Issue` (subject/subject_kind, plus optional `as_of_period`/`supplier` correlation keys) and STORY-005's `DemandAnomaly`/`SupplierDelayAnomaly` and STORY-013's `SupplierRiskScore` (already-computed, not re-detected here), and proposes ranked `RootCauseCandidate`s with a severity-derived confidence (medium=0.55, high=0.7, critical=0.85 - a documented, reviewable assumption, not calibrated against real outcome data since none exists yet). Raises `RootCauseAnalysisError` only when *no* signal data at all was supplied (AC2's insufficient-data path); when data is supplied but nothing correlates, this is a successful result with an empty candidate list and a `note` saying so - a real finding ("checked, nothing found"), deliberately distinct from "couldn't check." Guards against a signal serving as its own cause (`_is_self_reference()`) - the "incorrect causal inference" failure path named in the story brief.
    - `root_cause/audit_trail.py` + `root_cause/logging_setup.py`: `RootCauseAuditStore`, a JSONL-backed, idempotent audit trail keyed on `(analysis_id, subject_kind, subject)`, recording timestamp + confidence + candidate_count for every analysis attempt - satisfies AC3 ("logs all root cause analyses with timestamps and confidence levels"). Structurally identical to `supplier_evaluation/audit_trail.py` (STORY-013), same corrupted-line tolerance, same loud typed failure (`RootCauseAuditWriteError`) on a broken write.
    - `root_cause/evaluator.py`: `RootCauseEvaluator.run()` ties the two together, guaranteeing exactly one audit record per call. Three outcomes (not supplier_evaluation's two-way success/crashed split, because this story has its own AC for one of them): `success`, `insufficient_data` (AC2 - the evaluator's own `RootCauseAnalysisError` becomes a reported `limitation` string, not a crash), `crashed` (any other exception - the "analysis API failure" failure path, audited before being reported back rather than propagating uncaught).
    - `agents/root_cause_agent.py`: `RootCauseAnalysisAgent` - plugs into the existing `AgentQuery`/`AgentResponse`/Orchestrator contract, same shape as `RiskDetectionAgent`/`SupplierEvaluationAgent`. Unlike `analyze_root_cause()` itself, this agent accepts raw `demand_history`/`delivery_rows` (same shape those two agents already use) and derives the anomaly/delay/reliability signals itself via the exact same STORY-005/013 pure functions (`detect_demand_spikes`, `detect_supplier_delays`, `evaluate_supplier_reliability`), each independently best-effort/degradable - a caller investigating one issue doesn't need to have already run those agents first. A missing/invalid subject or subject_kind is a request error (caller bug, not an analysis outcome); `insufficient_data`/`crashed` evaluator outcomes become `status="error"` AgentResponses with the limitation/crash message; a successful analysis becomes `status="ok"` with `confidence` set straight from the analysis.
    - `root_cause/run_sample_root_cause_analysis.py`: three-scenario demo (`real_data` via the STORY-001 data_integration orchestrator; `synthetic_demand_spike` proving AC1+AC3 deterministically; `synthetic_insufficient_data` proving AC2 deterministically rather than relying on the environment's absence of credentials the way the `real_data` scenario alone would). Added `analysis_audit_log.jsonl` to `.gitignore` (same pattern as `evaluation_audit_log.jsonl`/`stage_audit_log.jsonl`/`audit_log.jsonl`).
    - 41 new tests across `root_cause/tests/` (9 analysis, 7 audit trail, 8 evaluator) and `agents/tests/test_root_cause_agent.py` (8, including a full Orchestrator round-trip), covering: insufficient data, demand-spike/supplier-delay correlation, multi-candidate ranking, the "data present but no correlation" zero-confidence case, both self-reference guards, an unflagged supplier score correctly not counting as a candidate, idempotent audit re-recording, audit-store write failures (crash reported instead of raised; original exception preserved over a secondary audit-write failure), and derivation from raw rows end-to-end.
    - Mid-session, user asked about "the dataset" - traced to STORY-005's existing `scripts/local_test_db.py` (pgserver-based local Postgres, no Docker) already being available; started it and re-ran the STORY-007 demo live against real `customer_orders`/`delivery_records` rows (12 and 3 rows respectively) to confirm the `real_data` scenario works against an actual database, not just the credential-absent fallback path. User separately asked about importing a public dataset into 5 tables (adding `purchase_orders`/`shipments`) - logged as a follow-up, not built now (out of STORY-007's scope; user chose to finish this story first when asked via AskUserQuestion).
  - Verification: `python3 -m pytest -q` -> 338 passed (up from 306 at session start; no regressions). `python3 -m root_cause.run_sample_root_cause_analysis` run twice: once with no PostgreSQL configured (clean `real_data` insufficient-data failure path) and once live against the real local Postgres from `scripts/local_test_db.py` (`real_data` scenario returns `status="ok"`, confidence 0.0, "no correlated cause found" - a real, correct read against genuine database rows, not a canned answer); both runs exit 0. `docs/stories/STORY-007.md`'s 3 boxes ticked; `.colaberry/progress.json`'s STORY-007 `criteria[].passed` set to `true` (verification block left `"in_progress"` - not yet synced to a real commit_sha, per this repo's established two-step convention of syncing verification only after the implementation commit exists).

Confirmed with the user this substitution was acceptable before building against it.

Same retry/timeout/logging shape as `postgres_connector.py`/`sheets_connector.py` (3 attempts, 2s fixed delay, 15s timeout, only transient errors - 429/5xx/connection/timeout - retried; a malformed query or unparseable response is not). Its result is not asserted in `demo_succeeded` (network-dependent, unlike the two synthetic scenarios), but running it live confirmed the whole path works: 2,248 raw port-month rows fetched, aggregated to 23 monthly points, correlated against 2026-07 (no anomaly that month - a real, correct "no correlated cause" finding, not a scripted one).
    - 8 new tests: `data_integration/tests/test_bts_border_crossing_connector.py` (7 - monthly summing across ports, skipping rows with missing/non-numeric fields, correct measure/lookback query params, retry-then-raise on repeated 5xx, no retry on a non-retryable 4xx, retry-then-raise on connection errors, no retry on malformed JSON) and one dispatch test in `data_integration/tests/test_orchestrator.py` confirming `BtsBorderCrossingDataset` routes to the new connector with the right arguments.

- [x] STORY-007: pre-commit code review (8 parallel angles) - 3 real correctness bugs found and fixed, plus commit and verification sync
  - Date: 2026-09-02
  - Session: CC-20260902-q7x2
  - What changed: User asked for a final review before committing. Ran `/code-review high` over the full STORY-007 diff (8 parallel finder angles). 3 real bugs found and fixed, all in `root_cause/analysis.py`, all verified live with a repro before and after the fix:
    - The self-reference guard (`_is_self_reference`) also excluded a supplier's own flagged `SupplierRiskScore` when investigating a supplier-kind issue about that exact supplier - "supplier" has no finer-grained identity below the supplier name the way period/PO do, so this always suppressed the one candidate a supplier-kind issue most needs. Fixed by exempting `_supplier_reliability_candidate` from that guard, since a `SupplierRiskScore`'s `explanation` decomposes into concrete delivery-level facts rather than restating the same finding.
    - `_SEVERITY_CONFIDENCE` had no `"low"` entry. `SupplierRiskScore.severity` can be `"low"` while still `flagged_for_review=True` (e.g. too few deliveries for a confident score - a real path in `supplier_evaluation/reliability.py`), so a genuinely flagged finding silently scored `0.0` confidence, indistinguishable from "no candidate found." Added `"low": 0.4`, extending the existing 0.15-step scale backward.
    - `_supplier_reliability_candidate` picked `matches[0]` instead of `max(matches, key=severity)` like the other two candidate builders - only observable with a hand-built multi-entry `supplier_scores` list (the agent's own path never produces duplicates), fixed for consistency.
    - 3 new regression tests added to `root_cause/tests/test_analysis.py`. 10 further findings (reuse/simplification/efficiency, e.g. the JSONL-audit-store pattern now duplicated 4x across `data_integration/`, `intelligence/`, `supplier_evaluation/`, `root_cause/`, crossing CLAUDE.md's own "three occurrences, lift it" threshold) were logged but not applied - fixing them would mean editing already-shipped code from other stories outside this session's scope; reported to the user for a separate decision rather than refactored unilaterally.
    - Committed `f0096b8` (21 files: `root_cause/`, `agents/root_cause_agent.py` + test, `data_integration/bts_border_crossing_connector.py` + test, `data_integration/orchestrator.py`/`test_orchestrator.py`, `requirements.txt`, `.gitignore`, `docs/stories/STORY-007.md`, `.colaberry/progress.json`, `PROGRESS.md`). - [x] STORY-008: Scenario Simulation - deterministic what-if impact assessment on top of the STORY-004 stockout-risk model, an audited simulation trail, and a pre-commit code review that fixed 3 real input-validation bugs
  - Date: 2026-09-03
  - Session: CC-20260903-q7f2
  - What changed: Built end-to-end in paced, user-confirmed steps (walking skeleton first, per this file's own Build-Break-Harden loop), reusing `inventory_risk/risk_model.py`'s already-deterministic `assess_stockout_risk()` (STORY-004) as the model to simulate against, rather than inventing a new one:
    - `scenario_simulation/simulation.py` (pure, no I/O): `ScenarioInput` (a baseline `InventoryPosition` plus four deltas - `demand_change_pct`, `lead_time_change_days`, `safety_stock_change`, `stock_change`, each defaulting to 0.0/no-change) and `simulate_scenario()`, which applies the deltas to build a projected position, runs `assess_stockout_risk()` on both baseline and projected positions, and reports the diff (`risk_level_changed`, `days_of_supply_delta`, a human-readable `detail`) as the "impact assessment" AC1 asks for. `ScenarioValidationError` (AC2's failure path) covers a blank `scenario_name`, a `demand_change_pct` below -1.0 (would make demand negative), and any delta combination `assess_stockout_risk()` itself rejects (e.g. a delta driving projected stock negative) - re-raised as a scenario-input problem, not a generic model failure. A 100% demand drop (`demand_change_pct == -1.0`) is deliberately treated as a valid, already-modeled scenario (zero demand -> infinite days of supply), not an error.
    - `scenario_simulation/audit_trail.py` (`ScenarioSimulationAuditStore`) + `scenario_simulation/logging_setup.py`: JSONL-backed, idempotent on `(simulation_id, scenario_name, sku)`, persisting `input_parameters` alongside the timestamp on every record (success or failure) - this is what satisfies AC3 ("logs all scenario simulations with timestamps and input parameters"; note this is a stronger requirement than STORY-007's own AC3, which only required confidence levels, not the actual inputs). Mirrors `root_cause/audit_trail.py`'s `RootCauseAuditStore` almost exactly, with `math.nan`/`math.inf` deltas normalized to `null` at the JSON boundary (same convention `agents/stockout_risk_agent.py` already applies to its own logging).
    - `scenario_simulation/evaluator.py` (`ScenarioEvaluator`): ties the two together with three outcomes - `success` / `invalid_input` / `crashed` - exactly one audit record per call regardless of outcome, mirroring `root_cause/evaluator.py`'s `RootCauseEvaluator` shape.
    - `agents/scenario_simulation_agent.py` (`ScenarioSimulationAgent`): wraps the evaluator as an `Agent` for the existing `Orchestrator` (STORY-002), same shape as `RootCauseAnalysisAgent`/`StockoutRiskAgent`. Reuses `inventory_risk/data_quality.py`'s `assess_inventory_data_quality()` (STORY-004) to validate the raw `baseline_row` before it becomes an `InventoryPosition` - this separates "data processing errors" (bad baseline data) from "invalid scenario inputs" (bad deltas), the two distinct failure paths this story names.
    - `scenario_simulation/run_sample_scenario_simulation.py`: a synthetic two-scenario demo (no natural real/live dataset exists for hypothetical what-ifs, unlike STORY-007's historical anomaly data) - a 300% demand-spike scenario proving AC1 (risk worsens low->high) and a stock-shock scenario with an impossible delta proving AC2 (clean rejection), both confirming AC3's audit trail afterward. Run live: exit code 0.
    - Added `simulation_audit_log.jsonl` to `.gitignore`, matching the existing `analysis_audit_log.jsonl` convention for the other services' own audit logs.
    - Pre-commit review: ran `/code-review high` targeting the new/untracked STORY-008 files plus cross-story alignment against `agents/contracts.py`, `agents/orchestrator.py`, and the `root_cause`/STORY-007 sibling this story's shape was copied from. Found and fixed 3 real bugs, all in `agents/scenario_simulation_agent.py`, all sharing one root cause (an `AgentQuery.context` value reaching the pure computation layer without type validation, crashing instead of returning the clean `status="error"` the agent's own docstring promised) and all verified live with a repro before and after the fix:
      - A non-dict `baseline_row` (e.g. an int or list - a realistic caller shape mistake) reached `assess_inventory_data_quality()` unguarded and raised an uncaught `TypeError`, which the Orchestrator would misclassify as `agent_communication_failed` instead of this story's "data processing errors" path. Fixed by requiring `isinstance(baseline_row, dict)` up front.
      - A non-string `scenario_name` (e.g. `5`) passed the old `not scenario_name or not str(scenario_name).strip()` guard (which coerced to `str` only for the check, not the value actually stored), then crashed inside `simulate_scenario()`'s own `.strip()` call. Fixed by requiring `isinstance(scenario_name, str)`.
      - An explicit `null` delta field (e.g. `{"demand_change_pct": None}` - realistic for a JSON API where an optional numeric field serializes as `null` rather than being omitted) bypassed `context.get(field, 0.0)`'s default, which only applies when the key is *absent*, and crashed inside a numeric comparison - misclassified as a "crashed" simulation failure rather than the documented "not supplied" default. Fixed with a new `_validate_deltas()` helper that normalizes `None` to `0.0` and rejects genuinely non-numeric deltas (e.g. a string) with a clean request error.
      - 4 new regression tests added to `agents/tests/test_scenario_simulation_agent.py`, one per bug plus the non-numeric-delta case.
  - Verification: `python3 -m pytest -q` -> 384 passed (up from 380 pre-fix, 349 before this story; no regressions). `python3 -m scenario_simulation.run_sample_scenario_simulation` run live: both scenarios completed as expected (`status="ok"` with `risk_level_changed=true` for the spike; `status="error"` with "invalid projected position" for the stock shock), exit code 0; both audit records confirmed present with real timestamps and the actual input parameters. All 3 pre-fix bugs reproduced live (confirmed uncaught `TypeError`/`AttributeError` and a misclassified `crashed` outcome) and confirmed fixed after (clean `status="error"` responses with the expected error text in all three cases).
  - Notes: `CLAUDE.md`'s "Per-session change report (HTML)" step (`node scripts/generateSessionChangelog.js <SessionID>`) could not be run - `scripts/generateSessionChangelog.js` does not exist anywhere in this repo's history, and no prior session entry in this file flags it as a known gap either, so this is a pre-existing infra gap this session is surfacing for the first time rather than one this story introduced. Building that generator is an unrelated piece of tooling infra, not part of REQ-014/STORY-008's own scope, so it was not built here - flagging it as an open follow-up rather than silently skipping the requirement. Not yet committed as of this entry; a follow-up entry will sync `.colaberry/progress.json`'s verification block once the commit SHA exists, matching STORY-007's own two-commit convention.

- [x] STORY-014: Shipment Delay Analysis - deterministic delay-pattern grouping and transportation cost analysis on top of the STORY-005 delay detector, plus an audited analysis trail
  - Date: 2026-09-03
  - Session: CC-20260903-m5v8
  - What changed: Built end-to-end in paced, user-confirmed steps (walking skeleton first), reusing `risk_detection/anomaly_detection.py`'s already-deterministic `detect_supplier_delays()` (STORY-005) rather than reimplementing per-row delay detection - the same reuse `supplier_evaluation/reliability.py` established for STORY-013:
    - `shipment_delay_analysis/delay_analysis.py` (pure, no I/O): `analyze_shipment_delays()` calls `detect_supplier_delays()`, then (AC1) groups the resulting delays into per-supplier `SupplierDelayPattern`s (`delay_count`, `total_delay_days`, `avg_delay_days`, `worst_severity`, `total_cost`, sorted worst-cost-first) and (AC2) costs out every delay as a `ShipmentDelayCost` (`delay_cost = cost_per_day_late * delay_days`, plus an optional per-row `transportation_cost` layered underneath as a base cost). Logged assumption, confirmed with the user via `AskUserQuestion` before writing any code (recommended option chosen): no delivery row anywhere in this codebase carries a real transportation-cost field yet, so delay cost is modeled as a flat, configurable `DEFAULT_COST_PER_DAY_LATE=$150/day` rate rather than blocking on data that doesn't exist. A `transportation_cost` present but non-numeric/negative is flagged in `cost_errors` and treated as $0 rather than raising (AC2's "cost calculation errors" failure path); `DelayCostError` is raised only for a bad *parameter* (negative `cost_per_day_late`), the same caller-vs-data-problem split `reliability.py` and `anomaly_detection.py` already draw.
    - `shipment_delay_analysis/audit_trail.py` (`ShipmentDelayAuditStore`) + `shipment_delay_analysis/logging_setup.py`: JSONL-backed, idempotent on `(analysis_id, po_id)`, mirroring `supplier_evaluation/audit_trail.py`'s `SupplierEvaluationAuditStore` almost exactly - one record per delayed PO, plus a `po_id=None` run-level record when zero delays are found, so a completed run is never left with zero audit trace. This is what satisfies AC3.
    - `shipment_delay_analysis/evaluator.py` (`ShipmentDelayEvaluator`): ties the two together, catching both the documented `DelayCostError`/`SupplierDelayError` and any unexpected crash into a reported `crash_error` (never an unhandled exception), and never letting a broken audit write mask the original analysis failure - same shape `supplier_evaluation/evaluator.py`'s `_try_record()`/`_fail_run()` pattern uses.
    - `agents/shipment_delay_analysis_agent.py` (`ShipmentDelayAnalysisAgent`): wraps the evaluator as an `Agent` for the existing `Orchestrator` (STORY-002), same shape as `SupplierEvaluationAgent`. One deliberate divergence from that sibling: zero delayed POs is reported `status="ok"` with empty findings (every delivery on time is a real, successful finding), not `status="error"` - unlike `SupplierEvaluationAgent`, where zero scores means the evaluation itself couldn't run for anyone. Integration-tested through the `Orchestrator` into `RecommendationAgent` (STORY-006).
    - `shipment_delay_analysis/run_sample_shipment_delay_analysis.py`: a two-scenario demo mirroring `supplier_evaluation/run_sample_supplier_evaluation.py`'s shape - a `real_data` pull via `data_integration`'s orchestrator (degrades cleanly to "no delivery data provided" with no Postgres configured, not asserted in `demo_succeeded`) and a `synthetic_delays` scenario (two on-time Acme deliveries plus three SlowFreight delays of increasing severity, one with a real `transportation_cost`, one with a deliberately bad one) exercising all three ACs in one pass. Run live: exit code 0, 3 delays grouped into one `SlowFreight` pattern (25 total delay days, critical worst severity), $4,350 total cost, 3 audit records confirmed written via `records_for_analysis`.
    - Added `delay_analysis_audit_log.jsonl` to `.gitignore`, matching the existing per-service audit-log convention.
    - Ticked all 3 boxes in `docs/stories/STORY-014.md` and synced `.colaberry/progress.json`'s STORY-014 criteria/`files_touched`/`tests_added`/`notes` (verification block deliberately left `in_progress` - synced in a follow-up commit once the real commit SHA exists, matching every prior story's own two-commit convention); bumped the `totals` rollup's `criteria_passed` 35->38.
  - Verification: `python3 -m pytest -q` -> 425 passed (up from 386; no regressions), including 39 new tests across `shipment_delay_analysis/tests/` and `agents/tests/test_shipment_delay_analysis_agent.py` covering the happy path, both new failure paths (bad `cost_per_day_late` parameter, unusable `transportation_cost`), the delegated `detect_supplier_delays` failure path, and boundary cases (no rows, no delays, no usable supplier). `python3 -m shipment_delay_analysis.run_sample_shipment_delay_analysis` run live: exit code 0, patterns/costs/audit trail all confirmed as described above.
  - Notes: Not yet committed as of this entry; a follow-up entry will sync `.colaberry/progress.json`'s verification block once the commit SHA exists, matching STORY-007/STORY-008's own two-commit convention.

- [x] STORY-014: pre-commit code review (alignment with sibling stories) - 1 real correctness bug fixed, plus a demo-wiring gap closed to match the STORY-013 precedent
  - Date: 2026-09-03
  - Session: CC-20260903-m5v8
  - What changed: User asked for a review of the STORY-014 code for alignment with the other stories and to fix any bugs found. Ran `/code-review high --fix` against the working-tree diff. Findings, both addressed:
    - **Real bug, fixed**: `shipment_delay_analysis/delay_analysis.py`'s `ShipmentDelayAnalysisReport.total_transportation_cost` was computed as `sum(c.total_cost for c in delay_costs)` - the same expression as `total_delay_cost`'s numerator plus the base cost, not `sum(c.base_transportation_cost ...)` - so it silently double-counted every delay penalty into a field meant to report actual freight spend only. A row with `$0 transportation_cost` and a `$500` delay penalty produced `total_transportation_cost == 500.0`, locked in (wrongly) by the original test. Fixed to sum `base_transportation_cost` only, with both total fields' semantics documented inline (`total_delay_cost` = pure delay penalty, `total_transportation_cost` = pure base freight cost, never overlapping). Updated the test that had locked in the wrong value and added a `total_transportation_cost == 750.0` assertion to the layered-cost test.
    - **Alignment gap, fixed**: `recommendation/run_sample_recommendation_demo.py`'s own docstring documents that `SupplierEvaluationAgent` was added to its shared stage-1 agent list "after STORY-013 shipped, once its own story was complete" - `ShipmentDelayAnalysisAgent` had not received the same treatment, leaving STORY-014's real integration path (`Orchestrator` -> `RecommendationAgent`, alongside every sibling agent) undemonstrated outside its own standalone script and unit tests. Added `ShipmentDelayAnalysisAgent` to that same stage-1 list (it already receives the same `delivery_rows` context `SupplierEvaluationAgent` uses - no new dataset/query needed) and updated the module docstring to name it alongside its precedent.
  - Verification: `python3 -m pytest -q` -> 425 passed, no regressions (fix didn't add/remove tests, just corrected one). `python3 -m shipment_delay_analysis.run_sample_shipment_delay_analysis` re-run live after the fix: exit code 0. `python3 -m recommendation.run_sample_recommendation_demo` run live after the wiring fix: `shipment_delay_analysis_agent` now appears as the 5th stage-1 agent in both scenarios' `stage1_agents` output, exit code 0 (the script's own pass/fail condition only depends on the `engineered_conflict` scenario, so this addition couldn't have silently broken it - confirmed unaffected by direct comparison of that scenario's output before and after).

- [x] STORY-015: Data Quality Monitoring - a domain-agnostic 0-100 Data Quality Score, poor-quality alerting for the data steward, and an audited trail of every quality check
  - Date: 2026-09-04
  - Session: CC-20260903-d9k4
  - What changed: Implemented REQ-017 end-to-end as a new `data_quality_monitoring/` package, built and confirmed with the user one step at a time (walking skeleton first):
    1. `data_quality_monitoring/logging_setup.py` - same JSON logger shape every other service in this repo uses, restamped `service: "data_quality_monitoring"`.
    2. `data_quality_monitoring/quality_checks.py` - pure computation, no I/O. `assess_data_quality()` is deliberately domain-agnostic (parameterized by `required_fields`) rather than a third hand-rolled, single-dataset validator alongside `inventory_risk/data_quality.py` (STORY-004, hardcoded to the inventory row shape) and `risk_detection/anomaly_detection.py`'s own delivery-row checks (hardcoded to `REQUIRED_DELIVERY_FIELDS`) - both already own their domain's business rules (non-negative stock, positive lead time, parseable dates) and are not re-validated here. Scores a completeness dimension - reusing the same missing-vs-falsy field distinction those two modules already established (a legitimately falsy value like integer `0` is never mistaken for a missing field) - into a 0-100 Data Quality Score, higher-is-better (opposite polarity from this repo's risk scores, since "quality" reads naturally that direction). AC1. A score below the "fair" severity-bucket floor (70/100), or zero rows to assess at all, raises `poor_quality` with human-readable `alert_reasons` rather than fabricating a number - the "Incorrect Data Quality Score" failure path; "no data" is a different condition from "bad data" and must not silently read as either a pass or a fail.
    3. `data_quality_monitoring/audit_trail.py` - `QualityAuditStore`: JSONL-backed, idempotent `record()` keyed on `(check_id, dimension)`, same corrupted-line tolerance as `supplier_evaluation`/`shipment_delay_analysis`'s own audit stores. A broken write raises a typed `QualityAuditWriteError` rather than swallowing silently - closes the "audit trail not recorded for quality checks" failure path.
    4. `data_quality_monitoring/evaluator.py` - `DataQualityEvaluator` ties the two together: audits every dimension the assessment scores (never leaves a run with zero trace), catches unexpected exceptions at the one orchestration boundary so "Quality monitoring fails" is a typed, audited `crashed` outcome rather than a raw exception, and preserves the original failure's exception over a secondary audit-write failure. Alerting the data steward (AC2) is a logged assumption, not escalated - no notification service (email, Slack, etc.) is wired up for this project yet, the same situation every other `*Evaluator` in this repo is in: the alert is surfaced as a distinct, loudly logged `data_quality_alert_raised` event (`outcome: "failure"`, correlation_id=check_id) plus a run-level audit record (`dimension=None`) persisting the alert reasons, so the alert itself leaves a durable trace tied to the check that raised it (AC3/Trust), for a future notification service to consume.
    5. `data_quality_monitoring/run_sample_data_quality_monitoring.py` - three scenarios mirroring the sibling demos' convention: `real_data` (pulls `delivery_records` via the STORY-001 `data_integration` orchestrator; no PostgreSQL credentials configured in this environment, so it exercises the "zero rows -> alert, not a crash" path), `synthetic_good_quality` (every row complete -> 100/100, no alert), `synthetic_poor_quality` (3 of 4 rows missing a required field -> 25/100, alert raised and audited).
  - Verification: `python3 -m pytest -q` -> 449 passed (up from 425; no regressions), including 24 new tests across `data_quality_monitoring/tests/test_quality_checks.py`, `test_audit_trail.py`, and `test_evaluator.py` covering the happy path, both new failure paths (bad `required_fields` parameter, an unexpected crash mid-assessment), the missing-vs-falsy field distinction, the poor-quality alert threshold, idempotent re-recording, run-level records surviving reload/corrupted lines, and the audit-store-write-failure path. `python3 -m data_quality_monitoring.run_sample_data_quality_monitoring` run live: exit code 0, all three scenarios' scores/alerts/audit-record counts matched the demo's own asserted expectations (printed above). Pre-commit review pass (careful re-read of every new module plus `python3 -m py_compile` across the package): no correctness bugs found this pass - unlike STORY-013/014, which each found one real bug in code of comparable shape.
  - Notes: Ticked all 3 boxes in `docs/stories/STORY-015.md` and synced `.colaberry/progress.json`'s STORY-015 criteria/`files_touched`/`tests_added`/`notes` (verification block deliberately left `in_progress` - synced in a follow-up commit once the real commit SHA exists, matching every prior story's own two-commit convention); bumped the `totals` rollup's `criteria_passed` 38->41. Added `quality_audit_log.jsonl` to `.gitignore` (matching the existing `audit_log.jsonl`/`delay_analysis_audit_log.jsonl` entries) since the demo script generates that file as a runtime artifact. This is the last story in the current plan - no further story work is queued after this one commits.

Before building, reviewed all 15 stories against `.colaberry/progress.json` (the authoritative source, not the story `.md` files' own checkboxes, which are stale for several already-verified stories) to inventory what every prior story already produces, then confirmed scope with the user via AskUserQuestion on three judgment calls: STORY-007/STORY-008 (Root Cause Analysis, Scenario Simulation) stay off the dashboard as live tiles - both need a specific question/scenario to run, not a plain refresh, and will likely be invoked through STORY-010's future chat interface instead; STORY-012's four-stage Intelligence Model stays out of scope - it runs as a separate pipeline nothing else in this repo treats as the primary path; STORY-006's synthesized recommendation *is* included as its own tile, chaining both `Orchestrator.coordinate()` stages the same way `recommendation/run_sample_recommendation_demo.py` already does.
    1. `dashboard/logging_setup.py` - same JSON logger shape every other service in this repo uses, restamped `service: "dashboard"`.
    2. `dashboard/metrics.py` - pure computation, no I/O. `DashboardMetric`/`DashboardSnapshot` dataclasses and `build_dashboard()`, which turns a stage-1 (and, once chained, stage-2 recommendation) `Orchestrator` run's `CoordinationResult`s into one tile per agent. Deliberately depends only on the shared `AgentResponse`/`AgentFinding`/`CoordinationResult` contracts (`agents/contracts.py`, `agents/orchestrator.py`), never on any one agent's identity or internal computation type - the same boundary `RecommendationAgent` (STORY-006) already holds to, which is what lets every current agent (and any future one) render correctly with no change to this module. AC1: `build_dashboard()` never raises - an agent that failed (either an orchestration-level failure, e.g. a timeout, or an agent-level clean failure, e.g. "no supply chain data provided") becomes an "error" tile plus a `data_processing_errors` entry rather than an exception, so one broken agent can never hide every other agent's metric. AC2's notification: `overall_status` is `"ok"`/`"degraded"`/`"error"` depending on how many tiles failed, and a human-readable `notification` string names every failed data source once `data_processing_errors` is non-empty.
    3. `dashboard/audit_trail.py` - `DashboardAuditStore`: JSONL-backed, idempotent `record()` keyed on `(dashboard_id, metric_id)`, same corrupted-line tolerance and loud-typed-failure-on-write-error convention as every sibling `*AuditStore` in this repo (`data_quality_monitoring/audit_trail.py` most directly). This is what satisfies AC3 ("logs all dashboard updates with timestamps and data sources") - each record carries its `source_agent`.
    4. `dashboard/evaluator.py` - `DashboardEvaluator` ties `build_dashboard()` to the audit store, same "Evaluator" shape as `data_quality_monitoring/evaluator.py`'s `DataQualityEvaluator`: records every tile regardless of its status, bails out with a `crashed` `DashboardBuildRun` (not a silently-incomplete trail) if the audit store itself is broken, and writes a run-level record (`metric_id="_build"`) if `build_dashboard()` itself crashes unexpectedly (distinct from a single agent's failure, which never raises).
    5. `dashboard/data_freshness.py` - `DataFreshnessEntry` + pure `build_data_freshness()`, bridging `data_integration.orchestrator.run_integration_with_audit()`'s own return value (`list[DatasetResult]`) into a per-dataset freshness reading. Added as a separate module from `metrics.py` specifically to preserve that module's own "never import a specific data source's internals" boundary. Deliberately does *not* read `data_integration/audit_trail.py`'s `AuditStore` directly - that store has no method to list its past records back out (only `has_processed()`), and adding one would mean modifying a file outside this story without asking first (per this story's own brief); building freshness from the pull's own return value achieves the same AC3 "data sources" goal with zero changes to `data_integration/`.
    6. `dashboard/render.py` - `render_dashboard_html()`: a self-contained HTML "control tower" page (tiles, severity badges, a notification banner, a Data Sources section). Every agent-derived string (a tile's headline, an error message, the notification text) is HTML-escaped before interpolation - regression-tested against an XSS payload. Defensive at two levels, covering the "Dashboard rendering failure" / "Data visualization errors" / "User interface display issues" failure paths this story names explicitly: a single tile (or data-source row) that fails to render is replaced with a logged fallback card instead of taking down the page; if page assembly itself fails unexpectedly, the whole function still returns a minimal, valid fallback error page rather than raising.
    7. `dashboard/run_sample_dashboard.py` - demo entry point, wiring the full stage-1 fleet (STORY-003's `DemandForecastingAgent`, STORY-004's `StockoutRiskAgent`, STORY-005's `RiskDetectionAgent`, STORY-013's `SupplierEvaluationAgent`, STORY-014's `ShipmentDelayAnalysisAgent`, STORY-015's `DataQualityMonitoringAgent`) plus a chained stage-2 `RecommendationAgent`, exactly as `recommendation/run_sample_recommendation_demo.py` already does, then through `DashboardEvaluator` and `render_dashboard_html()`. Two scenarios, each rendering its own HTML file: `real_data` (this environment has no PostgreSQL configured, so every stage-1 pull fails; 5 of 6 stage-1 agents error, `DataQualityMonitoringAgent` still succeeds per its own documented "zero rows is itself the alert" behavior, `RecommendationAgent` still produces a recommendation off that one output - the dashboard comes out `overall_status="degraded"` with a notification naming the 5 failed sources, demonstrating AC2 end-to-end) and `synthetic_healthy` (hand-built 8-month demand history, 4 on-time deliveries for one supplier, 2 well-stocked SKUs - all 7 tiles succeed, `overall_status="ok"`, no notification, 0 critical/high findings, demonstrating AC1 end-to-end).
    8. Pre-commit review (`/code-review high`, 3 parallel passes) found no correctness bugs in the new code, only reuse/simplification findings, all fixed: `dashboard/metrics.py`'s two near-identical error branches collapsed into one; three separate passes over `response.findings` (severity/critical-count/high-count) replaced with a single-pass `_severity_summary()`; `dashboard/evaluator.py`'s two near-identical record-loops (agent tiles, data-source freshness) replaced with one shared `_record_all()` helper; `dashboard/render.py`'s three hand-built `logger.error(...)` blocks replaced with a shared `_log_render_failure()` helper, and its duplicated status-color ternary replaced with a shared `_status_color()` helper; `dashboard/run_sample_dashboard.py`'s five repeated `snapshot.X if snapshot else <default>` ternaries in `_summarize()` replaced with one `snapshot is None` branch. Re-ran the full test suite and the live demo after each fix to confirm behavior was unchanged. The same review pass also surfaced two items genuinely outside this story's scope, both pre-existing uncommitted changes from before this session started (visible in the session-start `git status`, neither touched by this diff): `.mcp.json` auto-installs `mssql-mcp-server` via `npx` in what `docs/CONNECT-YOUR-REPO.md` (also pre-existing, not written this session) describes as a public repo, and `command-center/js/tabs/pm.js` carries a stale `?? s.verification?.points` fallback for a field format `.colaberry/progress.json` migrated away from several stories ago. Flagged both to the user rather than fixed, per this story's own "stop and ask before changing a file outside this story" rule.

  - Date: 2026-09-04

  - What changed: User pointed out real Postgres data was available and asked whether that could raise confidence in this story. Found a real bug: `main()`'s exit-code gate hardcoded `real_data["overall_status"] == "degraded"` and `notification is not None`, silently assuming this environment would never have PostgreSQL credentials configured. Against real data every stage-1 pull succeeded and the dashboard correctly came out `"ok"` with 5 real critical findings - the dashboard worked exactly right, but the demo script's own success gate then reported exit code 1, a false failure. This is the exact class of bug the "BUILD-BREAK-HARDEN" loop exists to catch (an assumption baked into a happy-path gate that a config change falsifies), just caught by the user handing me real data rather than by my own BREAK phase during the original build.
    - Fixed by decoupling "prove AC2" from "hope this environment stays credential-less": added a third, fully synthetic `partial_failure` scenario (inventory data only, no demand history or delivery records) that deterministically leaves 3 of 6 stage-1 agents without the data they require regardless of whether Postgres is configured, so the dashboard reliably comes out `"degraded"` with a notification either way. `real_data`'s own gate was loosened to "the pipeline ran end-to-end and produced tiles" - never a specific `overall_status` - since asserting a specific status there was asserting a fact about the environment this script does not control. Updated the module docstring to describe all three scenarios and to name the original design mistake directly rather than silently smoothing it over.

Did not stop the local test Postgres server afterward - `scripts/local_test_db.py`'s own docstring documents it as a persistent dev convenience meant to survive across shell sessions, same as prior stories' own use of it.

- [x] STORY-010: AI Chat Interface Implementation - a deterministic natural-language query router answering straight from the STORY-009 dashboard snapshot, with its own audited trust spine
  - Date: 2026-09-07
  - Session: CC-20260907-p4j9
  - What changed: Implemented REQ-016 end-to-end as a new `chat_interface/` package, built and confirmed with the user one step at a time (walking skeleton first, per this story's own "paced co-pilot" instruction). Before building, reviewed STORY-009's own closing note in this file, which explicitly named the intended integration point: "STORY-010 can call into `dashboard/metrics.py`/`dashboard/evaluator.py` directly once it exists, since both are pure/typed with no CLI or HTML dependency baked in." Followed that: chat answers a query from an already-computed `dashboard.metrics.DashboardSnapshot` (the same stage-1 6-agent fleet + stage-2 `RecommendationAgent` every other demo in this repo already coordinates) rather than re-running any agent or standing up a second orchestration path - per CLAUDE.md's core determinism principle ("LLMs are probabilistic, production systems must be deterministic"), there is no LLM call anywhere in this story; "chat" is a deterministic keyword router over data the agent fleet already produced.
    1. `chat_interface/logging_setup.py` - same JSON logger shape every other service in this repo uses, restamped `service: "chat_interface"`.
    2. `chat_interface/router.py` - pure computation, no I/O. `classify_query(query_text)` matches free text against a fixed keyword table, one topic per existing agent's `metric_id` (`demand_forecasting_agent`, `stockout_risk_agent`, `supplier_evaluation_agent`, `shipment_delay_analysis_agent`, `data_quality_monitoring_agent`, `recommendation_agent`, `risk_detection_agent`). A blank or fully unrecognized query returns `None` (AC2's failure path).
    3. `chat_interface/chat.py` - `answer_query(query_text, snapshot)` ties the router to a `DashboardSnapshot`: `"answered"` when the matched topic has a healthy tile (its headline + confidence become the chat answer), `"data_unavailable"` when the topic is recognized but that tile errored or wasn't part of this snapshot's fleet (the "Data processing errors" failure path this story names explicitly - answered honestly, never a crash or a fabricated number), `"unsupported"` when no topic matched at all (AC2 - the message names every topic actually supported, not just "I don't understand").
    4. `chat_interface/audit_trail.py` - `ChatAuditStore`: JSONL-backed, idempotent `record()` keyed on `interaction_id`, same corrupted-line tolerance and loud-typed-failure-on-write-error convention as every sibling `*AuditStore` in this repo (`data_quality_monitoring/audit_trail.py` most directly). Persists query text, timestamp, status, topic, and answer for every interaction - this is what satisfies the Trust AC.
    5. `chat_interface/evaluator.py` - `ChatEvaluator` ties `answer_query()` to the audit store, same "Evaluator" shape as `data_quality_monitoring/evaluator.py`'s `DataQualityEvaluator`: every interaction (answered, unsupported, or data-unavailable) gets exactly one audit record; an unexpected crash (a malformed snapshot, or any bug) is caught at this one orchestration boundary, logged, given its own failure-outcome audit record, and returned as a typed `crashed` `ChatInteractionRun` instead of propagating - this is what turns "Chat interface failure"/"Chat API failure" from a raw exception into a tested, audited outcome.
    6. `chat_interface/run_sample_chat_interface.py` - demo entry point. Builds two `DashboardSnapshot`s via the same stage-1/stage-2 Orchestrator wiring `dashboard/run_sample_dashboard.py` and `recommendation/run_sample_recommendation_demo.py` each already duplicate inline (matching that established per-demo-owns-its-wiring convention rather than importing another module's private helpers): a "healthy" one off `dashboard/run_sample_dashboard.py`'s own hand-tuned `SYNTHETIC_DEMAND_HISTORY`/`SYNTHETIC_DELIVERY_ROWS`/`SYNTHETIC_INVENTORY_ROWS` constants (reused directly rather than re-derived, since those are already calibrated against `forecasting/data_quality.py`'s and `inventory_risk/risk_model.py`'s own thresholds), and a "partial" one (inventory only) matching that same demo's own "partial_failure" scenario shape. Four queries run across both snapshots, covering AC1's happy path and both documented failure paths.
    7. Pre-commit `/code-review high` found and fixed 1 real bug: `classify_query()` originally picked whichever topic was listed first in `_TOPIC_KEYWORDS` whenever a query's text happened to match more than one topic's keywords - e.g. "is our supplier causing shipment delays?" resolved to `supplier_evaluation_agent` purely because that topic's entry came earlier in the table, not because it was the more relevant answer (`shipment_delay_analysis_agent`, the topic the query is actually about, sat later). Fixed by matching on the *longest* keyword found across all topics instead of table order, with a regression test (`test_classify_query_prefers_longest_match_when_two_specific_topics_collide`) locking in the corrected behavior. All previously-passing router tests kept their same expected outcomes under the new matching rule.
    8. Added `chat_audit_log.jsonl` to `.gitignore`, matching the existing `quality_audit_log.jsonl`/`dashboard_audit_log.jsonl` entries, since the demo script generates that file as a runtime artifact.
  - Verification: `python3 -m pytest -q` -> 523 passed (up from 484; no regressions), including 39 new tests across `chat_interface/tests/test_router.py`, `test_chat.py`, `test_audit_trail.py`, and `test_evaluator.py` covering the happy path, both documented failure paths (an errored/missing tile, an unrecognized query, a blank query), the longest-match routing fix and its regression case, idempotent audit recording, reload-from-disk/corrupted-line tolerance, the audit-store-write-failure path, and an unexpected-crash failure path with the original exception preserved even when the subsequent audit write also fails. `python3 -m chat_interface.run_sample_chat_interface` run live (both before and after the router fix, to confirm behavior on the intended cases was unchanged): exit code 0 both times; all 4 sample queries resolved exactly as designed - "what's our stockout risk?" answered from a real computed tile (30 days of supply, low risk, confidence 1.00), "what's the weather today?" returned the unsupported message naming all 7 real supported topics, "what's the demand forecast?" and "how reliable are our suppliers?" against the partial snapshot both returned `data_unavailable` surfacing the real underlying agent error ("no historical demand data provided", "no delivery data provided for supplier evaluation") rather than crashing; all 4 interactions confirmed present in the audit store via `has_recorded()`. Ticked all 3 boxes in `docs/stories/STORY-010.md` and synced `.colaberry/progress.json`'s STORY-010 criteria/`files_touched`/`tests_added`/`notes` (verification block deliberately left `in_progress` - synced in a follow-up commit once the real commit SHA exists, matching every prior story's own two-commit convention); bumped the `totals` rollup's `criteria_passed` 44->47.
  - Notes: No agent wrapper (`agents/chat_agent.py`) was added, deliberately - chat is a *consumer* of an already-built `DashboardSnapshot`, not something the STORY-002 Orchestrator itself coordinates, so there is nothing for it to plug into. This is the only `in_progress` story remaining in `.colaberry/progress.json` at the start of this session; with this commit, all 16 stories in the current plan have their acceptance criteria passing.

    1. **Real Postgres run**: added a third, informational-only "real" scenario to `chat_interface/run_sample_chat_interface.py` (`build_real_data_snapshot()`, pulling the same `DATASETS` `dashboard/run_sample_dashboard.py` already uses via `data_integration.orchestrator.run_integration_with_audit`). Started `scripts/local_test_db.py`'s seeded local Postgres and ran the demo live: real, specific, correct answers came back off genuinely live data - SKU-GIZMO's critical 0.5-day-of-supply shortage, the 2025-07 demand spike (z-score 213.58) via a "what anomalies" query, and PO-1003's actual 15-day/$2,250 Beta Logistics delay via a "which shipments are delayed" query. Exit code 0 both with and without credentials configured (informational scenario never gates the exit code, matching `dashboard/run_sample_dashboard.py`'s own "real_data" scenario precedent).
    2. **Browser chat UI**: added `chat_interface/serve_chat_ui.py` - a dependency-free `http.server`-based local server (no new package, per CLAUDE.md's 12-factor "explicit dependencies" principle) serving one self-contained HTML page (a text box + transcript, styled consistently with `dashboard/render.py`'s existing executive look) plus a `POST /api/chat` endpoint that calls `ChatEvaluator.run()` directly - the same trust-spine every batch-driven interaction already gets. Verified live via curl: the page loads, a real query gets a real answer, an unsupported query gets the limitations message, a malformed request gets a clean 400, and both valid interactions showed up in the audit log.
    3. **Pre-commit `/code-review high` (8 parallel finder angles + verification)** on the full diff found 4 real, confirmed bugs (independently corroborated by more than one finder in three of the four cases) and fixed all of them:
       - `chat_interface/router.py`: `classify_query()`'s "longest match wins" fix (from STORY-010's original pre-commit review) still silently fell back to table order whenever two *different* topics tied at the same keyword length (e.g. "our supplier and shipment carrier update" - "supplier" and "shipment" both 8 characters - resolved to `supplier_evaluation_agent` purely by table position). Fixed by detecting a genuine cross-topic tie at the best length and returning `None` (the unsupported-query path) instead of an arbitrary guess; a tie between two keywords of the *same* topic still resolves normally. Two new regression tests lock in both behaviors.
       - `chat_interface/run_sample_chat_interface.py`: `all_audited` had been widened to fold the three informational "real data" interactions into the pass/fail exit-code gate, contradicting the module's own docstring ("printed but not gated") - a transient audit-write hiccup on a `chat-demo-real-*` interaction could flip the demo's exit code even though all four documented, AC-gating interactions answered exactly as specified. Fixed by gating only on the four `_GATED_CASES` interactions; also replaced the four hand-indexed `interactions[i]["status"] == ...` assertions with one data-driven `zip()` over `_GATED_CASES`, so a future fifth query is automatically included in the gate instead of needing a second, easy-to-forget manual edit (the same class of omission that caused the `all_audited` bug in the first place).
       - `chat_interface/serve_chat_ui.py`: `do_POST` parsed `Content-Length` with a bare `int()` outside any try/except, so a non-numeric header value raised an uncaught `ValueError` with no HTTP response at all (verified live with a crafted `http.client` request before the fix). Separately, a negative `Content-Length` (e.g. `-1`) made `self.rfile.read(-1)` block forever waiting for EOF on a connection the client doesn't close, wedging the entire single-threaded server for every other client - verified live: with one such stuck connection open, a concurrent request to `/` timed out completely. Fixed with a new `_read_request_body()` that validates the header is a parseable, non-negative integer no larger than a new `MAX_REQUEST_BODY_BYTES` (64 KiB) cap, returning a clean 400 for any violation. Three new regression tests cover all three cases, including one that issues a follow-up request after the negative-length attempt to prove the server is still responsive.
       - `chat_interface/serve_chat_ui.py`: `_choose_snapshot()` gated real-vs-synthetic data purely on `SUPPLYMIND_PG_HOST`'s presence, but `data_integration/config.py`'s `load_postgres_config()` actually requires four variables (`_HOST`/`_DATABASE`/`_USER`/`_PASSWORD`) - with only `SUPPLYMIND_PG_HOST` set, every dataset pull would fail while the UI's own banner still claimed "real Postgres data," a misleading label for what was actually an all-error snapshot. Fixed by gating on `load_postgres_config()` itself succeeding (catching `MissingConfigError`) - the same canonical check `data_integration/postgres_connector.py` already uses - rather than one hardcoded variable name. Verified directly: with only `SUPPLYMIND_PG_HOST` set, `_choose_snapshot()` now correctly falls back to the synthetic label instead of mislabeling.
    4. Also applied two safe, in-scope simplifications surfaced by the same review, both contained to files authored this session: `ChatUIHandler`'s `evaluator`/`snapshot`/`data_source` moved from class attributes (shared, global, mutable state that would silently clobber a second concurrent server instance) to instance attributes on a new `ChatUIServer(HTTPServer)` subclass, the standard idiom for passing per-server state into `BaseHTTPRequestHandler`; and `_PAGE_TEMPLATE` switched from `str.format()` (forcing every literal CSS/JS brace to be escaped as `{{`/`}}`) to `string.Template`'s `$`-placeholders, which need no such escaping. `ChatAuditRecord.to_json()` simplified to `dataclasses.asdict(self)` instead of a hand-listed field mirror that could drift from the dataclass definition.
    5. **Explicitly out of scope, not fixed**: several other review findings proposed extracting shared base classes/helpers across 7-8 *pre-existing* modules repo-wide (`logging_setup.py`'s `JsonFormatter`, the `*AuditStore`/`*Evaluator` orchestration scaffold, a shared HTML page-shell between `dashboard/render.py` and `serve_chat_ui.py`) - real, valid DRY observations, but refactoring them would mean touching multiple files well outside this story per its own "stop and ask before changing a file outside this story" rule, for a pattern that predates `chat_interface/` by several stories. Flagged here rather than silently fixed or silently dropped, same treatment STORY-009 gave its own out-of-scope findings (`.mcp.json`, `pm.js`).
  - Verification: `python3 -m pytest -q` -> 536 passed (up from 523; no regressions), including 13 new tests (`chat_interface/tests/test_serve_chat_ui.py`, 11 tests covering the HTTP server end-to-end including all 3 Content-Length regression cases; 2 new `test_router.py` cases for the cross-topic-tie fix). `python3 -m chat_interface.run_sample_chat_interface` run live three times (no credentials, with `scripts/local_test_db.py`'s real seeded Postgres, and after all fixes) - exit code 0 every time, real-data answers matched the seeded data exactly. `python3 -m chat_interface.serve_chat_ui` run live via curl and raw `http.client` requests: normal GET/POST both correct, the non-numeric-Content-Length crash is now a clean 400, the negative-Content-Length hang is now an immediate 400 with the server proven still responsive via an immediate follow-up request, and the partial-Postgres-config mislabeling is fixed (falls back to the honest synthetic label). `python3 -c "..."` direct check confirmed `_choose_snapshot()`'s corrected behavior.
  - Notes: STORY-010's own 3 acceptance criteria were already `verified` before this session's work (commits `5d34407`/`56eee74`) and remain unchanged - this entry documents confidence-raising hardening on top of an already-complete story, the same category STORY-009's own multiple post-verification follow-up entries fall into (real-Postgres bug fix, agent-integration gap, repo hygiene). No `docs/stories/STORY-010.md` or `.colaberry/progress.json` change applies here since no acceptance criterion changed.
