- [x] New capability (not story-scoped): data-console Slice 1 - connect to Postgres and browse tables/columns against this repo's known required-column checklists
  - Date: 2026-09-08
  - Session: CC-20260908-t3x9
  - What changed: User asked for a much larger "fully functional app" - a data-connection UI (table/column picker, joins, a raw-query fallback, CSV/zip upload, record limits for large tables) feeding into a chat-driven historical/forecast Q&A experience with charts. Before writing any code, walked through the request point-by-point and flagged the two decisions that are genuinely governance-level per this repo's own rules rather than implementation details: (1) whether free-form chat questions should be understood by a rule-based router (keeps this repo's stated zero-LLM, fully-deterministic design) or by an actual language model (a real AI-model-class change CLAUDE.md's Autonomy Model explicitly requires sign-off for, not something to decide unilaterally); (2) whether a raw-SQL text box should be allowed as a fallback when the guided picker can't express a query (a real, if small for a single-operator local tool, security surface). Confirmed both via AskUserQuestion, re-explaining the SQL-box question in plain terms (with a before/after mockup) after the user said they didn't understand the first framing - user chose rule-based/no-LLM for the chat engine, and yes-with-guardrails for the SQL box. Both decisions gate later slices, not this one; this entry covers only the user-agreed first slice ("connect and browse, no editing or querying yet").
    1. New `data_console/` package, structured like every other trust-spine module in this repo:
       - `column_requirements.py` - the single source of truth for what each of the 3 known datasets (`customer_orders`, `inventory`, `delivery_records`) actually needs, with a human-readable description and example value per column. Every required/optional column and its constraints were read directly out of the code that consumes it (`forecasting/aggregation.py`, `inventory_risk/data_quality.py`, `supplier_evaluation/reliability.py`, `shipment_delay_analysis/delay_analysis.py`) via a dedicated research pass earlier this session, not guessed from demo data - this becomes the one place a future column rename in any of those modules needs to be reflected, instead of being re-derived by every caller.
       - `requirements_check.py` - pure logic (no I/O, fully unit-testable without a database): compares a list of real column names against one or all known datasets' requirements, case-insensitive and whitespace-tolerant (Postgres already folds unquoted identifiers to lowercase, and a future CSV header's exact case shouldn't have to be exact either).
       - `schema_inspector.py` - reads real tables/columns out of a connected Postgres via `information_schema.tables`/`information_schema.columns`, reusing `data_integration.postgres_connector.fetch_rows()` rather than opening a second connection path, so the existing retry/timeout/`PostgresIntegrationError` behavior applies unchanged. The table name in the columns query is a bound parameter, never interpolated into the SQL text, so an unrecognized table name just comes back as zero rows rather than a query-injection surface.
       - `serve_data_console.py` - a dependency-free `http.server` page (same precedent as `chat_interface/serve_chat_ui.py`): lists the connected database's tables, and for a selected table shows its real columns plus a green/red checklist against all three known datasets. "Not connected" (missing env vars or an unreachable database) renders as a plain-language banner instead of a crash, mirroring `_choose_snapshot()`'s existing MissingConfigError/PostgresIntegrationError handling pattern in `chat_interface/serve_chat_ui.py`.
       - `logging_setup.py` - the same JSON logger shape every other service uses, stamped `service: "data_console"`.
    2. Pre-commit self-review caught one real issue before it shipped: the first draft of the page's client-side JavaScript built HTML by string-concatenating live database identifiers (table names, column names) into `innerHTML`, using a `textContent`-roundtrip `escapeHtml()` helper that (correctly) escapes `&`/`<`/`>` for text-node content but does **not** escape quote characters - safe for the text between tags, not safe for values placed inside an HTML attribute or inline `onclick="..."` JavaScript string, which is exactly how table names were being embedded. A real Postgres identifier containing a quote is unlikely but not impossible (quoted identifiers permit it), and CLAUDE.md's own security rules and `dashboard/render.py`'s own docstring both treat exactly this class of value (schema/content that came from outside this module's control) as untrusted for HTML-injection purposes. Rewrote the client-side rendering to build DOM nodes directly (`document.createElement` + `textContent` + `dataset`, with click listeners instead of inline `onclick` strings) for every database-derived value, which sidesteps the escaping question entirely rather than trying to get a manual escaper right for every context.
    3. 25 new tests across `data_console/tests/` (`test_column_requirements.py`, `test_requirements_check.py` - case-insensitivity, missing/extra columns, empty input, all three datasets checked at once; `test_schema_inspector.py` - mocks `postgres_connector.fetch_rows` the same way `data_integration/tests/test_postgres_connector.py` already does; `test_serve_data_console.py` - mirrors `chat_interface/tests/test_serve_chat_ui.py`'s in-process HTTP server fixture, covering the not-connected/missing-config, not-connected/unreachable-database, table-not-found, and happy-path-with-requirement-checks cases, plus URL-decoding a table name with a space in it).
  - Verification: `python3 -m pytest data_console/ -q` -> 25 passed. `python3 -m pytest -q` (full suite) -> 562 passed (up from 537; no regressions). Ran the real server live (`eval "$(python3 scripts/local_test_db.py)"` then `python3 -m data_console.serve_data_console`) against the seeded local Postgres and exercised it two ways: `curl` against `/api/tables` and `/api/tables/inventory/columns` confirmed the real seeded database's 9 tables list correctly and `inventory`'s 5 real columns satisfy the Inventory checklist while correctly failing the other two; a headless-Chromium session (installed locally this session via `npx playwright install chromium`, plus a scratch-directory `npm install playwright` so a Node script could drive a real click - both dev-only, removed after use, no manifest change) loaded the page, clicked the `inventory` table, and screenshotted the resulting green/red requirement cards rendering exactly as described above, not a mockup.
  - Notes: Scope is deliberately Slice 1 only - browse, no query building, no joins, no column mapping for differently-named columns, no CSV/zip upload, no raw-SQL box yet, no chat yet. Those are the next agreed slices (2 through 6), each to be built and confirmed the same one-step-at-a-time way STORY-010's chat interface was. Not tied to any `docs/stories/STORY-XXX.md` acceptance criterion, so no story file or `.colaberry/progress.json` change applies here - same treatment as the earlier `.mcp.json`/`pm.js` repo-hygiene entry and this session's own dashboard-readability entry above.

- [x] New capability (not story-scoped): data-console Slice 2 - pick columns, join a second table, and preview a capped, safely-built query
  - Date: 2026-09-08
  - Session: CC-20260908-t3x9
  - What changed: Continued the agreed slice order from Slice 1. Before starting, user asked two grounding questions: where database connection details are stored, and whether an empty/CSV-only setup would break the design. Answered both directly from the code (data_integration/config.py reads 5 env vars fresh every call, nothing is cached in a file; the repo's own `.env` had every line missing `export`, so `source .env` silently would not have exported anything to a Python subprocess - fixed by adding `export ` to each real `KEY=value` line via `sed`, verified live by sourcing it and confirming `load_postgres_config()` picks it up; confirmed the missing-Postgres path degrades to a clean "not connected" message rather than crashing, per Slice 1's own tested failure path, and that CSV/Sheets browsing simply doesn't exist yet - a real gap, not a hidden break, since `requirements_check.py`'s pure logic already works from a bare list of column names regardless of source).
    1. `data_console/query_builder.py` - pure logic (no I/O, matching `requirements_check.py`'s own split): `QuerySelection`/`SelectedColumn`/`JoinSpec` dataclasses, `validate_selection()`, and `build_preview_query()`. Every table/column name is checked against a live schema snapshot the caller supplies before any SQL is built (raises `InvalidSelectionError` otherwise: no columns picked, an unknown table/column, a join that omits the base table, a table joined to itself), and every accepted identifier is still quoted per Postgres's own identifier-quoting rule (`"name"`, embedded `"` doubled) rather than trusted as pre-safe just because it passed the allow-list - documented in the module's own docstring as two independent guards, neither sufficient alone. `PREVIEW_ROW_LIMIT = 500` is baked into every query and is never something a caller can widen, continuing the "never let a live table's full row count reach a browser tab" design decision from the earlier data-connection design conversation.
    2. `data_console/preview_runner.py` - the I/O half: fetches a *fresh* schema snapshot (via `schema_inspector.list_columns()`) for every table a selection references right before building/running its query, rather than trusting whatever the browser last saw the schema to be, then executes the built query through `data_integration.postgres_connector.fetch_rows()` (same retry/timeout/error handling as everywhere else) and recomputes the requirement checklist against the *combined* selected columns - so a user can see whether joining two tables together now satisfies a check that neither table alone did.
    3. `data_console/serve_data_console.py`: added `POST /api/preview` (parses/validates the request shape, builds a `QuerySelection`, calls `run_preview()`, maps `InvalidSelectionError` to 400, `MissingConfigError`/`PostgresIntegrationError` to the same `connected: false` shape Slice 1 already established) plus the same guarded `_read_request_body()`/`MAX_REQUEST_BODY_BYTES` Content-Length handling `chat_interface/serve_chat_ui.py` already proved necessary - added proactively this time, before any bug forced it, as a direct application of that earlier lesson. Also added `_json_default()`: psycopg2 hands back `Decimal` for numeric columns and `date`/`datetime` for date columns, neither of which `json.dumps()` can serialize on its own - caught in a live run against real data (the very first real preview crashed on `inventory.current_stock`), fixed by converting to a float/ISO-8601 string at the JSON boundary, the same plain types every other JSON boundary in this repo already uses.
    4. Extended the page's client-side JS: checkboxes per column (all checked by default for the base table, unchecked for a joined-in table), a "+ Join another table" control that fetches the chosen table's columns via the already-existing `/api/tables/<name>/columns` endpoint and offers two dropdowns to pick the join columns (pre-guessing a same-named column on both sides, always overridable), and a Preview button rendering the SQL actually run, the resulting rows (capped, with a "there may be more" note when truncated), and the requirement checklist re-evaluated against the current selection. All of it built the same DOM-node way Slice 1's browse view was (`textContent`/`dataset`, no `innerHTML` string-concatenation of database-derived values) rather than reusing Slice 1's `escapeHtml()` helper, which - as Slice 1's own commit already documented - only escapes `&`/`<`/`>`, not quote characters, so is not actually safe for attribute or inline-script contexts.
    5. Two real bugs caught only by live browser testing, not by the (separately passing) unit test suite: (a) the first live test run silently failed because an **earlier Slice-1 server process from this same session had never actually been killed** and was still holding port 8766, so every "live" check was against stale, pre-Slice-2 code with no error of any kind - confirmed via `lsof -i :8766` and the crash trace in the new server's own stdout (`OSError: Address already in use`), not a code bug at all but a process-hygiene mistake worth naming since it produced a convincingly wrong "the feature doesn't exist" signal; (b) the client-side rendering initially checked `if (!data.connected)` before `if (data.error)` when handling the preview response, but a 400 (invalid selection) body has no `connected` key at all, so that ordering would have rendered an "undefined" not-connected banner instead of the real validation error - caught by re-reading the two response shapes side by side before testing, fixed by checking `error` first, and confirmed live afterward (see verification).
  - Verification: `python3 -m pytest data_console/ -q` -> 54 passed (up from 25; 29 new: 17 in `test_query_builder.py` covering every validation branch including a double-quote-containing identifier, 12 in `test_preview_runner.py` and the preview additions to `test_serve_data_console.py` including a dedicated Decimal/date serialization regression test and the same three Content-Length edge cases `chat_interface`'s test suite already covers). `python3 -m pytest -q` (full suite) -> 591 passed, no regressions. Live end-to-end run against `scripts/local_test_db.py`'s seeded Postgres via a headless-Chromium session driven by a real Node/Playwright script (dev-only, installed to a scratch directory, removed after use): selected `inventory`, unchecked `lead_time_days`, added a join to `customer_orders` (auto-matched on `sku`), checked `customer_orders.quantity`, clicked Preview, and confirmed the exact SQL shown (`SELECT "inventory"."sku", ... FROM "inventory" JOIN "customer_orders" ON "inventory"."sku" = "customer_orders"."sku" LIMIT 500`), 12 real joined rows including the seeded July demand-spike row (quantity 900), zero browser console errors, and the requirement checklist correctly flipping `Inventory` to "missing something" once `lead_time_days` was unchecked. Separately drove the no-columns-selected case end-to-end and confirmed the 400 error renders in the error box with the real validation message, not the not-connected banner.
  - Notes: Still exactly one join supported per preview (matching the "cap the guided builder, offer a raw-query escape hatch beyond that" design decision from the earlier architecture discussion) - a second join is Slice 3's raw-SQL fallback, not an extension of this UI. No `docs/stories/STORY-XXX.md` or `.colaberry/progress.json` change applies here, same reasoning as Slice 1's own entry above. The `.env` fix earlier in this session is not part of this commit - it's gitignored, untracked, and already verified working live; nothing to ship.

- [x] New capability (not story-scoped): data-console "Map Your Data" - point each of the 3 known datasets at any table, even with completely different column names, using this repo's existing multi-tenant mapping engine
  - Date: 2026-09-08
  - Session: CC-20260908-t3x9
  - What changed: While testing Slice 2's join builder, the user tried joining `customer_orders` to `acme_inventory` on `sku = item_sku` and got 0 rows with the checklist still saying "Inventory - missing something," even though `acme_inventory` conceptually has everything Inventory needs under different names. Investigated rather than assumed: `risk_detection/run_sample_multi_tenant_risk_detection.py`'s own docstring confirmed `acme_*`/`globex_*` are two intentionally-unrelated fictitious-tenant fixtures seeded specifically to exercise `data_integration/connection_profile.py`'s schema-mapping engine - the join legitimately matched nothing, and the checklist gap was real (exact-name matching only). Reported both findings back plainly, then scoped the fix as a new capability: reuse `connection_profile.py`'s existing, already-tested `ConnectionProfile`/`validate_profile()`/`fetch_profile_data()`/`remap_rows()` rather than inventing a second mapping engine. Before building, asked the user how far to take "help me find the right table" (manual search vs. an auto-ranking scan of every table) - they redirected the framing entirely: show what's required *first*, in plain language, so a person can judge for themselves which table matches, rather than the tool guessing; and separately asked what happens when the needed data genuinely isn't in this database at all. Both answers shaped the design below rather than being decided unilaterally.
    1. `data_console/mapping_suggester.py` - pure, no I/O: `suggest_mapping()` proposes one column per required/optional field using exact-name-first, then a small synonym table (`SYNONYMS`, partly drawn directly from the real `acme_inventory`/`acme_orders`/`acme_deliveries` column names in the multi-tenant fixture: `item_sku`, `on_hand`, `min_stock`, `daily_use`, `lead_days`, `order_dt`, `qty`, `po_number`, `due_date`, `received_date`). Explicitly documented as a starting-point guess a person still confirms or overrides - `connection_profile.py`'s own docstring already insists a mapping be "explicit, never inferred" at the point it's used for a live pull, so this module only pre-fills the form, it never itself decides a mapping is correct.
    2. `data_console/mapping_store.py` - a tiny local JSON file (`data_console/.mappings.json`, gitignored - it encodes real schema details about whatever database this console happens to be pointed at, which has no business in a public portfolio repo) holding one `DatasetMapping` per known dataset kind with a real tri-state `status`: `"mapped"` (table + column_mapping), `"unavailable"` (this data genuinely isn't in this database - the user's exact second question), or absent entirely ("not mapped yet"). Corrupted/unreadable file degrades to "nothing saved" rather than crashing, the same tolerant-reload pattern every `*AuditStore` in this repo already follows for its own JSONL file.
    3. `data_console/mapping_service.py` - bridges the store to the real engine: `save_mapping()` builds a `ConnectionProfile` (query built via `query_builder.quote_identifier()`, promoted from private to public this session since a second module now needs the exact same Postgres-identifier-quoting logic - one implementation to audit, not two) and calls `connection_profile.validate_profile()` *before* ever writing to the store, so nothing is saved unless it's already confirmed against the live database; `mark_unavailable()`/`clear_mapping()` wrap the store directly; `preview_mapping()` calls `connection_profile.fetch_profile_data()` (validate + fetch + remap + audit, unchanged) with a `LIMIT` appended to the profile's own query, capped at the same `query_builder.PREVIEW_ROW_LIMIT` every other preview in this console already uses.
    4. `data_console/serve_data_console.py`: added `GET /api/datasets` (the 3 datasets' field requirements, so the UI can show "what's needed" before any table is even considered - directly per the user's redirection), `GET /api/mappings` (current status of all 3), `POST /api/mappings/<kind>` (validate + save, `SchemaMappingError` -> 400), `POST /api/mappings/<kind>/unavailable`, `DELETE /api/mappings/<kind>` (reset to not-mapped), and `GET /api/mappings/<kind>/preview` (also maps a `SchemaMappingError` here specifically to a clear 400 rather than a crash or a misleading "not connected" - this is what happens automatically if the underlying table changes after a mapping was saved, since `fetch_profile_data()` re-validates the live schema on every call). Extended the existing `/api/tables/<name>/columns` response with a `suggested_mappings` field so the browse view and the mapping picker share one source for suggestions.
    5. New "Map Your Data" section, rendered above the existing browse view (turns "hundreds of tables to scroll through" into "3 slots to fill," regardless of how many tables exist underneath): one card per dataset always showing its full required/optional field list with plain-English descriptions and examples up front (per the user's explicit redirection away from an auto-guessing table-suggestion feature), a status line, and buttons that change with state (`Map this`/`Not available in this database` when unmapped; `Preview`/`Change`/`Clear` when mapped; `Map this`/`Undo "not available"` when marked unavailable). "Map this" opens an inline search box (client-side filter over the already-fetched table list - the direct answer to "hundreds of tables") plus, once a table's picked, one dropdown per field pre-filled with `suggest_mapping()`'s guess, always overridable, with client-side validation blocking Save until every required field has a real column chosen. Reused Slice 2's `buildRowsTable()`/`el()`/`clear()` helpers rather than duplicating table-rendering logic a third time.
    6. One real bug caught only by live testing, not by the (separately passing) unit suite: the rows-table preview inside a `.mapping-card` clipped silently when wider than the card (visible in a live screenshot, not from any test assertion) - fixed by wrapping `buildRowsTable()`'s table output in a new `.table-scroll` (`overflow-x: auto`) container, applied to both Slice 2's and this session's preview so a wide result scrolls instead of clipping either place.
    7. Repeated the exact stale-server mistake this session's own Slice 2 entry already named, and caught it the same way before it could produce a false signal: killed the leftover process and confirmed a genuinely fresh server (`curl .../api/datasets`) before running any live test this time, rather than discovering it mid-test again.
  - Verification: `python3 -m pytest data_console/ -q` -> 93 passed (up from 54; 39 new across `test_mapping_suggester.py`, `test_mapping_store.py` - including a corrupted-file-tolerance test - `test_mapping_service.py`, and the mapping-endpoint additions to `test_serve_data_console.py`, all using a `tmp_path`-backed `MappingStore` so no test ever touches the real `data_console/.mappings.json`). `python3 -m pytest -q` (full suite) -> 630 passed, no regressions. Live end-to-end run via a fresh headless-Chromium/Playwright session (dev-only, scratch directory, removed after use) against `scripts/local_test_db.py`'s seeded Postgres, using the exact `acme_inventory` table that surfaced this need: opened the page, confirmed all 3 dataset cards show their full field requirements before any table is picked; clicked "Map this" on Inventory, searched "acme_inv", picked `acme_inventory`, confirmed all 5 fields were correctly pre-suggested (`sku`->`item_sku`, `current_stock`->`on_hand`, `safety_stock`->`min_stock`, `daily_demand_rate`->`daily_use`, `lead_time_days`->`lead_days`), saved, and confirmed the card flipped to "Mapped to \"acme_inventory\""; clicked Preview and confirmed real `acme_inventory` data came back already relabeled under the standard field names (`sku`, `current_stock`, etc.); marked Delivery Records "Not available in this database" and confirmed the card showed that state with an undo option. Zero browser console errors throughout.

Deleted `buildReqGrid()` itself and its `.req-grid`/`.req-card`/`.req-line`/`.dot`/`.dot-yes`/`.dot-no` CSS, none of which had any other caller.

- [x] New capability (not story-scoped): data-console "Map Your Data" - raw-SQL fallback for a dataset whose columns are split across more tables than the guided picker reaches
  - Date: 2026-09-08
  - Session: CC-20260908-t3x9
  - What changed: Direct follow-up to the previous entry's second open question - Map Your Data's guided table picker maps a dataset to exactly one table (no join at all, stricter even than Slice 2's own one-join browse builder), so a dataset whose required fields are genuinely split across two or more tables had no path to a saved mapping. Recommended two options (widen the guided picker's join cap, or build the raw-SQL escape hatch the user had already agreed to via AskUserQuestion earlier this session) and recommended the raw-SQL path as already-agreed and immediately sufficient rather than an open-ended multi-join picker; user confirmed ("Yes. build it").
    1. `data_console/raw_query_validator.py` (new, pure, no I/O): `validate_read_only_query()` - the guardrails the user signed off on earlier ("Yes, include it (Recommended)"). Documented explicitly, in its own module docstring, as a blunt text-level guard rather than a substitute for a read-only database role: rejects an empty query, more than one statement (a second `;` beyond an optional single trailing one), anything not starting with `SELECT`/`WITH`, and a denylist of write/DDL/session keywords and a few dangerous functions (`INSERT`, `DROP`, `COPY`, `PG_SLEEP`, `DBLINK`, etc.) via whole-word matching - explicitly not a SQL parser, so a query starting with a comment is rejected rather than having its comment stripped, and a string literal that happens to contain a blocked word is also rejected; both documented tradeoffs favor rejecting a legitimate query over accepting an unsafe one.
    2. `data_console/mapping_store.py`: `DatasetMapping` gained `source_kind` (`"table"` default, or `"query"`) and `query` fields, so a saved mapping now records *how* it was built, not just what it maps to. Old saved files with neither field still load correctly (`source_kind` defaults to `"table"`), since `MappingStore.load_all()` already builds `DatasetMapping` from a plain `**data` unpack.
    3. `data_console/mapping_service.py`: `_build_profile()` now takes a fully-formed query string rather than a bare table name (the table-picker path builds `SELECT * FROM "table"` exactly as before; the query path passes the validated raw SQL straight through - `connection_profile.py` itself already accepted arbitrary SQL in `ConnectionProfile.query`, confirmed by reading `validate_against_live_schema()`'s own use of `postgres_connector.fetch_columns()`, so no change was needed there). Added `probe_query_columns()` (validates, then wraps the query in `fetch_columns()`'s existing zero-row LIMIT-0 subquery probe - the same technique already proven for a bare table, now proven for an arbitrary join too) and `save_mapping_from_query()` (validate-guardrail, then validate-live-schema via the same `connection_profile.validate_profile()` every other mapping path already uses, then persist - nothing saved unless both checks pass). `preview_mapping()` now branches on `source_kind`, and its LIMIT-append was hardened from bare string concatenation to the same subquery-wrapping technique (`SELECT * FROM (query) AS mapping_preview LIMIT n`) for both source kinds - a raw query is exactly the kind of input that could legally end in its own `ORDER BY`, or worse, a trailing `--` comment that would have silently swallowed a concatenated `LIMIT`, defeating the row cap that is this console's one hard safety line against ever loading a whole table into a browser tab.
    4. `data_console/serve_data_console.py`: added `POST /api/query-columns` (probes a typed query's result columns + returns the same `suggested_mappings` shape `/api/tables/<name>/columns` already returns, so the auto-guess quality is identical regardless of path) and `POST /api/mappings/<kind>/from-query` (mirrors `_handle_save_mapping`'s error-mapping exactly, plus `UnsafeQueryError` -> 400). Extracted `_parse_column_mapping()` since both save handlers needed the identical shape check. Frontend: `startMapping()` now offers "Or write your own SQL query" alongside the existing table search; `writeQueryForMapping()` renders a textarea, a visible one-line summary of the guardrail, and a "Check columns" step before the mapping form appears; the field-dropdown form itself (`buildMappingFieldForm()`) was extracted out of the former `pickTableForMapping()` so both paths render and validate the exact same UI rather than a second near-duplicate. `mappingStatusLine()` now reads "Mapped via a custom query" for a query-sourced mapping instead of a table name.
    5. One real bug caught only by live browser testing, not by the (separately passing) unit suite: two of the new plain-English strings used an apostrophe escaped as `\'` inside the Python source's own `_PAGE_TEMPLATE` triple-quoted string - Python's own string escaping silently strips that backslash before the HTML is ever served, so the *served* JavaScript contained a real, unescaped apostrophe closing its string literal early, producing a page-breaking `Unexpected identifier 'll'` syntax error the moment the page loaded (visible only as a blank "Loading..." mapping section with zero cards, in the browser - not in any Python test, since nothing in this repo executes the served JavaScript). Fixed by rewriting both strings to avoid the trap (one reworded without a contraction, one switched to a JS double-quoted literal), and reproducing + re-verifying the fix live before moving on.
  - Verification: `python3 -m pytest data_console/ -q` -> 121 passed (up from 85; 36 new: 19 in `test_raw_query_validator.py` covering every guardrail branch including the deliberate string-literal/comment false-negative cases, plus additions to `test_mapping_service.py` and `test_serve_data_console.py` for the new save/probe/preview paths). `python3 -m pytest -q` (full suite) -> 658 passed, no regressions. Live end-to-end run against `scripts/local_test_db.py`'s seeded Postgres, using two throwaway scratch tables created and dropped this session specifically to prove a real cross-table join (`scratch_po_dates` + `scratch_po_supplier`, joined on `po_id`, standing in for exactly the "Delivery Records split across two tables" scenario that motivated this feature): `curl`-driven checks confirmed `/api/query-columns` correctly probed the real joined result (4 of 5 fields auto-suggested, `transportation_cost` correctly left for manual pick since `ship_cost` isn't in the synonym list), saving via `/api/mappings/delivery_records/from-query` persisted with `source_kind: "query"`, and `/api/mappings/delivery_records/preview` returned real remapped rows from the actual two-table join. Guardrails re-verified as real HTTP behavior, not just unit-test mocks: a `DROP TABLE` attempt, a stacked `SELECT 1; DELETE ...` attempt, and a genuinely malformed query were each rejected with a clear 400 and the scratch table's row count was confirmed unchanged afterward. A full headless-Chromium/Playwright pass through the actual UI (dev-only, scratch directory, removed after use) - caught the apostrophe bug above, then re-ran clean after the fix - clicked "Map this" -> "Or write your own SQL query" on the Delivery Records card, typed the same real join, clicked "Check columns," filled the one unsuggested field, saved, confirmed the card read "Mapped via a custom query" with a green border, clicked Preview, and confirmed the real joined rows rendered with zero browser console errors, screenshotted at each step.
  - Notes: Both scratch Postgres tables and the saved mapping used for live verification were dropped/cleared before ending the session, per this console's own gitignored-state convention - `data_console/.mappings.json` never carries test artifacts into the next session. Not tied to any `docs/stories/STORY-XXX.md` acceptance criterion, same reasoning as every other data-console entry above.

- [x] New capability (not story-scoped): data-console "Map Your Data" - CSV upload as a fourth mapping source, usable with no database connection at all
  - Date: 2026-09-08
  - Session: CC-20260908-t3x9
  - What changed: Next roadmap slice after the raw-SQL fallback, scoped deliberately to CSV only this pass - zip-of-CSVs and Google Sheets (a real external-dependency/OAuth-vs-public-link design fork) were named up front and deferred to their own future steps rather than folded into one large slice. This directly answers the user's own open question from earlier in the session ("if we only have csv or Google sheet files, these values will be empty, will it break the design?") with working code instead of just a design answer.
    1. `data_console/file_store.py` (new): saves an uploaded CSV to `data_console/uploads/<uuid>.csv` (gitignored, mirrors `.mappings.json`'s own reasoning - real uploaded data has no business in a public repo) and re-reads it fresh on every probe/validate call, the same "never trust a schema snapshot the browser last saw" principle every live-Postgres path in this console already follows. `MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES` (10 MB) guards against a mistaken huge upload exhausting this single-threaded dev server's memory, the same reasoning `MAX_REQUEST_BODY_BYTES` already applies elsewhere - `_read_request_body()` was parameterized (`max_bytes=`) so the tiny 64KB cap on every other JSON route is untouched while the upload route alone gets the larger one.
    2. `data_console/file_mapping_service.py` (new) - a sibling to `mapping_service.py`, not a branch inside it: `connection_profile.py`'s `ConnectionProfile` always carries a `PostgresConfig`, so a file-backed mapping (no database connection at all) genuinely doesn't fit through that engine. Reuses what's actually shared and Postgres-agnostic - `REQUIRED_FIELDS_BY_DATASET_KIND` and the pure `remap_rows()` - and raises the same `SchemaMappingError` connection_profile.py's own Postgres path already raises for the same two failure shapes (incomplete mapping; a mapped column that doesn't actually exist), so `serve_data_console.py`'s existing exception handling needed no new branch for it.
    3. `data_console/mapping_store.py`: `DatasetMapping` gained a third `source_kind` value (`"file"`) plus `file_id`/`filename` fields - `filename` is stored directly on the mapping (not looked up from the upload store at render time) purely so the status line can name the file back to a person without a second lookup.
    4. `data_console/serve_data_console.py`: `POST /api/uploads` (raw CSV bytes as the body, original filename passed via an `X-Filename` header - URL-encoded client-side and decoded server-side, since a raw header can't safely carry an arbitrary filename - probes columns immediately and returns the same `suggested_mappings` shape every other probe endpoint returns) and `POST /api/mappings/<kind>/from-file`. `mapping_service.preview_mapping()` now dispatches to `file_mapping_service.preview_file_mapping()` for a file-backed mapping via a one-way import (file_mapping_service never imports mapping_service back), keeping the two engines as clean siblings rather than one importing into the other; `UnknownUploadError` is a `LookupError` subclass, so the HTTP handler's existing `except LookupError` branch already covers "the saved mapping points at a file that no longer exists" with no new code. `mappingStatusLine()` now reads `Mapped from uploaded file "..."` for this source kind.
    5. A real, no-Postgres-required design bug caught before it shipped, not after: the very first draft of `startMapping()`'s not-connected handling `clear()`-ed the *entire* picker area (including the newly-added "Or write your own SQL query" and "Or upload a CSV file" links) the moment `/api/tables` reported no database - which would have made CSV upload itself unreachable in exactly the one scenario it exists for. Caught by re-reading the existing not-connected branch before adding the new upload link next to it, not by any test (no test asserted on this). Fixed by scoping the not-connected banner to just the table-search results box, leaving both escape-hatch links always reachable regardless of database state.
    6. A second real bug, this one caught by live testing (not any unit test) and with real consequences: `file_mapping_service.py` imports `MappingStore` under its own module-level name (`from data_console.mapping_store import ... MappingStore`), so the existing `isolated_mapping_store` test fixture - which patched only `mapping_service.MappingStore` and `serve_data_console.MappingStore` - never touched it. The first from-file save test appeared to pass (200, `{"saved": true}`) but a follow-up `GET /api/mappings` showed `not_mapped`, because the save had gone to the real, unpatched `MappingStore()` default - meaning the test run wrote real (fake, nonexistent-file-id) rows into the actual local `data_console/.mappings.json` on disk. Fixed by adding the third patch target to the fixture, and cleaned up the polluted file (confirmed, after the fix, that a full test run leaves it untouched). Named here because it is exactly the "patch where it's used, not where it's defined" class of bug, and it briefly wrote real garbage into gitignored-but-real local state before being caught.
  - Verification: `python3 -m pytest data_console/ -q` -> 144 passed (up from 121; 23 new across `test_file_store.py`, `test_file_mapping_service.py`, and the upload/from-file/preview-for-file additions to `test_serve_data_console.py`). `python3 -m pytest -q` (full suite) -> 681 passed, no regressions. Live end-to-end run with a fresh server started with **zero Postgres environment variables set at all** (confirmed via `curl .../api/tables` returning `connected: false` before touching anything else) - the exact scenario this slice exists for: `curl`-driven checks confirmed `POST /api/uploads` accepted a real CSV with no database reachable, correctly probed its two columns and auto-suggested both required Customer Orders fields; `POST /api/mappings/customer_orders/from-file` validated against the file's real headers and saved; `GET /api/mappings/customer_orders/preview` returned the real remapped CSV rows - all three with the database fully disconnected the entire time. A full headless-Chromium/Playwright pass through the actual UI (dev-only, scratch directory, removed after use) confirmed the "Or upload a CSV file" link is visible and clickable even while the table-search box shows its own not-connected banner right below it, uploaded a real file via a native file input, confirmed all 5 Inventory fields auto-suggested, saved, confirmed the card read `Mapped from uploaded file "upload.csv"`, and previewed the real uploaded rows - zero browser console errors, screenshotted at each step.
  - Notes: `data_console/uploads/` added to `.gitignore` alongside the existing `.mappings.json`/`mapping_audit_log.jsonl` entries - same reasoning, real user data has no business in this repo. Every mapping and uploaded file used for live verification was cleared/deleted before ending the session. Not tied to any `docs/stories/STORY-XXX.md` acceptance criterion, same reasoning as every other data-console entry above. Zip-of-CSVs upload and Google Sheets support remain explicitly deferred, named to the user as this slice's own scope boundary rather than silently dropped.

- [x] New capability (not story-scoped): data-console "Map Your Data" - upload a .zip of several CSVs to fill more than one dataset slot from a single file
  - Date: 2026-09-08
  - Session: CC-20260908-t3x9
  - What changed: User set the order for the remaining roadmap explicitly - zip-of-CSVs first, then Google Sheets, then a UI check before moving on to chat-driven Q&A. This entry covers zip only. No new external dependency: Python's stdlib `zipfile` module reads the archive; nothing new was added to any manifest.
    1. `data_console/file_store.py`: added `extract_csvs_from_zip(content) -> list[(file_id, member_filename)]` and a new `UnsafeArchiveError`. Every `.csv` member gets saved individually through the *existing* `save_upload()` (so a zip's contents are indistinguishable from three separate single-file uploads once extracted - no second storage code path to keep in sync). Three safety checks before anything is written to disk: the archive must actually be a valid zip; it must contain no more than `MAX_ZIP_MEMBERS` (10) entries (this console only ever has 3 dataset slots to fill, so a much larger zip is almost certainly not "one CSV per dataset"); and no `.csv` member may exceed `MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES` once *decompressed* - a deliberate zip-bomb guard, since a tiny compressed file can expand to something huge and the raw upload's own size cap only bounds the compressed bytes actually received over HTTP. A member's path inside the archive (e.g. `"data/orders.csv"`) is used only as display text via its basename; every file this function writes still gets a fresh `save_upload()`-generated id, so there is no zip-slip / path-traversal surface regardless of what a member's own name claims to be.
    2. `data_console/serve_data_console.py`: `POST /api/uploads` now branches on the uploaded filename's extension - a `.zip` is extracted (each member immediately probed for columns and suggested mappings, same as a single CSV) and returned as `{"kind": "zip", "filename": ..., "members": [...]}`; anything else is treated as a single CSV exactly as before, now returned as `{"kind": "csv", ...}` so the frontend can tell the two shapes apart. Extracted a `_suggested_mappings_for(columns)` helper - the same `{dataset_name: suggest_mapping(...)}` computation had reached three call sites (columns endpoint, query-columns endpoint, and now the upload endpoint), crossing this repo's own "three call sites, lift it" composition rule; all three now share one implementation.
    3. Frontend: `uploadFileForMapping()`'s file input now also accepts `.zip`; a `"zip"`-kind response renders a new `renderZipMemberPicker()` (a list of the archive's CSV member names with their column counts - click one to open the same mapping form a single CSV upload would show, via a new shared `renderFileMappingForm()` extracted out of what was single-CSV-only logic before). A module-level `lastZipUpload` cache remembers the most recently uploaded zip's already-extracted members for the rest of the page's lifetime, so mapping a second or third dataset from the *same* zip surfaces an "Or pick from the last uploaded zip" link instead of asking the file to be re-uploaded two more times - this is what actually makes "one zip, three datasets" practical rather than just technically possible.
  - Verification: `python3 -m pytest data_console/ -q` -> 154 passed (up from 144; 10 new: 7 in `test_file_store.py` covering every guardrail branch of `extract_csvs_from_zip` including a patched-down `MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES` to exercise the zip-bomb check without actually constructing a huge file, and 3 new upload-endpoint tests in `test_serve_data_console.py`). `python3 -m pytest -q` (full suite) -> 691 passed, no regressions. Live end-to-end run with a fresh server started with **zero Postgres environment variables set** (same no-database condition as the CSV-upload entry above): built a real 3-file zip (`orders.csv`, `inventory.csv`, `deliveries.csv`) and, via a headless-Chromium/Playwright script (dev-only, scratch directory, removed after use), uploaded it once on the Customer Orders card, picked `orders.csv` from the member list, saved; then reused the cached "last uploaded zip" link on both the Inventory and Delivery Records cards (no second or third upload) to pick `inventory.csv` and `deliveries.csv` respectively - all three cards ended mapped (`Mapped from uploaded file "..."`), all three green-bordered, zero browser console errors, screenshotted before and after. Separately confirmed both guardrails as real HTTP behavior rather than only unit-test mocks: a 15-member zip was rejected with "zip contains more than 10 files," and a plain text blob named `fake.zip` was rejected with "not a valid zip file" - neither wrote anything to the uploads directory.
  - Notes: Every mapping and every uploaded file (single-zip's 3 extracted CSVs plus a rejected too-many-members test zip) used for live verification were cleared/deleted before ending the session. Not tied to any `docs/stories/STORY-XXX.md` acceptance criterion, same reasoning as every other data-console entry above. Google Sheets support is next per the user's own stated order, still deferred; the user has also asked to keep all data-console work local and not push to GitHub until explicitly told to - no `git push` has been run this session or any prior one, confirmed via `git log origin/main..HEAD`.

- [x] New capability (not story-scoped): data-console "Map Your Data" - Google Sheets CSV link support (public "publish to web" links, no OAuth)
  - Date: 2026-09-08
  - Session: CC-20260908-t3x9

Researched via WebSearch and answered in full before touching any code (see the conversation turn immediately preceding this one): confirmed each Google Sheets tab needs its own distinct export URL (`...export?format=csv&gid=N`) - "multiple sheets from one link" isn't actually possible, and "multiple links for one project" is already free given each of the 3 dataset cards already takes an independent source; recommended against building multi-database-engine support now since it's a real architecture change (new driver dependency, new identifier-quoting dialect, new connector module per engine) rather than a config edit, and flagged that decision as exactly the kind of "new external dependency" call this repo's own CLAUDE.md requires explicit sign-off for; confirmed `.env` needs zero changes for the Sheets-link approach specifically (no credentials at all - a public link needs none) but would not be sufficient on its own for a new database engine. Every request carries an explicit 10-second timeout and a capped read size (`MAX_SHEET_BYTES`, same 10MB cap as an uploaded file), per this repo's own "every outbound call has an explicit timeout" rule. A single attempt, not retried - documented as a deliberate simplification, since the two realistic failure causes (a mistyped/unpublished link, or a response that isn't real CSV) are both input problems a retry cannot fix. Sniffs the response body itself (not just the `Content-Type` header) for an HTML page, since the most common real failure mode - a sheet that isn't actually shared publicly - returns Google's HTML sign-in page with a plain 200 status rather than any error status, which would otherwise be silently parsed as garbage "CSV."

Deliberately *not* a variant of the file-upload path: a sheet's content is never saved to disk at all, and every probe/validate/preview call fetches fresh over the network - the same "never trust a stale snapshot" principle a live Postgres table's schema and an uploaded file's on-disk headers already follow, applied here so that editing the live Google Sheet later is reflected the next time this console previews it, rather than showing a frozen copy from whenever the link was first pasted.

Frontend: a fourth escape-hatch link, "Or paste a Google Sheets link," opens a single-line URL input + "Check link" step (same shape as the raw-SQL box's "Check columns" step), then the same shared `buildMappingFieldForm()` every other source kind already uses. `mappingStatusLine()` reads "Mapped from a Google Sheet link" for this source kind.

Fixed by adding the fourth patch target; a full test run afterward leaves the real file untouched. Named explicitly in the fixture's own comment now, calling out that this exact gap already bit `file_mapping_service` once, so the next new source-kind module added later should check this fixture immediately rather than rediscovering the same bug a fourth time.

- [x] Bug fix (not story-scoped): data-console - CSV upload cap rejected a real, non-malicious dataset export
  - Date: 2026-09-08
  - Session: CC-20260908-t3x9
  - What changed: During their own "check the UI" pass, the user hit a hard 400 uploading a real CSV (`DataCoSupplyChainDataset.csv`, ~91MB / 95,910,149 bytes) via the Choose File + Upload flow: `invalid request: Content-Length 95910149 exceeds the 10485760-byte limit`. Root cause: `MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES` in `data_console/file_store.py` was set to 10MB, sized around "a handful of mapped columns," not around what a real-world supply-chain CSV export actually weighs once it includes every column and every row, not just the few this console maps. This is a genuine dataset, not a mistaken or malicious upload, so the fix is to raise the cap rather than ask the user to shrink or pre-trim the file.
    1. Raised `MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES` from `10 * 1024 * 1024` to `200 * 1024 * 1024` in `data_console/file_store.py` - comfortable headroom above the file that triggered this, while still bounding a truly absurd multi-gigabyte upload from hanging this single-threaded local dev server (the cap's original purpose, per its own comment, which was reworded to say so explicitly). This single constant also gates the upload route's own `Content-Length` pre-check (`serve_data_console.py`'s `_handle_upload` calls `_read_request_body(max_bytes=MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES)`) and the per-member decompressed-size guard for a zip upload (`extract_csvs_from_zip`), so both paths pick up the new limit for free - no other code changed.
    2. Confirmed no test hardcodes the old 10MB value or its exact byte count anywhere except `test_file_store.py`'s own zip-bomb-guard test, which already patches `MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES` down to `5` bytes locally via `unittest.mock.patch` rather than relying on the real constant - unaffected by the change.
  - Verification: `python3 -m pytest data_console/tests/ -q` -> 184 passed, no regressions.
  - Notes: Not yet re-verified against the user's actual 91MB file end-to-end (that requires the user's own browser action) - flagging here so it's checked as part of the same "check the UI" pass already in progress, rather than assumed fixed from the code change alone. - [x] Bug fix (not story-scoped): data-console - CSV upload appeared to hang indefinitely in Safari for a large file
  - Date: 2026-09-08
  - Session: CC-20260908-t3x9
  - What changed: Immediately after the 200MB cap fix above, the user retried their real ~91MB CSV in Safari and reported the upload as genuinely stuck ("Uploading...", no error, no completion). Root cause, confirmed rather than assumed: the upload button's handler did `const content = await file.arrayBuffer();` before ever calling `fetch()` - materializing the entire file into a JS ArrayBuffer up front. WebKit/Safari has a well-known, disproportionate slowdown converting a large `File`/`Blob` into an `ArrayBuffer` this way (this can take minutes or appear to hang, even though the identical code is fast in Chromium). Confirming evidence: a `curl` upload of a 70MB CSV straight to the running server completed in 1.3 seconds both before and after this fix, proving the backend was never the bottleneck - and, tellingly, that curl request succeeded *while* the user's Safari tab was reportedly still stuck, meaning the browser had not even reached the network step yet (the `fetch()` call only happens after `arrayBuffer()` resolves) - strong evidence the hang was purely client-side, in that JS step.
    1. `data_console/serve_data_console.py`'s `uploadFileForMapping()`: removed the `await file.arrayBuffer()` step entirely and pass the `File` object directly as `fetch()`'s `body`. `fetch()` accepts a `File`/`Blob` body natively, streams it straight from disk with a correctly computed `Content-Length`, and never requires the whole file to be copied into JS heap memory first - strictly better for every browser, not just a Safari-specific workaround (also halves peak memory use in the tab, since the old code held both the browser's own file buffer and a duplicate JS ArrayBuffer copy at once).
    2. No backend change needed - `/api/uploads` already reads the raw request body by `Content-Length` regardless of what produced it browser-side.
  - Verification: `python3 -m pytest data_console/tests/ -q` -> 184 passed, no regressions (pure frontend JS change, no Python surface touched). `python3 -c "import data_console.serve_data_console"` confirms the edited `_PAGE_TEMPLATE` JS is still embedded in valid Python (this repo's own recurring gotcha - Python's string escaping can silently mangle embedded JS; grepped for it again here, clean). Re-ran the same `curl -X POST /api/uploads` timing test against the restarted server post-fix: still 1.3s for a 70MB file, confirming the backend path is unaffected. Test mapping/upload artifacts created during backend-side verification were cleared via `DELETE /api/mappings/<kind>` and deleting the uploaded file's id from `data_console/uploads/` before ending, so the console is back to a clean `not_mapped` state for the user's next real attempt.
  - Notes: The actual fix (skipping `arrayBuffer()` in favor of passing `File` directly) could not be verified against real Safari in this session - no Safari automation available here, and the user is testing by hand in their own Safari anyway. Asked the user to retry their real 91MB upload next; this is the natural, and only reliable, way to confirm the fix. If it's still slow after this change, that would point away from the arrayBuffer-conversion theory and toward something else (e.g. antivirus/proxy inspection on the loopback connection, or disk I/O on their machine) - worth knowing either way. Local-only; no `git push` run.

- [x] Bug fix (not story-scoped): data-console - real CSV upload crashed the server on a non-UTF-8 encoding, hanging the browser with zero response
  - Date: 2026-09-09
  - Session: CC-20260908-t3x9
  - What changed: The user's real upload of their DataCo Supply Chain dataset (a well-known public CSV export) still showed "Uploading..." forever, even after the earlier Safari-arrayBuffer fix. The server log showed the real cause: an uncaught `UnicodeDecodeError` inside `file_store.py`'s `_read_text()` - `path.read_text(encoding="utf-8-sig")` assumed the upload was UTF-8, but this dataset embeds Portuguese city/state names (e.g. "São Paulo") as Latin-1 bytes, which are not valid UTF-8. Because nothing on that code path caught `UnicodeDecodeError`, the exception propagated all the way up to `BaseHTTPRequestHandler`'s default handling, which logs a traceback server-side and drops the TCP connection with zero response bytes sent - indistinguishable, from the browser's `fetch()` side, from an indefinite hang (no resolve, no reject the UI code was watching for), which is exactly why it looked permanently "stuck" rather than showing any error.
    1. **Root-cause fix** (`data_console/file_store.py`'s `_read_text()`): read raw bytes and try `utf-8-sig` first as before; on `UnicodeDecodeError`, fall back to decoding as `latin-1`. latin-1 is a single-byte encoding where every value 0x00-0xFF maps to a real character, so this fallback is mathematically guaranteed to succeed rather than raising a second time - this is also the standard, widely-documented fix for this exact public dataset (commonly needs `encoding='latin1'` in pandas tutorials too), not a guess.
    2. **Hardening, per this repo's own Failure-First Design / Build-Break-Harden Loop mandate**: this bug class (an exception type nothing on a given path is prepared to catch = the client hangs forever with zero response) could recur for any future unrelated bug, in any handler, not just this one. Refactored `serve_data_console.py`'s `do_GET`/`do_POST`/`do_DELETE` into thin wrappers (`_route_get`/`_route_post`/`_route_delete` hold the actual routing logic unchanged) around a new `_dispatch_safely()` helper that catches any `Exception`, logs it with a stable `error_class` (matching this repo's structured-logging convention), and always sends a `500` JSON error response instead of silently dropping the connection. This does not change behavior for any exception type already handled by a specific route (those still return their existing, more specific responses) - it only changes what happens for an exception nothing was watching for, which used to be a silent hang and is now a diagnosable 500.
  - Verification: `python3 -m pytest data_console/tests/ -q` -> 187 passed (184 + 3 new: `test_a_non_utf8_file_falls_back_to_latin_1_instead_of_crashing` in `test_file_store.py`, unit-level; `test_post_upload_of_a_non_utf8_csv_succeeds_instead_of_hanging` in `test_serve_data_console.py`, full HTTP-path regression proving the exact reported bug is fixed end to end; `test_an_unexpected_exception_returns_a_clean_500_instead_of_dropping_the_connection`, proving the new safety net independently of the encoding bug, by injecting an unrelated `RuntimeError` into an unrelated route). Live-reproduced the original crash first (`python3 -c` decode check confirmed a hand-built Latin-1 fixture is not valid UTF-8, matching the real server traceback's byte position/error), then live-verified the fix against the running server via `curl -X POST /api/uploads` with that same fixture - real `200` response with correctly parsed columns, and a clean server log with no traceback this time (previously confirmed the crash via the actual traceback in `/tmp/dc_server.log` from the user's real attempt). Server restarted with both fixes; `/api/mappings` confirmed back to a clean `not_mapped` state.
  - Notes: Did not touch `sheet_mapping_service.py`'s equivalent `.decode("utf-8-sig")` for a fetched Google Sheet - Google always exports Sheets CSVs as UTF-8, so there is no realistic scenario forcing that path to fail the same way, and CLAUDE.md's own guidance is not to add handling for a scenario that can't happen. Local-only; no `git push` run.

- [x] Bug fix (not story-scoped): data-console - mapping-form dropdowns overflowed their card and were visually covered by the next card
  - Date: 2026-09-09
  - Session: CC-20260908-t3x9
  - What changed: The user's own real DataCo Supply Chain dataset has longer column names than the earlier synthetic test fixtures used this session (e.g. `"order date (DateOrders)"`), and reported the mapping form's `<select>` dropdowns rendering cut off, "covered by other box." Root cause: `.mapping-form-row select { flex: 1; }` - a flex item with `flex: 1` defaults to `min-width: auto`, meaning a `<select>` refuses to shrink below the intrinsic width of its longest `<option>` text. With enough real column names, the select overflowed its card's boundary and, since CSS grid siblings paint in DOM order, visually collided with the next card in the same row.
    1. `data_console/serve_data_console.py`'s CSS: `.mapping-form-row select { flex: 1; }` -> `.mapping-form-row select, .mapping-form-row input { flex: 1; min-width: 0; max-width: 100%; margin-bottom: 0; }` - the standard fix for this exact class of flexbox overflow bug (also covers `input`, needed for the new database-connection form's fields - see the next entry below). A native `<select>`'s own text now truncates within the card instead of the box overflowing it.
  - Verification: `python3 -m pytest data_console/tests/ -q` -> 195 passed (pure CSS change, no Python logic touched). Confirmed no other rule depends on the old unconstrained width.
  - Notes: Local-only; no `git push` run.

- [x] New capability (not story-scoped): data-console - "Connect a Database" - enter Postgres credentials directly in the UI, no environment variables or restart required
  - Date: 2026-09-09
  - Session: CC-20260908-t3x9
  - What changed: The user asked for an explicit 3-way choice per dataset ("Postgres Database," "CSV/ZIP file," or "Google Sheet") with the database option offering an in-page form to enter connection details, rather than requiring `SUPPLYMIND_PG_*` environment variables to already be set before the server starts.
    1. `data_console/runtime_db_config.py` (new): an in-memory-only, process-global `PostgresConfig` override (`set_runtime_config()`, `clear_runtime_config()`, `get_postgres_config()`). `get_postgres_config()` returns the override if one has been set this session, otherwise falls back to the existing environment-variable path (`load_postgres_config()`), raising the same `MissingConfigError` when neither is available - every existing "not connected" behavior is unchanged when nobody has ever used this new form. Deliberately **never written to disk and never logged** - the module's own docstring states this explicitly, matching the Security Enforcement Layer's "no secrets in files, no secrets in logs" rule applied to a password typed into a local browser tab instead of read from an env var. Resets on every server restart by design - a local development convenience, not a persisted credential store.
    2. `data_console/schema_inspector.py` and `data_console/mapping_service.py`: every direct `load_postgres_config()` call site switched to `get_postgres_config()`, so table browsing, column listing, custom-query columns, and table/query mapping all transparently pick up a runtime-entered connection the moment one is set - no other call site or route needed to change.
    3. `data_console/serve_data_console.py`: new `POST /api/db-connection` endpoint (`_handle_connect_database`) - validates the submitted fields, then **tests the connection for real** (`fetch_rows("SELECT 1", config=submitted)`, reusing the same shared connector's retry/timeout/error-classing behavior every other Postgres read in this repo already gets) *before* committing it as the session override, so a typo never leaves the tool pointed at a broken connection. On success, returns a redacted summary (host/port/database/user - **the password is never echoed back**). On failure, returns a 400 with the real error and leaves any prior state untouched.

Frontend restructuring: `startMapping()`'s old behavior (always show a search box, disabled with an error banner when not connected, with the CSV/query/Sheets links stacked as easy-to-miss "escape hatches" below) is now a real branch. **Not connected**: `renderSourceChooser()` shows three explicit, equal-weight options - "Connect a Database" (-> `renderDbConnectionForm()`, new host/port/database/user/password fields), "Upload a CSV or ZIP File," "Connect a Google Sheet" - plus the existing "reuse the last uploaded file/zip" quick-picks, extracted into a shared `appendReuseUploadLinks()` helper used by both this and the connected view. **Connected**: `renderTablePicker()` is exactly today's existing search-and-pick-a-table experience (unchanged), still offering the custom-query/upload/Sheets alternatives as peers, since a connected database doesn't rule out mapping one specific dataset from a file instead. - [x] New capability (not story-scoped): data-console - per-required-field "Not available in this dataset" option, so a real dataset missing a required column can still be mapped
  - Date: 2026-09-09
  - Session: CC-20260908-t3x9

The user proposed extending the existing whole-dataset "Not Available in This Database" button down to individual required fields; before building, confirmed with the user how the downstream analysis should behave when a required field is missing this way - chosen: skip just the specific calculation that needs it, with a clear reason, while everything else keeps working. Investigated first (rather than guessing) whether that behavior already exists: `StockoutRiskAgent.run()` and the delivery-delay path already return a clean "flagged for review" `AgentResponse` when a required field is structurally absent from every row, rather than crashing - so the only real gap was that the mapping layer itself refused to *save* a mapping missing a required field at all, blocking the user from ever reaching that already-graceful downstream state.
    1. **`data_integration/connection_profile.py`** (the shared, cross-module validation engine `inventory_risk/`, `risk_detection/`, and every source kind route through): `ConnectionProfile` gained `unavailable_fields: frozenset[str]` - canonical field names explicitly declared genuinely absent, not merely unmapped. `validate_mapping_completeness()` and `validate_against_live_schema()` both exempt a field in this set from the "every required field must have a real column" check.
    2. **`data_console/mapping_validation.py`** (the equivalent shared check for the two non-Postgres source kinds): `validate_column_mapping()` gained the identical `unavailable_fields` parameter and exemption.
    3. **`data_console/mapping_store.py`**: `DatasetMapping` gained `unavailable_fields: list[str]` (persisted to `.mappings.json`, defaults to `[]` so an older saved mapping still loads).
    4. **`data_console/mapping_service.py`, `file_mapping_service.py`, `sheet_mapping_service.py`**: `save_mapping()`, `save_mapping_from_query()`, `save_mapping_from_file()`, `save_mapping_from_sheet()` all gained the parameter, threading it into validation and persistence; `preview_mapping()`/`preview_file_mapping()`/`preview_sheet_mapping()` re-supply the saved `unavailable_fields` on every re-validate, so previewing an already-saved partial mapping doesn't regress to "incomplete."
    5. **`data_console/serve_data_console.py`**: new `_parse_unavailable_fields()` (optional `unavailable_fields: list[str]` in the POST body, defaults to none so an old client is unaffected) wired into all four save routes; `_mapping_status_dict()` now returns `unavailable_fields` in `GET /api/mappings` for a mapped dataset. Frontend: each *required* field's row in `buildMappingFieldForm()` (shared by all four source kinds - table, query, file, sheet) gained a "Not available in this dataset" checkbox that disables/clears that field's dropdown and exempts it from the client-side "please choose a column" block; a mapped dataset card now shows an amber note listing any unavailable fields and stating that analysis needing them will be skipped.
  - Verification: `python3 -m pytest data_console/tests/ data_integration/tests/ inventory_risk/tests/ risk_detection/tests/ agents/tests/ -q` -> 458 passed (7 new tests: 3 in `test_connection_profile.py` for the completeness/live-schema exemption, 1 in `test_mapping_service.py`, 2 in `test_file_mapping_service.py` (save + preview), 2 in `test_serve_data_console.py` for the route-level behavior and its input validation; 3 pre-existing tests updated for the new `unavailable_fields` key in the `GET /api/mappings` response shape). `python3 -c "import ..."` clean across every touched module. Restarted the dev server (also picked up an already-empty `.mappings.json` - the console had no mappings saved when this session resumed, confirmed via `/api/mappings` before touching anything, so nothing of the user's was at risk). Extracted the served page's `<script>` block and ran `node --check` on it (Node already present on this machine, nothing installed) to confirm no JS syntax error. **Live end-to-end proof against the real running server** (not mocked): uploaded a real 2-column CSV (`sku`, `on_hand` - deliberately missing `safety_stock`/`daily_demand_rate`/`lead_time_days`, mirroring the user's actual dataset's gap) via `curl`, then `POST /api/mappings/inventory/from-file` with `unavailable_fields` set to those three - got a real `200 {"saved": true}` (previously would have been a `400 SchemaMappingError`), and `GET /api/mappings` confirmed the mapping persisted correctly with the partial `column_mapping` and the three `unavailable_fields`. Cleared that test mapping and its uploaded file afterward.
  - Notes: Scope was deliberately kept to *making the mapping savable* plus a clear on-card note - did not touch `inventory_risk/`, `risk_detection/`, or any agent, because they were confirmed (by reading `StockoutRiskAgent.run()` directly, not assumed) to already degrade gracefully per-dataset when a required field is structurally missing from every row; this feature's whole job was removing the mapping-layer block standing in front of that already-correct behavior. `sku`/`current_stock` (inventory) and `expected_date`/`actual_date` (delivery_records) are each part of one atomic calculation with no finer internal granularity to "skip partially" - marking any one of a calculation's required fields unavailable effectively skips that whole calculation, which is exactly the behavior the user asked for, not a partial implementation of something bigger. Did not visually verify the new checkbox/note in a real browser (same "don't install anything not required" constraint - no Playwright); verified instead via real HTTP calls against the live server plus `node --check` for JS syntax, which covers what curl alone cannot. - [x] New capability (not story-scoped): data-console - "Compute from another date + a day offset" mapping option for a date field, so a real dataset with a base date plus a lead-time day count (not the target date itself) can still be mapped
  - Date: 2026-09-09
  - Session: CC-20260908-t3x9
  - What changed: The user's real DataCo CSV has no `expected_date`/`actual_date` columns for `delivery_records` - instead it has `order date (DateOrders)` (a real calendar date) plus `Days for shipment (scheduled)` and `Days for shipping (real)` (day counts). Asked the user whether to (1) derive real dates as `order_date + day_count`, preserving full fidelity but needing a new "computed field" mapping concept, or (2) teach the delay detector a duration-only mode that loses real dates in its output. User chose (1).
    1. **`data_console/column_requirements.py`**: `ColumnRequirement` gained `is_date: bool = False`, set `True` for `order_date`, `expected_date`, `actual_date` - the three canonical fields whose value is a calendar date, so the mapping UI knows which fields can offer this option (not a naming-convention guess).
    2. **`data_integration/connection_profile.py`** (the shared engine): `ConnectionProfile` gained `computed_date_fields: dict[str, dict[str, str]]` (canonical field -> `{base_date_column, offset_days_column}`). `validate_mapping_completeness()`/`validate_against_live_schema()` exempt a field listed here from needing a direct `column_mapping` entry, checking instead that both referenced real columns exist. New pure `compute_date_fields()` derives each row's value as `parse_delivery_date(base) + timedelta(days=offset)` - **reuses `risk_detection.anomaly_detection.parse_delivery_date` rather than re-implementing date parsing**, honoring that function's own docstring, which explicitly discourages a second copy (two disagreeing notions of "which dates parse" would silently diverge downstream); a row whose base date doesn't parse or whose offset isn't numeric gets `None` for that field - the same "flag it, don't crash" contract `remap_rows()` already documents for a missing mapped column. New `remap_and_compute_rows()` merges `remap_rows()` + `compute_date_fields()` per row - the one combined transform every source kind's fetch/preview path now calls (`fetch_profile_data()`, `file_mapping_service.preview_file_mapping()`, `sheet_mapping_service.preview_sheet_mapping()`), replacing their direct `remap_rows()` calls.
    3. **`data_console/mapping_validation.py`, `mapping_store.py`, `mapping_service.py`, `file_mapping_service.py`, `sheet_mapping_service.py`**: the identical `computed_date_fields` parameter/exemption/persistence threaded through every save/preview path for all four source kinds (table, query, file, sheet), the same pattern the prior `unavailable_fields` slice established.
    4. **`data_console/serve_data_console.py`**: new `_parse_computed_date_fields()` (validates each entry has non-empty string `base_date_column`/`offset_days_column`, nothing else) wired into all four save routes; `/api/datasets` now reports each field's `is_date`; `GET /api/mappings` now reports `computed_date_fields` for a mapped dataset. Frontend: each *required* **date** field's row in `buildMappingFieldForm()` gained a second checkbox, "Compute from another date + a day offset," mutually exclusive with the existing "Not available" checkbox - checking it reveals a nested row with two column pickers (base date, day offset) and disables the direct-column select; the client-side completeness check now also accepts "compute checked with both pickers filled" as satisfying a required field. A mapped card shows a new note (`fieldName = "base" + "offset" day(s)`) when any field was computed this way.
  - Verification: `python3 -m pytest` (whole repo) -> 754 passed (14 new tests: 9 in `test_connection_profile.py` covering completeness/live-schema exemption, `compute_date_fields()`'s happy path and its two graceful-None paths - unparseable base date, non-numeric offset - and `remap_and_compute_rows()`'s merge behavior including the empty-computed-fields passthrough; 3 in `test_file_mapping_service.py` for save/reject/preview against the exact real-world shape (an order date plus a scheduled-days column); 2 in `test_serve_data_console.py` for the route-level save-and-retrieve and its input validation). `python3 -c "import ..."` clean across every touched module. Restarted the dev server; `GET /api/mappings` before and after confirmed the user's own real, already-saved `customer_orders` and `inventory` mappings (against their actual `DataCoSupplyChainDataset.csv`) survived untouched. Extracted the served page's `<script>` and ran `node --check` (Node already present, nothing installed) - clean. **Live end-to-end proof against the real running server, using the user's actual uploaded file**: `POST /api/mappings/delivery_records/from-file` with `computed_date_fields` set to `expected_date = "order date (DateOrders)" + "Days for shipment (scheduled)"` and `actual_date = "order date (DateOrders)" + "Days for shipping (real)"` - real `200 {"saved": true}`, confirmed persisted via `GET /api/mappings`. `GET /api/mappings/delivery_records/preview` against the real 500-row sample returned real rows with the new `expected_date`/`actual_date` keys present (proving the merge into each remapped row works) - but every value was `None`.
  - Notes: **Root-caused the `None` values rather than reporting a false success**: this file's `order date (DateOrders)` values are `"1/31/2018 22:56"` (M/D/YYYY H:MM), and `parse_delivery_date()` only accepts `"YYYY-MM-DD"` strings or real `date`/`datetime` objects - confirmed directly (`parse_delivery_date('1/31/2018 22:56')` -> `None`, `parse_delivery_date('2026-01-05')` -> a real date) rather than assumed. This is `compute_date_fields()` behaving exactly per its documented "flag it, don't crash" contract - not a bug in this slice - but it means **the feature just built cannot yet produce real dates from this specific file** until `parse_delivery_date()` also accepts this format. That function is shared beyond this slice (`risk_detection/anomaly_detection.py`'s own delay detection, `supplier_evaluation/reliability.py`), so widening its accepted formats is a separate, explicitly out-of-scope decision for the user, not something to fold in silently - flagged to the user directly rather than expanding scope unilaterally (per this repo's Scope Lock rule). Cleared the test `delivery_records` mapping afterward rather than leaving an all-`None` mapping active in the user's live console. Local-only; no `git push` run.

- [x] New capability (not story-scoped): dashboard - `run_sample_dashboard.py`'s "real_data" scenario now sources each dataset from data_console's saved mapping when one exists, instead of only its own hardcoded Postgres queries
  - Date: 2026-09-10
  - Session: CC-20260908-t3x9
  - What changed: The user asked what happens after finishing all three data_console mappings. Investigated rather than assumed: `dashboard/run_sample_dashboard.py`'s "real_data" scenario pulled via three hardcoded `PostgresDataset` queries (`SELECT * FROM customer_orders LIMIT 500`, etc.) through `data_integration/orchestrator.py`'s generic multi-source puller - a completely separate system from `data_console`'s `MappingStore`/`ConnectionProfile`, which nothing outside `data_console` referenced at all (confirmed via a repo-wide grep). So finishing a mapping in data_console had no effect on the dashboard. User confirmed wiring the two together was the right next step.
    1. **`data_integration/orchestrator.py`**: `SourceType` widened to add `"csv_upload"` (additive - `DatasetResult.source_type`/`DataFreshnessEntry.source_type` are both display-only strings nothing pattern-matches exhaustively over, confirmed by reading every consumer).
    2. **`dashboard/run_sample_dashboard.py`**: new `_dataset_result_from_data_console(dataset_kind)` - checks `data_console.mapping_store.MappingStore` for a saved mapping and, if one exists, sources that dataset via the same `preview_mapping()` the console's own "Preview" button already calls (same `PREVIEW_ROW_LIMIT=500` cap this module's own hardcoded queries already use - not a new concept). Returns `None` (not mapped at all) so the caller falls back to the legacy hardcoded `PostgresDataset` pull for that one dataset - a tenant who has mapped some but not all 3 datasets through data_console gets a real per-dataset mix, matching data_console's own existing "mixed sources" capability.   - Date: 2026-09-10

  - Date: 2026-09-10

A missing file (dashboard never generated) gets a plain-English 404 page telling the user to run `python3 -m dashboard.run_sample_dashboard` first, not a raw error.
