    2. **`dashboard/run_sample_dashboard.py`**: `_nav_links()` now prepends a "Data Console" entry pointing at `http://127.0.0.1:<port>/` - reads `SUPPLYMIND_DATA_CONSOLE_PORT` the same way `data_console.serve_data_console.main()` does, so the link still resolves if the console wasn't started on its default port 8766.
    3. **Deliberately no import between the two modules in either direction**: `dashboard/run_sample_dashboard.py` already imports from `data_console` (`MappingStore`, `preview_mapping`, ...), so `data_console` importing `dashboard` back would be the same live "A imports B imports A" cycle this repo's Modular Composition Rule forbids, named explicitly in two earlier entries above (`forecasting/aggregation.py`'s date-format duplication, the render.py CSS reuse). `serve_data_console.py` only imports `DEFAULT_PORT` reading logic already inline in its own module and reaches the dashboard's HTML output via a plain filesystem path (`Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent / "dashboard"`), never a Python import of `dashboard`'s code. The scenario/label pair is intentionally duplicated (not imported) from `dashboard/run_sample_dashboard.py`'s own `_SCENARIO_LABELS`, for the same reason.
  - Verification: `python3 -m pytest` (whole repo) -> 780 passed (1 new test in `dashboard/tests/test_run_sample_dashboard.py` confirming `_nav_links()` leads with a "Data Console" entry pointing at the right port before the 3 scenario entries; 5 new HTTP-level tests in `data_console/tests/test_serve_data_console.py` against the module's existing route test harness - the served root page links to all 3 dashboard pages; `/dashboard/<filename>` serves the file when present; `/dashboard/` defaults to `control_tower_real_data.html`; a not-yet-generated dashboard returns a 404 naming the `run_sample_dashboard` command to fix it; and a path-traversal attempt (`/dashboard/../<filename>`) is rejected with 404 by the allowlist check before any filesystem access, proving the path-safety design claim rather than just asserting it in a comment). `python3 -c "import data_console.serve_data_console; import dashboard.run_sample_dashboard"` clean, confirming no import cycle actually got introduced. **Verified live against the real running server**: found this feature already written but uncommitted from before the session's context was compacted (see the render.py entry above for the same discovery) - reviewed the diff in full, then restarted the actual dev server (`kill` the old process, relaunch `python3 -m data_console.serve_data_console`) to pick up the new route; confirmed via `GET /api/mappings` before and after that the user's 3 real mappings survived the restart untouched. Regenerated the dashboard pages (`python3 -m dashboard.run_sample_dashboard`) so their own nav bars pick up the new "Data Console" link. Then, against the live server: `GET /dashboard/control_tower_real_data.html` -> 200 with the real page; `GET /dashboard/` -> 200 (defaults to real_data); `GET /dashboard/does_not_exist.html` -> 404 (allowlist rejects it, confirming the path-safety claim above isn't just asserted in a comment); `GET /` shows Data Console's own new nav bar linking to all 3 dashboard pages.   - What changed: User asked whether the mapping for their real upload (`~/Downloads/DataCoSupplyChainDataset.csv`, the DataCo Supply Chain retail-order dataset) was actually correct, plus 4 concrete UI fixes.

Re-marked `current_stock` and `lead_time_days` (previously wrongly mapped to "Days for shipment (scheduled)", the customer-shipping SLA, not a supplier reorder lead time) as `unavailable_fields`, alongside the already-correctly-unavailable `safety_stock`/`daily_demand_rate`. `delivery_records` was already sound: `expected_date`/`actual_date` computed correctly from "order date (DateOrders)" + "Days for shipment (scheduled)"/"Days for shipping (real)" (verified the day-offset arithmetic against 10 real rows by hand); `po_id` -> "Order Id" is a reasonable proxy (DataCo has no real purchase-order concept - it's a B2C retail export - but Order Id is a stable per-order key, which is what the delay-scoring logic actually needs) - flagged as a semantic caveat, not a bug.
    2. **`data_console/serve_data_console.py`**: `loadTables()`'s disconnected-state view (the bottom-of-page "Browse Tables" panel) had its own standalone "Connect a Database" button, redundant with the one already in the "Connect Your Data" section higher up the same page - removed the button, kept the explanatory placeholder text pointing up at the section above instead.
    3. **`dashboard/render.py`**: `.explore-panel .chart-card` had no width cap (unlike the quick-review grid's cards, which are bounded by `.charts-grid`'s `minmax(320px, 1fr)`), so the single visible Explore chart stretched to the full 1180px page width and scaled its bars/text up with it - added `max-width: 560px` to match a quick-review card's width.
    4. **`dashboard/render.py`**: new `_render_headline_lines()`/`_highlight_headline_segment()` replace the old single-line `<div class="tile-headline">{_esc(_condense_headline(...))}</div>`. The condensed headline's segments (already split on "; "/" | " by `_condense_headline`) now each render as their own `<div class="headline-line">`, and within each line, numeric/currency/percentage values are wrapped in `<span class="hl-value">` (brand-blue, bold) and severity words (critical/high/medium/low) in `<span class="hl-severity">` colored via the existing `_SEVERITY_COLORS` map - both applied only after `_esc()` has already escaped the segment, so highlighting can never reintroduce markup. The value regex keeps a fraction ("65/100") or hyphenated date ("2025-07") as one token rather than splitting at the "-"/"/", and strips a trailing list-separator comma back outside the span (so "1360, 1360" doesn't highlight "1360," with the separator trapped inside).
  - Verification: `python3 -m pytest` (whole repo) -> 804 passed (5 new tests in `dashboard/tests/test_render.py`: each headline segment gets its own line, numbers/severity words are color-coded, highlighting cannot reintroduce markup even from a malicious headline, a list-separator comma stays outside the value span; 3 pre-existing tests updated to assert against the new color-coded markup instead of the old plain-text substring, since wrapping "65/100" in a span means that exact substring no longer appears verbatim - confirmed the new assertions against real rendered output before committing to them, not guessed). `python3 -m pytest data_console/tests/` -> 210 passed unchanged. **Regenerated the real dashboard** (`python3 -m dashboard.run_sample_dashboard`, exit 0) and confirmed against the user's actual live data: the Stockout Risk tile's Show Details now reads "missing field(s): current_stock, safety_stock, daily_demand_rate, lead_time_days" (previously only 2 of these 4 showed as missing, because 2 were wrongly mapped instead of honestly marked unavailable) with each SKU example correctly showing a real Product Card Id (e.g. 1360) instead of the shared category code (73); explore-panel chart width capped; headline lines and color-coding present (21 headline-line divs, 86 value spans, 2 severity spans across the real page). Restarted the live `data_console` server (`kill` + relaunch `python3 -m data_console.serve_data_console`) so it picks up the button removal, then confirmed via `curl` that only 1 "Connect a Database" entry point remains reachable from a normal page load (the top Connect-Your-Data option card); the other 2 remaining string matches in source are a code comment and a per-dataset contextual button (`renderSourceChooser`, only shown when mapping an individual not-yet-connected dataset) that were never the reported duplicate. `GET /api/mappings` confirmed the corrected inventory mapping persisted through the restart.
  - Notes: The deeper finding, not just a mapping-config bug: DataCoSupplyChainDataset.csv is a retail order/transaction export, not an inventory-on-hand snapshot - no remapping of its existing columns can produce true `current_stock`/`safety_stock`/`daily_demand_rate`/`lead_time_days` values, because the underlying data those fields need doesn't exist in this file at all. With the mapping now honest about that, the Stockout Risk tile will keep reporting "insufficient data" for effectively all rows against this file - that's the correct behavior given the data, not a regression. A separate, genuinely inventory-shaped file (or a supplemented sheet with those 4 columns added) would be needed to exercise that agent meaningfully. - [x] Rebuilt the stockout-risk engine to match the product's own detailed spec (Phase 1, part 1 of 4: Stockout Risk / Root Cause / Recommendations / What-If)
  - Date: 2026-09-11
  - Session: CC-20260908-t3x9
  - What changed: User handed over a detailed 12-feature product vision doc plus an explicit stockout-risk formula spec (ADD, days-of-supply, expected-inventory-before-replenishment, day-based Critical/High/Medium/Low thresholds, Z-score safety stock, stockout probability, revenue-at-risk, a weighted 0-100 composite score) and said the app felt "very basic." A background audit (Explore agent) found nearly all 12 features already have real backend modules (`root_cause/`, `scenario_simulation/`, `chat_interface/`, `recommendation/`, `supplier_evaluation/`, `shipment_delay_analysis/`, `inventory_risk/`) - the gap is mostly UI exposure, plus 2 places the actual computation was simpler than the new spec: the stockout model (a lead-time-relative coverage ratio, not day-based/statistical) and the AI Assistant (keyword router, no LLM). User picked, via AskUserQuestion: Phase 1 = Stockout Risk + Root Cause + Recommendations + What-If, made excellent first; AI Assistant = expand the deterministic router rather than add a paid LLM (a Strategic Decision under this repo's Autonomy Model that needed the user's own sign-off, not a unilateral pick). This entry covers the first quarter of that phase.
    1. **`inventory_risk/risk_model.py`** (full rewrite, deliberate breaking change to the internal classification logic - coordinated across every consumer in this same change, per CLAUDE.md's "renaming/restructuring a public type requires... a single coordinated PR"): risk levels are now day-based (`CRITICAL_DAYS_THRESHOLD=7`, `HIGH_DAYS_THRESHOLD=14`, `MEDIUM_DAYS_THRESHOLD=30`, all named module constants so a business can tune them, matching the spec's own "configurable, not universal" instruction) instead of the old `days_of_supply / lead_time_days` coverage ratio. `InventoryPosition` gained 3 optional fields (`incoming_stock`, `unit_price`, `demand_std_dev`) that each unlock one new capability without breaking any existing caller supplying only the original 5 fields. New pure functions: `compute_average_daily_demand` (ADD), `compute_recommended_safety_stock` (Z x demand-std-dev x sqrt(lead time) - verified against the spec's own worked example: Z=1.65, std=10, lead=9 -> 49.5), `estimate_stockout_probability` (a normal-CDF approximation via `math.erf` when `demand_std_dev` is known, else a smooth logistic fallback on coverage ratio - explicitly documented as a statistical formula standing in for a future trained model, not a prediction from one, matching the spec's own "the ML model can later improve the prediction"), `compute_revenue_at_risk` (shortage units x unit price), and `compute_risk_score` (the 0-100 composite; the 2 optional supplier/incoming-shipment signals the spec names aren't wired at the data layer yet - no SKU-to-supplier link or incoming-PO source exists anywhere in this repo - so the weights renormalize proportionally over whichever signals are actually supplied rather than defaulting a missing one to a false neutral midpoint). `StockoutRiskAssessment` gained `expected_inventory`, `stockout_probability`, `risk_score`, `revenue_at_risk`, `recommended_safety_stock` fields.
    2. **`inventory_risk/data_quality.py`**: new `OPTIONAL_NUMERIC_FIELDS` (`incoming_stock`, `unit_price`, `demand_std_dev`) and `_normalize_optional_fields()` - present-and-valid gets normalized to float (same Decimal-from-Postgres tolerance the 5 required fields already have); present-but-invalid (wrong type, negative, NaN) is dropped silently rather than flagging the whole row, since these are enhancements the core assessment doesn't depend on - a bad optional value shouldn't cost a SKU its risk assessment.
    3. **`agents/stockout_risk_agent.py`**: now passes through whichever `OPTIONAL_NUMERIC_FIELDS` are present on a row into `InventoryPosition`, not just the 5 required fields - previously these would have been silently dropped even if a real mapped source supplied them.
    4. **`data_console/column_requirements.py`**: `INVENTORY` dataset gained the same 3 fields as its own optional columns (previously had none), so a real connected source can actually supply `unit_price` etc. through the existing mapping UI.
    5. **`scenario_simulation/simulation.py`**: no code change needed - it already calls `assess_stockout_risk()` generically and inherits the new day-based classification automatically.
    6. **`dashboard/run_sample_dashboard.py`**: bumped `SYNTHETIC_INVENTORY_ROWS`' stock levels (300->400, 450->600) - both SKUs sat at exactly the old coverage-ratio "well past 1.5x lead time" mark, which happened to land exactly on the new 30-day boundary (still classified "medium", not the demo's intended "low") - confirmed by regenerating the real page and seeing "medium" before this fix, "low" after.
  - Verification: `python3 -m pytest` (whole repo) -> 844 passed. New: 49 tests in `inventory_risk/tests/test_risk_model.py` (every day-based boundary condition worked through by hand with real numbers before writing the assertion, not guessed - including the specific case where days-of-supply alone would read one level but the expected-inventory-negative or below-safety-stock-at-lead-time-end condition overrides it to a worse one; the safety-stock formula against the spec's own 49.5 worked example; the stockout-probability logistic's center-at-coverage-1.0 property; the composite score's full-weights-sum-to-100 property and its "adding an extra risky signal can only raise the score, never leave it flat" property), 5 new tests in `inventory_risk/tests/test_data_quality.py` (valid optional fields normalized and kept; a row with none present is untouched; an invalid optional value is dropped from the row entirely rather than left in place as the raw bad value - this was an actual bug caught while writing the test, see Notes; a negative optional value dropped), 2 new tests in `agents/tests/test_stockout_risk_agent.py` (a `unit_price` on the row reaches `revenue_at_risk` in the finding's detail text; a `Decimal` optional field from a simulated real database row doesn't crash). 11 pre-existing tests across `inventory_risk/tests/test_risk_model.py`, `scenario_simulation/tests/test_simulation.py`, `agents/tests/test_scenario_simulation_agent.py`, and `risk_detection/tests/test_risk_score.py` updated for the new thresholds/constructor shape - every updated expectation was recomputed by hand against the new formulas (e.g. "300% demand spike" scenario: baseline 100/5=20 days is now "medium" not "low", projected 100/20=5 days is "critical" not "high") and one test's entire premise (`test_stock_and_safety_stock_deltas_combine_to_cross_the_critical_threshold`) was replaced rather than patched, since under the new day-based rule the original two-delta interaction it demonstrated no longer required both deltas to reach its asserted outcome - a fresh interaction (lead-time + safety-stock combining to cross into "high") was found and verified instead of leaving a test that passed for the wrong reason. **Regenerated all 3 dashboard scenarios** (`python3 -m dashboard.run_sample_dashboard`, exit 0) and confirmed the richer detail text (expected inventory, stockout probability, revenue at risk when priced) flows all the way through to the real rendered page's color-coded, multi-line Show Details section from an earlier entry, with no wiring changes needed there.
  - Notes: **Real bug caught by test-writing, not by inspection**: the first draft of `_normalize_optional_fields`'s integration merged `{**row, **normalized}` - a field the normalizer decided to *drop* (invalid type/negative/NaN) was never removed from `row` itself, so the raw bad value silently survived into `clean_rows` instead of actually being absent. Caught while writing `test_an_invalid_optional_field_is_dropped_not_left_as_the_raw_bad_value`, fixed by building the base dict with `OPTIONAL_NUMERIC_FIELDS` explicitly excluded before merging the normalized ones back in. **What's deliberately not done yet**: `compute_risk_score`'s supplier-lead-time-risk and incoming-shipment-risk components have no data source wired to them anywhere in this repo (no SKU->supplier link, no incoming-PO table/mapping) - the function accepts them as optional parameters and renormalizes without them for now; wiring real values in is follow-up work, likely alongside the Root Cause chain (part 2 of this phase), which will need to correlate stockout risk against supplier delay data by SKU/supplier for the same reason. **Next in this phase**: Root Cause causal-chain rebuild, Recommendation restructuring into Problem/Recommendation/Reason/Confidence, and a real What-If Simulator screen (backend already solid, no UI yet) - not started in this entry. Local-only; no `git commit`/`git push` run per the user's 2026-09-10 instruction.

- [x] Root Cause Analysis now builds a real multi-step causal chain, not just independent candidate causes (Phase 1, part 2 of 4)
  - Date: 2026-09-11
  - Session: CC-20260908-t3x9
  - What changed: The product spec's root-cause example is a chain ("Demand up -> Supplier delay up -> Inventory down -> Stockout risk up"), but `root_cause/analysis.py`'s existing `analyze_root_cause()` only ever produced up to 3 *independent* candidate causes for one issue - a genuinely good design for "what could explain this," but not a linked story. Rather than rewrite that (it's correct on its own terms and other things may depend on its exact shape), added a chain-building layer on top of it.
    1. **`root_cause/analysis.py`**: new `CausalStep`/`RootCauseChain` dataclasses, `build_causal_chain(analysis, demand_anomalies, supplier_delays)` and `describe_chain(chain)`. Purely additive - `analyze_root_cause()` itself is unchanged. `build_causal_chain` takes an already-computed `RootCauseAnalysis` (never re-derives one) and, when the strongest candidate is a `supplier_delay`, looks for a `demand_spike` whose period plausibly *precedes* that delay's `expected_date` (`_period_plausibly_precedes()`: same month through `MAX_ANTECEDENT_MONTHS=2` months earlier, a named/configurable constant, not a hardcoded universal rule) - prepending it as an earlier step only when a real temporal link supports it. With no such link (or no `demand_anomalies` supplied), the chain degrades to the single strongest candidate - still a fully valid, honest answer, not a failure. `describe_chain()` renders the exact narrative shape the product spec asks for: `"Demand ↑ (2024-12) → Supplier delay ↑ (PO-1) → Stockout risk ↑ (SKU-1)"`.
    2. **`agents/root_cause_agent.py`**: `run()` now calls `build_causal_chain()` using the same `demand_anomalies`/`supplier_delays` it already derived for the evaluator (no redundant recomputation), and `_format_recommendation()` uses `describe_chain()` for the headline instead of the raw `cause` identifier string. New `_chain_findings()` turns every non-terminal causal step into an `AgentFinding` (subject/subject_kind/severity/detail) - the terminal "issue" step is excluded since it describes the thing being investigated, not a contributing cause - so `dashboard/charts.py`'s existing per-subject-kind chart machinery can surface a causal chain's individual links with zero changes needed there, the same "a future agent's findings work for free" guarantee that module's own docstring already claims.
  - Verification: `python3 -m pytest` (whole repo) -> 853 passed. New: 7 tests in `root_cause/tests/test_analysis.py` (a demand spike 1 month before a delay chains correctly; a spike 12 months before does not; a spike *after* the delay does not - direction matters, not just proximity; a demand-spike-only candidate is a 2-step chain; no candidates at all is a 1-step "issue only" chain with 0 confidence; `describe_chain`'s exact rendered string matches the product spec's own example shape; a non-SKU issue (`subject_kind="supplier"`) gets a "Supplier risk ↑" label, not a hardcoded "Stockout risk ↑"). 2 new tests in `agents/tests/test_root_cause_agent.py` (findings contain one entry per causal step, not the terminal issue step; a delay is correctly NOT chained to a demand spike that comes *after* it, proving the direction check is wired end-to-end through the agent, not just the pure function). 2 pre-existing agent tests updated from asserting a raw cause-identifier substring (`"demand_spike"`/`"supplier_delay"`) to the new narrative text (`"Demand ↑"`/`"Supplier delay ↑"`), since the recommendation text is now the human-readable chain, not the internal identifier.
  - Notes: The chain-linking is currently supplier_delay <- demand_spike only, matching the product spec's own named example exactly; a `supplier_reliability` candidate isn't chained further backward (its own aggregate score doesn't have an antecedent-worthy single date to compare against) - it still produces a valid 1-step chain on its own. Local-only; no `git commit`/`git push` run per the user's 2026-09-10 instruction.

- [x] Added a deterministic Problem/Recommendation/Reason/Confidence stockout playbook (Phase 1, part 3 of 4)
  - Date: 2026-09-11
  - Session: CC-20260908-t3x9
  - What changed: The product spec's own worked example ("Problem: Product A likely to stock out in 8 days. Recommendation: Replenish within 3 days. Reason: ... Confidence: 91%") is a fixed, structured 4-field shape - existing `recommendation/synthesis.py` produces a different, still-useful thing (one free-text `Recommendation(agent_name, text, confidence)` per agent, plus cross-agent conflict detection) that doesn't fit this shape and wasn't touched, since restructuring it would break its own real job (combining N agents' outputs) for no benefit here.
    1. **`recommendation/stockout_playbook.py`** (new module, sibling to synthesis.py, not a replacement): `StructuredRecommendation(problem, recommendation, reason, confidence)` and `build_stockout_recommendation(assessment, has_supplier_delay=False, demand_trend=None)` - a fixed, ordered rule table implementing the spec's own named rules exactly, first match wins (not a scored/blended decision, matching the spec's explicit "the AI should not invent the action"): (1) critical + no incoming stock -> immediate replenishment review, (2) critical + incoming stock already on the way -> expedite that shipment instead (a sensible variant the spec's own "no incoming stock" qualifier implies), (3) high + a known supplier delay -> review an alternative supplier/incoming shipment, (4) high-or-critical + increasing demand trend -> expedited replenishment, (5) high alone -> review the reorder point, (6) medium -> monitor, (7) low -> no action required. `describe_recommendation()` renders the exact "Problem: ... | Recommendation: ... | Reason: ... | Confidence: NN%" line shape from the spec's own example. `has_supplier_delay`/`demand_trend` are optional inputs this module doesn't compute itself - a caller wires them in from the same anomaly signals root_cause/analysis.py already consumes, keeping this a pure decision table over already-computed facts.
    2. **`inventory_risk/risk_model.py`**: `StockoutRiskAssessment` gained an `incoming_stock` field (carried forward from the input `InventoryPosition`) - needed so `build_stockout_recommendation` can tell "nothing incoming" (rule 1) from "already ordered, just not enough or not fast enough" (rule 2) from the assessment alone, without also needing the original `InventoryPosition` passed around separately.
  - Verification: `python3 -m pytest` (whole repo) -> 864 passed. New: 11 tests in `recommendation/tests/test_stockout_playbook.py` (each of the 7 rules fires on the exact condition it's supposed to, verified against real `assess_stockout_risk()` output rather than a hand-built assessment; a case where two rules' conditions are BOTH true confirms the documented priority order actually wins, not whichever was checked last; the problem statement includes revenue-at-risk only when it's actually nonzero; the rendered line matches the spec's own example shape). 1 pre-existing test (`risk_detection/tests/test_risk_score.py`'s `_stockout()` fixture) updated for the new required field.
  - Notes: **Not wired into an agent's live pipeline in this entry, on purpose**: `StockoutRiskAgent` only ever receives `inventory_rows`, which carry no supplier field and no link to demand history - the same "no SKU-to-supplier link exists anywhere in this data model" gap already flagged in this phase's first entry (the composite risk score's supplier/incoming-shipment components). Wiring `has_supplier_delay`/`demand_trend` into a real per-SKU agent call would mean fabricating a linkage the data doesn't actually support, so `build_stockout_recommendation` ships today as a standalone, fully-tested function a caller with real per-SKU supplier/demand context (e.g. a future per-SKU "why is this at risk" panel, or root_cause_agent's own issue-investigation flow, which does have that context) can call directly. Local-only; no `git commit`/`git push` run per the user's 2026-09-10 instruction.

- [x] What-If Simulator now has a real browser screen, not just a CLI demo (Phase 1, part 4 of 4 - completes the phase)
  - Date: 2026-09-11
  - Session: CC-20260908-t3x9
  - What changed: `scenario_simulation/simulation.py`'s `simulate_scenario()` was already solid (per the earlier gap-analysis audit) but only reachable via `run_sample_scenario_simulation.py` printing JSON to a terminal - the product spec names a "Scenario Simulator" as one of its 6 main screens.
    1. **`scenario_simulation/serve_scenario_simulator.py`** (new module, same dependency-free stdlib `http.server` pattern as `chat_interface/serve_chat_ui.py` and `data_console/serve_data_console.py` - no new package): a form for a baseline inventory position (current stock, safety stock, daily demand rate, lead time, plus the optional incoming-stock/unit-price/demand-std-dev fields from this phase's first entry) and 4 "what if" deltas (demand %, lead-time days, safety-stock units, stock-on-hand units), posting to `POST /api/simulate`. The response renders baseline vs. projected side by side - risk level, days of supply, stockout probability, the 0-100 composite risk score, revenue at risk when priced - plus, tying this phase's 3 other pieces together in one screen, the projected position's own `recommendation/stockout_playbook.py` recommendation line. Fixed by passing them through from `scenario.baseline` in `_apply_deltas()`.

Retrofitting every app to cross-link consistently is exactly the "unify into one shell" option the user did not pick as this phase's priority - flagging it as a natural candidate for that follow-up phase rather than doing a partial version of it here. **This completes Phase 1** (Stockout Risk / Root Cause / Recommendations / What-If) as scoped after the gap-analysis audit. - [x] Expanded the deterministic chat router for per-entity "why is X at risk?" questions; wired the stockout playbook into the live agent (Phase 2, part 1)
  - Date: 2026-09-11
  - Session: CC-20260908-t3x9
  - What changed: With Phase 1 complete, user said to continue. The AI Assistant approach the user picked earlier via AskUserQuestion - "expand the deterministic router" rather than add a paid LLM (a Strategic Decision this repo's Autonomy Model reserves for the user's own sign-off) - was the one Phase-1-adjacent item not yet started, and it directly leverages Phase 1's new per-SKU stockout detail/recommendation text, so it was the natural next step.
    1. **`chat_interface/router.py`**: new pure function `find_mentioned_subject(query_text, subjects)` - given the real subject strings (SKUs/POs/suppliers/periods) already present in a snapshot's findings, returns the one subject named in the query, or None. Word-boundary-anchored regex match (not `in`), case-insensitive, so "SKU-1" cannot spuriously match inside "SKU-10". When more than one subject matches, the *longest* wins (same "more specific keyword wins" rule `classify_query()` already applies) - needed because a shorter subject can be a genuine substring of a longer one at a real word boundary (e.g. "Acme" inside "Acme Freight"); a genuine tie between two *different*-named subjects of the same length still returns None (an honest "can't tell which you mean," matching `classify_query()`'s own cross-topic-tie behavior), not a guess.
    2. **`chat_interface/chat.py`**: `answer_query()` now checks `find_mentioned_subject()` against every finding subject in the snapshot *before* falling back to `classify_query()`'s per-topic routing - a query naming a specific entity ("why is SKU-1 at risk?") gets a genuinely more specific answer (every metric's finding for that subject, e.g. both the stockout-risk assessment and a root-cause causal-chain step naming the same SKU) than a generic per-topic headline, even when the query also happens to contain a topic keyword like "risk". New `ChatResponse.subject` field (mutually exclusive with `topic`) identifies which entity answered.
    3. **`agents/stockout_risk_agent.py`**: each per-SKU `AgentFinding.detail` now ends with a real `build_stockout_recommendation()` line (`recommendation/stockout_playbook.py`, built in Phase 1 part 3 but explicitly left unwired from any live agent) - e.g. "... stockout probability 0% Recommendation: No action required." Called with no `has_supplier_delay`/`demand_trend` context (this agent still has no SKU-to-supplier or demand-history link - the same gap flagged in Phase 1's first two entries), so only the context-free rules (critical/high/medium/low, with-or-without incoming stock) can fire here; a caller with real supplier/demand context can still call the playbook directly for the sharper answer.
    4. **`chat_interface/serve_chat_ui.py`**: `subject` added to the `/api/chat` JSON response and the transcript's meta-line ("| about: SKU-1"); input placeholder updated to mention per-SKU/PO questions are supported.
  - Verification: `python3 -m pytest` (whole repo) -> 888 passed (up from 875). New: 8 tests in `chat_interface/tests/test_router.py` (a named subject matches; case-insensitive; a longer subject like "SKU-10" is not spuriously matched by a shorter "SKU-1" pattern and vice versa; no subject named returns None; blank query returns None; empty subject set returns None; the longer-subject-wins rule when one subject is a genuine substring of another at a word boundary; a genuine tie between two different subjects returns None). 3 new tests in `chat_interface/tests/test_chat.py` (an entity query pulls findings from every metric that has one for that subject, not just one topic; a named subject takes priority over a topic keyword also present in the same query; a query naming no subject still falls back to topic routing unchanged). 1 new test in `agents/tests/test_stockout_risk_agent.py` (a per-SKU finding's detail carries a real "Recommendation:" line matching the assessed risk level). 1 new end-to-end test in `chat_interface/tests/test_serve_chat_ui.py` (a real HTTP POST with a named-SKU query returns `subject` set and `topic` null). **Verified live** against `chat_interface.run_sample_chat_interface.build_healthy_snapshot()`'s real computed snapshot (not a hand-built fixture): "why is 2025-04 at risk?" correctly pulled the risk-detection agent's real anomaly finding for that period; "why is SKU-100 at risk?" pulled both agents' independent findings for that SKU, with the stockout-risk agent's line now ending "Recommendation: No action required." confirming the playbook wiring reached all the way through a real orchestrated run, not just the unit tests.
  - Notes: `risk_detection_agent`'s own per-SKU findings (computed independently from `stockout_risk_agent`'s, by design, to support STORY-006's cross-agent-disagreement detection) do *not* get the new recommendation line, since they're built from the raw `StockoutRiskAssessment.detail` directly rather than through `stockout_risk_agent.py`'s wrapper - an entity answer can show one agent's line with a recommendation and the other's without, which is correct (two independent agents' views), not a bug. Not done in this entry, flagged as follow-up: Executive Dashboard KPI aggregation (revenue-at-risk/overstock/orders-pending rollups), and unifying the 4 separate mini-apps into one cross-linked shell (still the "unify" option not picked in Phase 1). Local-only; no `git commit`/`git push` run per the user's 2026-09-10 instruction.

- [x] Fixed broken nav-bar tabs between the 4 local apps; added a single-command launcher (Phase 2, part 2)

  - Session: CC-20260908-t3x9
  - What changed: User reported (with a screenshot of the What-If Simulator page) that clicking the other nav-bar tabs ("AI Assistant", etc.) didn't work. Root-caused live: only 2 of the 3 local server processes (data_console:8766, scenario_simulation:8767) were actually running - `chat_interface`'s server (8765) was not - so its nav-bar link failed with a connection error, not an application bug. This is exactly the "4 separate mini-apps, none of them consistently cross-linked, each needs its own manual `python3 -m ...` in its own terminal" gap flagged as follow-up in the last 2 PROGRESS.md entries, now a concrete, reported usability problem rather than a hypothetical one.
    1. **`local_apps/urls.py`** (new, dependency-free module - stdlib `os` only): single source of truth for the 3 local servers' URLs (`DATA_CONSOLE_URL`, `LIVE_DASHBOARD_URL`, `CHAT_UI_URL`, `SCENARIO_SIMULATOR_URL`), replacing 3 independent copies of the same port/URL-building literals that had already drifted (data_console's nav didn't link to chat or the simulator; chat_interface had no nav bar at all). Deliberately dependency-free so importing it never pulls a heavier stack (dashboard/live_refresh.py's full agent orchestration, chat_interface's evaluator) into a server whose job is just rendering HTML - the same cycle-avoidance reasoning `dashboard/live_refresh.py`'s own docstring already documents, now shared by all 4 render sites instead of independently re-derived by each.
    2. **`dashboard/live_refresh.py`**: `_nav_links()` now reads `DATA_CONSOLE_URL` from `local_apps.urls` (was its own duplicated literal) and appends "AI Assistant"/"What-If Simulator" entries - so the 3 static dashboard pages (`control_tower_*.html`) that were the *only* screens with a working nav bar before Phase 1 now link out to the newer 2 apps as well.
    3. **`data_console/serve_data_console.py`**: nav bar gained "AI Assistant"/"What-If Simulator" links (previously stopped at the 3 dashboard scenarios).
    4. **`chat_interface/serve_chat_ui.py`**: gained a nav bar for the first time (previously had none at all, a pre-existing gap flagged but not fixed in Phase 1's last entry) - links to Data Console, Live Data, and What-If Simulator.
    5. **`scenario_simulation/serve_scenario_simulator.py`**: its own nav bar (built in Phase 1) now reads its 3 URLs from `local_apps.urls` instead of its own locally-duplicated literals, and its "current page" label corrected from the stale "Scenario Simulator" to "What-If Simulator" matching its own `<title>`/`<h1>`.
    6. **`scripts/run_local_servers.py`** (new): a single-command launcher starting all 3 servers (data_console, chat_interface, scenario_simulation) together in one process, each on its own background thread, so every nav-bar link is guaranteed to actually work instead of depending on the user remembering to start 3 separate terminals. `build_server()` was extracted out of each of the 3 apps' own `main()` (which only ever exposed a blocking `serve_forever()` loop with no external handle) so this launcher can hold a real handle to each server and shut all 3 down cleanly on Ctrl+C. A bind failure (e.g. one server already running standalone on its default port, exactly what caused this bug report) is caught and reported as one clear, actionable message naming which env var to override, not a bare socket traceback.
  - Verification: `python3 -m pytest` (whole repo) -> 891 passed (up from 888). New/updated: `dashboard/tests/test_live_refresh.py`'s nav-links test extended for the 2 new entries; new regression tests in `data_console/tests/test_serve_data_console.py`, `chat_interface/tests/test_serve_chat_ui.py`, and `scenario_simulation/tests/test_serve_scenario_simulator.py` (3 new tests total) asserting each page's real rendered HTML contains a working `href` to the other 2 local apps, built from `local_apps.urls` directly rather than hardcoded literals so the tests can't drift from the same source the pages themselves read. `scripts/run_local_servers.py` itself is dev-only launcher tooling (same category as `scripts/local_test_db.py`, which also ships without a dedicated test file) - not covered by a separate test file, but every server it starts is the same `build_server()` already covered by that app's own test suite. **Verified live end-to-end**: killed all 3 standalone server processes, ran `python3 scripts/run_local_servers.py`, confirmed all 3 ports bind in one process and print correctly; `curl`'d all 4 URLs (Data Console, the real Live Data dashboard page, AI Assistant, What-If Simulator) and got 200 from every one; fetched each page's actual rendered nav bar and confirmed every one links to the other 3 with real, resolvable URLs (previously: data_console and the dashboard pages had no link to chat/simulator at all, and chat had no nav bar); regenerated the 3 static dashboard pages (`python3 -m dashboard.run_sample_dashboard`) and confirmed the new nav links appear there too (these are gitignored generated files, not tracked, so no stale committed copies exist); killed the launcher via SIGTERM and confirmed all 3 ports released cleanly (`lsof` showed nothing listening).
  - Notes: This does not merge the 4 apps into one process/port (the bigger "unify into one shell" option still not picked) - each keeps its own routes, port, and test suite; only how they're *started* changed. Left the launcher running in the background after this entry so the user can click through it directly. Not done in this entry: an actual single-process/single-port unification (would remove the "which of 3 servers is or isn't running" class of problem entirely, at the cost of a much larger, riskier change); Executive Dashboard KPI aggregation (still the other outstanding Phase 2 item). - [x] Added Executive Dashboard KPI rollups (revenue at risk, shipment delay cost) to the dashboard's stat row (Phase 2, part 3)
  - Date: 2026-09-11
  - Session: CC-20260908-t3x9
  - What changed: The original product vision doc named an "Executive Dashboard" with KPI numbers (revenue at risk, overstock, orders pending) an executive scans before anything else. The dashboard already had a KPI-shaped stat row (Overall status / Critical findings / High findings / Areas monitored, from STORY-009) - this extends it with the 2 dollar-value KPIs this repo can actually compute from real data. Overstock and orders-pending were deliberately NOT added: this repo has no overstock threshold defined anywhere and no incoming/pending-order data source, so either number would be fabricated, not computed - flagged explicitly rather than faked, same treatment every other "no data source for this yet" gap in this phase got.
    1. **`agents/contracts.py`**: `AgentFinding` gained an optional `metric_value: float | None = None` - a finding's own numeric magnitude in whatever unit its source agent's domain uses (dollars, in both cases used so far), deliberately generic/unitless at this contract layer the same way `detail` already is. Non-breaking (added at the end with a default; every existing `AgentFinding(...)` call site in this repo already uses keyword args, confirmed by grep before making the change).
    2. **`agents/stockout_risk_agent.py`** / **`agents/shipment_delay_analysis_agent.py`**: each per-SKU / per-PO finding now sets `metric_value` to that assessment's own `revenue_at_risk` / `total_cost` - numbers these agents already compute, just not previously exposed in a structured, aggregable form (only baked into free-text `detail`).
    3. **`dashboard/metrics.py`**: new `KPISummary` dataclass + `compute_kpi_summary(snapshot)`, summing `metric_value` across one named metric's findings. Carefully distinguishes "unknown" (`None` - that tile isn't in this snapshot, errored, or every finding lacks a value, e.g. no SKU has a `unit_price` yet) from "computed, genuinely zero" (`0.0` - e.g. shipment_delay_analysis_agent ran clean and found no delays at all) - the same "never default an unavailable signal to a false neutral midpoint" rule `inventory_risk/risk_model.py`'s `compute_risk_score` already applies to its own optional signals, applied here so an executive never sees a confident "$0 revenue at risk" that's actually "we don't know, nobody priced these SKUs."
    4. **`dashboard/render.py`**: 2 new stat cards ("Revenue at risk", "Shipment delay cost") in the existing stat-row, rendering `_format_currency_stat()` - `"$X,XXX"` for a real number, an em dash `"—"` for unknown (never `"$0"` when the true state is unknown). KPI computation is wrapped in the same try/except-and-degrade pattern the charts section already uses, so a bug here can never take down the rest of the page.
  - Verification: `python3 -m pytest` (whole repo) -> 902 passed (up from 891). New: 5 tests in `dashboard/tests/test_metrics.py` (sums correctly across multiple findings; None when no finding carries a value at all; 0.0 when the metric ran clean with zero findings; None when the tile errored; a mix of priced/unpriced findings sums only the priced ones). 3 new tests in `dashboard/tests/test_render.py` (both stat cards render with the right computed values; an absent metric renders the em dash, not a fabricated $0; a KPI-computation failure degrades gracefully without breaking the rest of the page, mirroring the existing charts-failure test). 2 new tests total across `agents/tests/test_stockout_risk_agent.py` and `agents/tests/test_shipment_delay_analysis_agent.py` confirming `metric_value` is actually populated (and correctly `None` when unpriced) end-to-end from each agent, not just at the dashboard layer. **Verified live**: regenerated all 3 dashboard scenarios (`python3 -m dashboard.run_sample_dashboard`) and inspected the real rendered HTML - `synthetic_healthy` correctly shows "$0" for shipment delay cost (it ran clean, no delays - a real zero) and "—" for revenue at risk (neither synthetic SKU has a `unit_price`, so it's honestly unknown, not a fabricated $0); `real_data` (no Postgres connected in this session) correctly shows "—" for both. Restarted `scripts/run_local_servers.py` so the running Data Console/AI Assistant/What-If Simulator all pick up this code.
  - Notes: Not done in this entry, flagged as follow-up: overstock and orders-pending KPIs (blocked on a data source this repo doesn't have - would need an actual overstock threshold definition and a pending/incoming-order table, not just more aggregation code); wiring `metric_value` onto any other agent's findings if a future one has its own natural dollar number. Local-only; no `git commit`/`git push` run per the user's 2026-09-10 instruction.

- [x] Added Human Approval & Audit Trail to the dashboard - Approve/Reject buttons per tile plus a durable decision log (Phase 2, part 4)
  - Date: 2026-09-11
  - Session: CC-20260908-t3x9
  - What changed: The last named feature from the original product vision doc's 12-feature list not yet touched. Every dashboard tile already carries an AI-generated recommendation; this adds the human half - a person can now approve or reject that recommendation, with every decision durably recorded (who, when, and an optional note), matching CLAUDE.md's own distinction between an AI's recommendation and a human's governance decision.
    1. **`dashboard/approval_store.py`** (new module): `ApprovalStore`, mirroring `dashboard/audit_trail.py`'s `DashboardAuditStore` almost exactly (same JSONL persistence, corrupted-line tolerance, loud typed write-failure) with one deliberate difference: `record()` is NOT idempotency-deduplicated. A human approving, then later rejecting, the same recommendation are two real, distinct decisions that both belong in the trail - collapsing them the way "did we already log this tile built ok" collapses retries would silently lose the earlier decision. Keyed on `(dashboard_id, metric_id)` - the same identity `DashboardAuditRecord` already uses, and stable across rebuilds for the 3 named demo scenarios (`real_data`/`partial_failure`/`synthetic_healthy` are fixed strings, not fresh UUIDs per build). `latest_by_metric()` (one badge per tile) and `records_for_dashboard()` (the full trail, oldest first) are the two read shapes the UI needs.
    2. **`dashboard/render.py`**: every tile now has Approve/Reject buttons plus a badge showing the most recent decision (`_render_approval_controls()`), and a new "Approval History" section at the bottom lists every decision ever made on this dashboard, newest first (`_render_approval_history_section()`) - same "don't show an empty section" convention the Data Sources section already follows. Both are optional parameters (`latest_approvals`, `approval_history`) on `render_dashboard_html()`, defaulting to empty so the function stays pure and every existing caller/test is unaffected. `dashboard_id`/`metric_id` are JSON-then-HTML-escaped (`_js_string_literal()`) before being embedded in the buttons' `onclick` attributes - two boundaries stacked (valid JS string literal, then safe inside a double-quoted HTML attribute), same paranoia this module's own docstring already applies to agent-derived text. A single shared `submitApproval()` JS function (not one per tile) posts to `/api/approve` and updates just the clicked tile's badge **in place** - deliberately not a full page reload, since the already-rendered static HTML file wouldn't contain the new decision anyway; the Approval History section itself only refreshes the next time the page is regenerated (Run Analysis, or the next scheduled build) - a documented scope cut, not an oversight.
    3. **`dashboard/live_refresh.py`**: new `approval_store()` getter (env-var-overridable path, same pattern as `_dashboard_audit_store()`) - public, not underscore-prefixed, because `data_console/serve_data_console.py`'s new route needs to read/write the exact same store this module's own render calls use, not a second, drifting copy. `_build_dashboard()` now resolves and passes `latest_approvals`/`approval_history` into every render call.
    4. **`data_console/serve_data_console.py`**: new `POST /api/approve` route + `_handle_approve()` - validates `dashboard_id`/`metric_id`/`decision` (`"approved"` or `"rejected"` only)/`reviewer` (non-blank)/`note` (optional), records via the shared `approval_store()`, returns the persisted record as JSON.
    5. **`.gitignore`**: added `dashboard_approvals.jsonl`, matching every sibling audit-log's own gitignore entry - this is local runtime state, never meant to ship.
  - Verification: `python3 -m pytest` (whole repo) -> 929 passed (up from 902). New: 10 tests in `dashboard/tests/test_approval_store.py` (record/reload/history/latest-by-metric/corrupted-line-tolerance/write-failure, mirroring `test_audit_trail.py`'s own coverage shape). 8 new tests in `dashboard/tests/test_render.py` (buttons render with correctly escaped ids; a latest-decision badge shows when present and doesn't when absent; the history section renders newest-first with notes, is absent when empty, and degrades gracefully on a simulated render bug without breaking the rest of the page; a dedicated XSS-safety test confirming a hostile `dashboard_id`/`metric_id` can't break out of the `onclick` attribute). 1 new test in `dashboard/tests/test_live_refresh.py` proving a decision recorded through `ApprovalStore` directly shows up the next time `refresh_real_data_dashboard()` renders that same dashboard_id - confirms the 2 entry points (render call, API route) genuinely share one store rather than drifting. 9 new tests in `data_console/tests/test_serve_data_console.py` for `/api/approve` (records and returns the decision; defaults a missing note to empty string; 6 parametrized 400 cases each naming the specific invalid/missing field; malformed JSON is a clean 400). **Verified live end-to-end** against the running launcher (`scripts/run_local_servers.py`): `curl`'d a real approve then a real reject for the same tile, regenerated the dashboard (`python3 -m dashboard.run_sample_dashboard`), and confirmed the tile's badge showed only the latest decision ("Rejected by ram") while the Approval History section preserved both, newest first, with the reject's note intact - then deleted this test data (`dashboard/dashboard_approvals.jsonl`) and regenerated again to leave a clean starting state, confirmed via a 0-count grep for any rendered badge.
  - Notes: **This completes Phase 2** as scoped from the original product vision doc's 12-feature list - every named feature now has real backend logic and, for the ones the user prioritized or that were quick, natural extensions (Stockout Risk/Root Cause/Recommendations/What-If from Phase 1, AI Assistant per-entity Q&A, Executive Dashboard KPIs, Human Approval & Audit Trail), a UI a person can actually click through. Not done, explicitly out of scope: the 3 "screen-only" gaps the gap-analysis audit already named and this phase didn't touch (Demand Forecast per-product/category/region breakdown beyond the existing aggregate total, Delivery Intelligence's cross-agent narrative like "Supplier A's delays could cause stockouts for 4 products," Data Quality Center's expansion beyond a single completeness check) - all real backend modules exist (`forecasting/`, `shipment_delay_analysis/`, `data_quality_monitoring/`), just not built out to the same depth as this phase's 4 pieces; and the "unify 4 local apps into one process/port" option, still not picked, still the natural next infrastructure step if the nav-bar/multi-server friction becomes a recurring complaint rather than a one-time fix. Local-only; no `git commit`/`git push` run per the user's 2026-09-10 instruction.

- [x] Added a "validity" dimension to Data Quality Center - not just "is every field present," but "is a present numeric field actually a real number" (Phase 3, part 1)
  - Date: 2026-09-11
  - Session: CC-20260908-t3x9
  - What changed: `data_quality_monitoring/quality_checks.py`'s own docstring already named this as the deliberate next step ("does not handle: whether a present field's *value* is well-formed (numeric, in range, parseable) - that is a later dimension, not this one") - the last of the 3 "screen-only" gaps the original gap-analysis audit flagged and Phase 2 didn't touch. Scoped to exactly what the module's own boundary already promises: presence-and-well-formed, not domain business rules (range checks like "cost must be non-negative" stay inventory_risk/shipment_delay_analysis's own job, per this module's existing "deliberately domain-agnostic" stance).
    1. **`data_quality_monitoring/quality_checks.py`**: `QualityDimension` widened from `Literal["completeness"]` to `Literal["completeness", "validity"]`. New `_is_valid_number(value)` (accepts int/float/Decimal - not NaN, not a bool even though `bool` is an `int` subclass in Python - and a numeric-looking string via a plain `float()` parse, since "parseable" is explicitly part of what this dimension promises) and `_validity_check(rows, numeric_fields)` - scoped to *presence* first: a row missing the field entirely counts against completeness only, never double-penalized against validity too, since that would understate the score for a mundane reason (field simply not supplied) rather than a genuine validity problem (field supplied but garbage). `assess_data_quality()` gained an optional `numeric_fields: tuple[str, ...] = ()` parameter - empty by default, so every existing caller keeps getting exactly the completeness-only report it always has; the validity dimension is only added to `dimension_results` when a caller actually opts in.
    2. **`data_quality_monitoring/evaluator.py`**: `DataQualityEvaluator.run()` gained the same `numeric_fields` passthrough parameter - needed zero other changes, since its own audit-recording loop already iterates `report.dimension_results` generically (whatever dimensions a report happens to contain get audited, a design choice from STORY-015 that paid off directly here).
    3. **`agents/data_quality_monitoring_agent.py`**: new `DEFAULT_NUMERIC_FIELDS = ("transportation_cost",)` - the one optional numeric field this repo's delivery rows already carry (`shipment_delay_analysis/delay_analysis.py` already validates the same field, but for its own "recover gracefully and keep computing" purpose; this agent's purpose - surface an aggregate score/alert for the data steward - is complementary, not duplicative, the same overlapping-but-distinct-purpose pattern `stockout_risk_agent`/`risk_detection_agent` already both independently assessing the same SKUs sets). `context.get("numeric_fields", DEFAULT_NUMERIC_FIELDS)` passed through, same optional-override shape `required_fields`/`check_id` already use.
  - Verification: `python3 -m pytest` (whole repo) -> 941 passed (up from 929). New: 9 tests in `data_quality_monitoring/tests/test_quality_checks.py` (no `numeric_fields` means only completeness is scored - backward compatibility; a real number scores 100; a non-numeric string is flagged; a numeric-looking string parses as valid; a real database `Decimal` is accepted; NaN is rejected; a `bool` is rejected despite being an `int` subclass; a row missing the field entirely is excluded from validity's own checked_rows rather than penalized; no row carrying the full numeric-field set scores `None`, not a fabricated number; the overall score correctly averages completeness and validity when both are present, hand-verified: (100+50)/2 = 75). 1 new test in `data_quality_monitoring/tests/test_evaluator.py` confirming `numeric_fields` reaches `assess_data_quality()` and the validity dimension gets its own audit record. 1 new test in `agents/tests/test_data_quality_monitoring_agent.py` confirming the agent scores validity off `transportation_cost` by default; 1 pre-existing test updated (idempotency test now expects 2 audit records - completeness + validity - not 1, since `DEFAULT_NUMERIC_FIELDS` is non-empty by default now). **Verified live**: regenerated all 3 dashboard scenarios (`python3 -m dashboard.run_sample_dashboard`) and confirmed the real rendered pages - `synthetic_healthy`'s delivery rows all carry genuine float `transportation_cost` values, so its Data Quality tile still reads "score 100/100 (good)" (completeness 100 + validity 100, averaging to the same 100 as before this change); `partial_failure`'s empty delivery_rows still correctly reads "no rows available to assess data quality" at 90% confidence, unchanged - confirming this addition doesn't alter behavior for data that doesn't exercise it.
  - Notes: Not done in this entry, still flagged as follow-up: Demand Forecast per-product/category/region breakdown, Delivery Intelligence's cross-agent narrative, and unifying the 4 local apps into one process. A future 3rd quality dimension (timeliness/freshness, consistency across related fields) would follow this same `dimension_results`-is-already-generic pattern with no further changes needed to `evaluator.py`. Local-only; no `git commit`/`git push` run per the user's 2026-09-10 instruction.

Because this all flows through the existing generic `AgentFinding`/`subject_kind` contract, `dashboard/charts.py`'s "explore chart" section and `chat_interface/chat.py`'s per-entity Q&A path ("why is SKU-1's demand trending up?") both pick this up automatically - zero changes needed in either, exactly the same "wire it through the existing generic contract, everything downstream lights up for free" payoff as the Data Quality validity-dimension entry. When no row carries `sku` (today's synthetic demo data, and any caller that hasn't mapped it) - unchanged: `findings` stays empty, aggregate-only recommendation text exactly as before. Updated 2 stale doc comments that named `DemandForecastingAgent` as the canonical "always-empty findings" example (`agents/contracts.py`, `chat_interface/chat.py`) since that's no longer true.

Also fixed a related pre-existing gap while touching this path: `dashboard/live_refresh.py`'s hardcoded inventory Postgres query listed only the 5 required columns (unlike `customer_orders`, which already used `SELECT *`) - meaning `incoming_stock`/`unit_price`/`demand_std_dev`/the new `supplier` were all silently dropped on the "real_data" fallback path even when a real table had them; changed it to `SELECT *` to match `customer_orders`'s already-working pattern.

Only correlates a supplier whose own `supplier_evaluation_agent` finding is flagged (medium/high/critical - a healthy supplier's SKUs aren't "affected" by anything) against SKUs that are *themselves* currently at elevated stockout risk (also medium/high/critical) - never every product a flagged supplier happens to source. New agent `agents/delivery_intelligence_agent.py` wraps it, structured like `RecommendationAgent` (STORY-006): its input isn't raw data, it's other agents' own already-validated `AgentResponse` outputs (`stockout_risk_agent`'s and `supplier_evaluation_agent`'s), consumed via the same `context["agent_outputs"]` contract. Three distinct honest outcomes, not collapsed into one: (1) a prerequisite agent missing/errored -> `confidence=None`, explains what's unavailable; (2) both present but no SKU has `supplier` mapped yet -> `confidence=None`, explains the mapping gap; (3) both present and mapped -> `confidence=1.0` either way, whether or not a real narrative was found ("no supplier-driven stockout risk detected" is itself a confident, checked answer, not a null result).
    Wired into both places that build the agent fleet (this repo has 2 independent copies of the stage1/stage2 orchestration - a pre-existing duplication, not introduced by this entry): `dashboard/live_refresh.py`'s and `chat_interface/run_sample_chat_interface.py`'s own `_stage2_results()`. `DeliveryIntelligenceAgent` now runs first in stage 2, over stage-1's own outputs, and its own output is added to what `RecommendationAgent` synthesizes - so a real narrative also surfaces in the combined recommendation text, and its own tile shows up in `run.snapshot.metrics` for free (no `dashboard/metrics.py`/`render.py` changes needed beyond one new `_TILE_LABELS` entry - "Delivery Intelligence" - confirming that module's own claim to "not depend on any one agent's identity"). Added a matching `chat_interface/router.py` topic (`"delivery intelligence"`/`"supplier impact"`/`"products at risk"` keywords) so it's chat-queryable too.

- [x] Fixed 3 live bugs the user found on their first real run of the merged server (Phase 3, part 6)
  - Date: 2026-09-11
  - Session: CC-20260908-t3x9
  - What changed: The user ran `scripts/run_local_servers.py` against their own real Postgres and reported 4 issues from screenshots. (1) The "Change" button in Data Console's "Connect Your Data" section appeared to do nothing. Root cause: its click handler set `activeSourceKind = null` then called `loadConnectSection()`, which unconditionally re-checked `/api/tables` - when a database is reachable via `SUPPLYMIND_PG_*` env vars (the user's exact setup), that check immediately found the same still-reachable database and silently re-adopted it, before the connect chooser ever rendered, so the click had no visible effect. Fixed with a `skipAutoReconnect` flag: set by the "Change" button, consulted exactly once by `loadConnectSection()` to go straight to the chooser instead of re-detecting the same database. (2 and 2.1) The "Live Data" dashboard page (and its 2 demo siblings) still showed a 6-item nav (the 4 shared destinations plus both demo-scenario links folded in) - the exact "shows more tabs" complaint this repo's own nav-unification entry earlier this session had explicitly decided was *not* a bug (`local_apps/theme.py`'s docstring called it "intentional... not drift"). The user's live report proved that call wrong, so reversed it: `dashboard/live_refresh.py`'s `_nav_links()` now returns the identical fixed 4-item nav every other screen shows (`local_apps/theme.py:render_nav_bar` extended to accept `current_label=None` for "none of the 4 is the current page," needed for the 2 demo-scenario pages); the 2 demo links moved to a small secondary "Demo dashboards: ..." row - the exact treatment `data_console/serve_data_console.py` already gave its own demo links, now generalized into a shared `theme.render_demo_links_row()` instead of leaving data_console's copy as its own one-off (its own hardcoded markup and duplicate `.demo-links` CSS were replaced with a call to the shared function; the CSS itself moved into `theme.TOKENS_CSS`, no longer duplicated per-screen). Fixing this needs no code change, only regenerating those 3 static files against the now-current `local_apps/urls.py` (path-based URLs on the single merged port) - which happens automatically the next time the user clicks "Run Analysis" in Data Console, or runs `python3 -m dashboard.run_sample_dashboard` for the 2 demo scenarios.

- [x] Fixed a real regression: AI Assistant/What-If Simulator's own JS still posted to their pre-merge, un-prefixed API paths (Phase 3, part 7)
  - Date: 2026-09-11
  - Session: CC-20260908-t3x9
  - What changed: The user clicked "Run Simulation" in their browser and got a plain "not found" in the results panel. Root cause, confirmed by grep before touching anything: `scenario_simulation/serve_scenario_simulator.py`'s embedded page JS hardcoded `fetch('/api/simulate', ...)`, and `chat_interface/serve_chat_ui.py`'s hardcoded `fetch('/api/chat', ...)` - both correct only when each runs as its own standalone server (root of its own origin), and both silently wrong once server-unification (this session's part 4 entry) mounted them under `/simulate/` and `/chat/` prefixes on the merged server. `local_apps/unified_server.py`'s own Python-side routing was already correctly prefixed from that entry, which is exactly why this slipped through every check at the time: every test (and my own curl verification) posted to `/simulate/api/simulate`/`/chat/api/chat` directly, never through the actual rendered page's own embedded fetch call - the one path a real browser click actually takes. Fixed by parameterizing `api_path` on both `render_page()` functions (default `/api/simulate`/`/api/chat`, unchanged for each module's own standalone server) and having `local_apps/unified_server.py`'s `UnifiedHandler.do_GET` pass the correctly-prefixed value (`/simulate/api/simulate`/`/chat/api/chat`) when serving through the merged server - `string.Template`'s existing `$theme_tokens`/`$nav_bar` placeholder pattern extended with one more (`$api_path`), no new templating mechanism introduced.
  - Verification: `python3 -m pytest` (whole repo) -> 1013 passed (up from 1009 before this change - 1 new regression test each in `scenario_simulation/tests/test_serve_scenario_simulator.py` and `chat_interface/tests/test_serve_chat_ui.py` locking in each standalone server's own default `api_path`; 2 new in `local_apps/tests/test_unified_server.py` - the critical ones, since they assert against the actual served page body's embedded fetch target rather than posting to the API path directly, which is exactly the gap that let this regression ship unnoticed the first time). **Verified live** on an isolated port (`SUPPLYMIND_DATA_CONSOLE_PORT=18766`, not the user's own running server): curled both `/chat/` and `/simulate/` and confirmed each page's own JS now embeds the correctly-prefixed fetch target (`fetch('/chat/api/chat'`, `fetch('/simulate/api/simulate'`); ran an actual simulation request through `/simulate/api/simulate` and got a real baseline/projected comparison back, not a 404. Also answered 2 non-bug questions from the same message: "Run Analysis" (confirmed via reading `dashboard/live_refresh.py:_dataset_result_from_data_console()`) does already respect whatever's mapped in Data Console per-dataset - table, CSV, or Sheet - falling back to a hardcoded Postgres query only for a dataset with no mapping at all; the "Your Tables" list under it is a separate, unrelated "browse any table" feature, not part of the Run Analysis flow. Flagged as a real, still-open gap rather than fixed here (out of scope for this message, needs the user's steer given it's a data-freshness/architecture question, not a one-line bug): the AI Assistant's snapshot is chosen once at server startup (`chat_interface/serve_chat_ui.py:choose_snapshot()`) purely on whether Postgres env vars are configured, and even then pulls from hardcoded literal table names (`chat_interface/run_sample_chat_interface.py:build_real_data_snapshot()`'s own `DATASETS`) rather than data_console's actual saved per-dataset mappings the way `refresh_real_data_dashboard()` does - so it neither reflects a CSV/Sheet connection nor refreshes when "Run Analysis" is clicked.
  - Notes: Left the user's own running server on port 8766 untouched again, same reasoning as the prior entry. The chat-snapshot staleness/mapping gap above is a real, confirmed architectural inconsistency between the 3 screens (Live Data honors mappings and refreshes on demand; Chat does neither) - deliberately not fixed unilaterally since it's a real design decision (should chat re-fetch fresh data every request, or refresh via the same "Run Analysis" trigger, or something else) rather than an obvious bug fix; flagged to the user, awaiting direction. Local-only; no `git commit`/`git push` run per the user's 2026-09-10 instruction.

- [x] Made the AI Assistant reuse Run Analysis's own latest result instead of a separate, frozen-at-startup snapshot (Phase 3, part 8)
  - Date: 2026-09-11
  - Session: CC-20260908-t3x9
  - What changed: Asked the user via AskUserQuestion whether to fix the chat-staleness gap flagged in the prior entry now; they chose "Yes, make it match Live Data." `dashboard/live_refresh.py` gained a module-level `_latest_real_data_snapshot` cache (in-process only, not persisted - same lifetime as the process itself) plus a `latest_real_data_snapshot()` accessor; `refresh_real_data_dashboard()` (the "Run Analysis" button's own server-side handler) now sets it after every successful build, guarded on `run.snapshot is not None` so a transient failure never clobbers a prior good result. `chat_interface/serve_chat_ui.py` gained `current_snapshot(startup_snapshot, startup_data_source)`: prefers `live_refresh.latest_real_data_snapshot()` when it's not None (Run Analysis has succeeded at least once), else falls back to whatever `choose_snapshot()` picked at server startup - called per-request (not cached at this layer) by both `ChatUIHandler.do_GET`/`do_POST` (the standalone server) and `local_apps/unified_server.py`'s `UnifiedHandler` chat routes (the merged server), so a "Run Analysis" click takes effect on the very next chat question with zero server restart needed. Since `refresh_real_data_dashboard()` already builds from whatever data_console currently has mapped (table, CSV, or Sheet - not just Postgres, per the prior entry's own reading of `_dataset_result_from_data_console()`), this closes the actual gap: chat now answers from the exact same data Live Data shows, refreshed the same way, instead of running its own separately-stale pipeline that only ever read hardcoded literal Postgres table names.
    Introduced this repo's first module-level mutable pytest fixture concern: `_latest_real_data_snapshot` being a plain global meant one test calling `refresh_real_data_dashboard()` silently leaked its result into an unrelated later test's own hand-built chat snapshot (caught immediately by the first full-suite run after wiring this up - `test_post_chat_api_answers_a_query` started failing with a snapshot it never constructed). Fixed by adding this repo's first root-level `conftest.py`: one autouse fixture resetting the global to `None` before and after every test.
  - Verification: `python3 -m pytest` (whole repo) -> 1018 passed (up from 1013 before this change - 1 new in `dashboard/tests/test_live_refresh.py` confirming `refresh_real_data_dashboard()` populates `latest_real_data_snapshot()`; 2 new in `chat_interface/tests/test_serve_chat_ui.py` unit-testing `current_snapshot()`'s 2 branches directly, plus 1 end-to-end test proving the standalone server's page and API both pick up a snapshot that appears *after* the server already started, with no restart; 1 matching end-to-end test in `local_apps/tests/test_unified_server.py` through the merged server, which is the path a real user actually clicks). **Verified live** on an isolated port (`SUPPLYMIND_DATA_CONSOLE_PORT=18766`): asked chat a stockout-risk question (answered from the startup/synthetic snapshot), triggered `/api/run-analysis` (which surfaced a real data-quality problem in the connected data - missing `safety_stock`/`daily_demand_rate` on 500 inventory rows, unrelated to this fix), asked the identical chat question again with no restart in between, and confirmed the answer changed to reflect that same degraded state Run Analysis had just reported - proving the refresh actually reaches chat end to end, good or bad.
  - Notes: Left the user's own running server on port 8766 untouched, same reasoning as the prior 2 entries - they'll need to restart it to pick up every fix from this session, including this one. `conftest.py` is new infrastructure (the repo's first) but purely test-scoped, not a production/runtime change - doesn't cross a CLAUDE.md governance boundary. Local-only; no `git commit`/`git push` run per the user's 2026-09-10 instruction.

- [x] Fixed 2 more live UI bugs: "Your Tables" ignoring the chosen data source, and a lone chart stretching full-width (Phase 3, part 9)
  - Date: 2026-09-12
  - Session: CC-20260908-t3x9
  - What changed: (1) The user pointed out that Data Console's "Your Tables" panel stayed visible regardless of what they picked under "Connect Your Data." Root cause: `loadTables()` gated itself purely on `/api/tables`'s own "is a database reachable at all" check, completely independent of `activeSourceKind` (the frontend's own record of what the user actually chose - database/file/zip/sheet). Since a database reachable via `SUPPLYMIND_PG_*` env vars (the user's exact setup, same root cause as the "Change" button bug 2 entries ago) is *always* detected as reachable, "Your Tables" kept showing a live table list even if the user explicitly picked "Upload a CSV" or "Connect a Google Sheet" instead. Fixed by gating `loadTables()` on `activeSourceKind !== 'database'` first; kept in sync by adding a `loadTables()` call right after every place that sets `activeSourceKind` and calls `loadConnectSection()` (the "Change" button, DB-connect success, upload success, sheet-connect success - 4 call sites), and by sequencing the initial page load as `loadConnectSection().then(loadTables)` instead of firing both in parallel, since `loadTables()`'s new check needs `activeSourceKind` to have already settled (loadConnectSection's own auto-detect against a reachable env-var database) rather than reading it while still `null`.
    (2) The user reported the first chart on the Executive Control Tower was too large. Root cause: `.charts-grid`'s CSS (`grid-template-columns: repeat(auto-fit, minmax(320px, 1fr))`) is fine with 2 quick-review charts (they share the row), but with only 1 qualifying (e.g. the user's data had 0 critical/high findings, so "Findings by area" was excluded per its own existing rule, leaving "Confidence by area" alone) that lone card's `1fr` track stretched to the entire page width with no sibling to share it with, and its SVG/text scaled up proportionally (`.chart-svg { width: 100%; }`). This exact bug class already existed and had already been fixed once before, just for a *different* single-card case (the explore section's one-panel-at-a-time view had its own `.explore-panel .chart-card { max-width: 560px; }` override) - the quick-review grid's own lone-card case was missed. Generalized: moved the `max-width: 560px` cap onto the base `.chart-card` rule itself (covers every chart-card regardless of how many siblings it has) and removed the now-redundant explore-panel-specific override, so this class of bug can't recur a third time in some other section that reuses `.chart-card`.
  - Verification: `python3 -m pytest` (whole repo) -> 1020 passed (up from 1018 before this change - 1 new in `data_console/tests/test_serve_data_console.py` locking in the `activeSourceKind`-gated check and the `loadConnectSection().then(loadTables)` sequencing; 1 new in `dashboard/tests/test_render.py` rendering a snapshot with 0 critical/high findings and confirming the lone "Confidence by area" card still gets `max-width: 560px`). **Verified live** on an isolated port (`SUPPLYMIND_DATA_CONSOLE_PORT=18766`): curled the root page and confirmed the new gating check and sequencing are both present, and confirmed exactly 4 `loadTables()` call sites now exist (the 4 "Connect Your Data" actions) beyond the initial sequenced call; curled a regenerated dashboard page and confirmed `max-width: 560px` is present in its own `<style>` block.
  - Notes: Both bugs were "the frontend has 2 independent checks/layouts that were supposed to agree but didn't" - the same root-cause shape as the "Change" button bug and the merged-server "not found" bug earlier this session, all caught only once a real user clicked through the actual page rather than a test hitting an endpoint directly. Local-only; no `git commit`/`git push` run per the user's 2026-09-10 instruction.

- [x] Second design-feedback round: fixed a real "Your Tables" gating hole, a page-recentering bug, decluttered mapping-card field lists, added a fixed background treatment (Phase 3, part 12)
  - Date: 2026-09-12
  - Session: CC-20260908-t3x9
  - What changed: The user came back after the polish pass with 6 more points, most concrete and actionable rather than purely aesthetic. (1) "I can still view the table even when the Database is not selected": the prior fix (part 9) gated `loadTables()` on `activeSourceKind === 'database'`, but that variable itself gets set silently by a background reachability check - both `loadConnectSection()`'s "already connected via SUPPLYMIND_PG_* env vars" branch and `startMapping()`'s own `/api/tables` check flip it to `'database'` with zero click from the user - so a user who mapped every one of the 3 datasets from an uploaded CSV (confirmed from their screenshot: all 3 cards read "Mapped from uploaded file") still saw a live database table browser, correctly reading the silent auto-adopt as never having chosen anything. Fixed by adding a second, narrower flag, `userSelectedDatabase`, set `true` in exactly one place - `renderDbConnectionForm`'s own submit handler, the single point a "Connect a Database" form was actually filled in and POSTed successfully (covers both the top-level chooser and any per-card one) - and reset to `false` alongside `activeSourceKind` on "Change." `loadTables()` now gates on `!userSelectedDatabase` instead. `activeSourceKind` itself is untouched and still drives mapping-continuity (which source a card defaults to).
    (2) "The page center is moving for the last tab": root cause was a plain CSS inconsistency - `chat_interface/serve_chat_ui.py` and `scenario_simulation/serve_scenario_simulator.py` both had `.page { max-width: 900px; }` while `data_console/serve_data_console.py` and `dashboard/render.py` both use `1180px`. Since the nav bar sits flush against `.page`'s left edge and `.page` is centered via `margin: 0 auto`, switching between a 1180px page and a 900px one shifts that left edge ~140px in a wide viewport - exactly the "moving center" reported when clicking into What-If Simulator (the literal last nav item) or AI Assistant. Fixed by matching both to `1180px`; each page's own narrower reading column inside it (chat's `.chat-box { max-width: 720px; }`, the simulator's flex form/result panels) is untouched.
    (3) "Too much information... too crowded... will not make sense to all people" (re: the 3 mapping cards' always-visible required/optional field descriptions): wrapped `data_console/serve_data_console.py`'s `buildFieldsList()` output in a native `<details class="mapping-fields-details">` (closed by default) with a one-line `<summary>What {label} needs (N fields)</summary>`, instead of every field's full 2-3 line description rendering inline unconditionally. Same "progressive disclosure" pattern `dashboard/render.py`'s own `.tile-detail` already established for the Live Data screen's technical explanations - the pattern this repo already had for exactly this kind of density problem, just not yet applied here.
    (4) "Add something to the background... a background image which remains static even when we scroll": added a background-image (3 soft radial-gradient blobs, brand/accent-2/success tinted, positioned in the corners) to `local_apps/theme.py`'s `TOKENS_CSS` `body` rule with `background-attachment: fixed`, so it applies to every screen automatically and stays pinned behind the content while the page scrolls.
