- [x] Seventh design-feedback round: fixed 2 real CSS clipping bugs hiding tooltips, shipped the glossary panel open by default, and added a Reset button to the What-If Simulator (Phase 3, part 17)
  - Date: 2026-09-12
  - Session: CC-20260908-t3x9
  - What changed: The user reported "I cannot see the tooltip or the glossary panel. It is not visible" - rather than guess, read the actual served CSS for every `.term` tooltip site and found 2 real, distinct clipping bugs (not a "user needs to hover harder" issue): (1) `dashboard/render.py`'s `.stat-card` (the Executive Control Tower's 6 KPI boxes) had `overflow: hidden` - added in an earlier round for a text-ellipsis pass that didn't actually need it on the parent (`.stat-value`/`.stat-label` already clip themselves) - which silently clipped the "Overall status" card's own `.term` tooltip, an absolutely-positioned popover that renders above its trigger and was being cut off by the card's own edge. Removed `overflow: hidden` from `.stat-card` (kept `min-width: 0`, which is unrelated and still needed for the grid). (2) `data_console/serve_data_console.py`'s `.mapping-box` had `overflow: hidden` for a real reason - its 2 rectangular child columns (`.mapping-box-left`/`.mapping-box-right`) have no border-radius of their own and would otherwise poke square corners past the box's rounded ones - but this clipped the "Run Analysis" status `.term` tooltip living inside it the same way. Moved the corner rounding onto each column directly (`.mapping-box-left { border-radius: 0 0 0 10px }`, `.mapping-box-right { border-radius: 0 10px 10px 0 }`) so the box no longer needs to clip anything at all, and removed its `overflow: hidden`. (3) The simulator's glossary panel (added 2 entries ago) was a real bug, not a discoverability issue: it shipped as a *collapsed* `<details>` with no `open` attribute - a closed `<details>` hides everything past its `<summary>` by design, so unless a user clicked the small "ⓘ What do these terms mean?" line, none of its content ever rendered. Added the `open` attribute so the definitions are visible immediately, while still leaving it collapsible for anyone who wants to close it. (4) "Provide a reset button, which removes everything and it will help in entering new data": added a `<button type="reset" id="reset-btn" class="btn-secondary">Reset</button>` next to Run Simulation in `scenario_simulation/serve_scenario_simulator.py` - the native `type="reset"` restores every input to its own `value="..."` default for free; a new `form.addEventListener('reset', ...)` also clears the result panel back to its starting placeholder, since the native reset event only knows about form fields, not the separate result panel a prior run may have replaced with a comparison or an error box. Styled as an outline `.btn-secondary` (not solid brand-blue) so it doesn't visually compete with the primary Run Simulation action.
  - Verification: `python3 -m pytest` (whole repo) -> 1054 passed (up from 1051 before this change - 5 new tests: `test_stat_card_no_longer_clips_its_own_tooltip` in `dashboard/tests/test_render.py`, `test_mapping_box_no_longer_clips_its_own_tooltip` in `data_console/tests/test_serve_data_console.py`, and `test_get_root_page_has_a_reset_button_that_clears_the_form_and_results` plus an updated assertion on the existing glossary-panel test for the `open` attribute in `scenario_simulation/tests/test_serve_scenario_simulator.py`). **Verified live** on an isolated port (`SUPPLYMIND_DATA_CONSOLE_PORT=18790`, user's own server on 8766 left untouched): curled the unified server's `/` and `/simulate/` pages and confirmed both `<style>` blocks still brace-balance (606/606, 93/93); confirmed the only remaining `overflow: hidden` string in the whole served root page is inside an explanatory code comment, not a live CSS rule; confirmed `.mapping-box {}`'s actual rule body contains no `overflow: hidden` and the 2 split `border-radius` declarations are present; confirmed `<details class="glossary-panel" open>` and the Reset button plus its `reset` event listener are all present in the served simulator page. Regenerated `dashboard/control_tower_real_data.html` (and the 2 demo dashboards) via `python3 -m dashboard.run_sample_dashboard` and confirmed the regenerated real-data file's `.stat-card` rule no longer contains `overflow: hidden` either - the static file had baked in the old bug and needed regeneration to pick up the fix, same as every prior round's Live Data changes.
  - Notes: `overflow: hidden` silently clipping an absolutely-positioned child is a known CSS footgun with no console warning or visual error - worth remembering the next time any `.term` tooltip (or any other `position: absolute` popover) is added inside a container that also needs `overflow: hidden` for an unrelated reason (corner-rounding a background, or containing a float): either move the rounding onto the actual opaque children (as done here for `.mapping-box`) or confirm the popover's nearest `overflow: hidden` ancestor is the popover's own direct positioning context, not something further up the tree. Local-only; no `git commit`/`git push` run per the user's 2026-09-10 instruction.

Root cause: every `learn/visuals.py` visual drew its own caption as an SVG `<text>` element inside a fixed 220-unit viewBox - a default root `<svg>`'s overflow is `hidden` per the UA stylesheet, so any caption wider than the viewBox silently disappeared past both edges, no console error. Fixed by removing every caption `<text>` from `learn/visuals.py`'s 4 render functions entirely (each now takes an `elem_id` instead, tagging its mutable sub-element - the fill `<rect>`, the marker `<circle>`, the 2 split-bar segments - with a stable id) and rendering the caption as a normal HTML `<div class="explainer-caption-text">` next to the SVG in `learn/serve_learn.py`, which can't overflow a fixed coordinate space because it isn't one. The 2 short structural labels timeline/split-bar draw inside their own SVGs ("Order placed"/"Arrives", the 2 segment names) stayed, since they're proportionally sized and never overflowed. Also addressed in the same message: (2) "give some dynamic ideas" - every one of the 8 cards now has an `<input type="range">` slider that live-updates its own visual (resizing the fill rect / repositioning the marker / resizing both split-bar segments and their labels) and caption via a client-side `<script>` at the bottom of the page - a JS mirror of new `_meter_band()`/`_meter_caption()`/`_scale_caption()`/`_timeline_caption()` helper functions in `learn/serve_learn.py` (kept in sync by hand, same acknowledged tradeoff already logged for other JS/Python duplication in this app), verified to produce identical output via `node -e` against the extracted formulas. (3) "make these boxes more better" - each card gained a colored top border matching its own visual's accent color (reusing existing tokens, no new hues) and the caption text (now real HTML, not SVG `fill`) got proper weight/sizing. (4) "Instead of Learn, change the name to something more meaningful": renamed the nav label, page `<h1>`, and `<title>` from "Learn" to "Glossary" (the page's actual content is exactly that) in `local_apps/theme.py`'s `_NAV_DESTINATIONS`, `dashboard/live_refresh.py`'s `_nav_links()`, and `learn/serve_learn.py`; renamed `theme.ICON_LEARN` to `theme.ICON_GLOSSARY` for the same reason. The module/package/URL prefix stayed `learn`/`/learn/` deliberately - internal identifiers no user ever sees, so renaming them would be pointless file churn. (5) "Glossary has not been removed from other pages": true - the plan when the Glossary page shipped was to keep the compact per-page glossary panels as a "quick reference" alongside it, but the user wants the full glossary to live in exactly one place. Removed the `$glossary_panel`/`{glossary_html}` wiring and its `theme.render_glossary_panel(...)` calls from `dashboard/render.py`, `chat_interface/serve_chat_ui.py`, `data_console/serve_data_console.py`, and `scenario_simulation/serve_scenario_simulator.py` (also dropping the now-unused `GLOSSARY` import in `data_console/serve_data_console.py` and the now-unused `_GLOSSARY_ENTRIES` constant in the simulator) - the inline `.term` hover tooltips on all 4 screens are untouched, only the standalone panels are gone.
